to (
2.7
) with respect to
A
, such that
(\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\phi|_{\Sigma_{0}})=(\upphi,T\upphi)
.

If we take
A\in\Omega^{1}(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{M,Q})
and
G_{A}\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{M,Q})
, then
\psi=r\phi\in C^{\infty}(\widehat{\mathcal{M}_{M,Q}})
.

We define the energy densities
\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi]
as follows:

\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi]:=(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}\Omega^{2}|D_{X}\psi|^{2}+(\Omega^{-1}r)^{\min\{p,0\}}(\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}|D_{T}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|D_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}).

Note that
\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi]
are gauge invariant!

When
A=\widehat{A}
, we can express:

\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi]=(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}\Omega^{2}|X\psi|^{2}+(\Omega^{-1}r)^{\min\{p,0\}}(\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}|D_{T}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|\psi|^{2}).

Given
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{3}
consider the operators
\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}
and
D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}
as defined in (
2.17
) and (
2.18
). Then we define the following higher-order energy densities:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]:=

\displaystyle\>(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}\Omega^{2}|D_{X}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}+(\Omega^{-1}r)^{\min\{p,0\}}(\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}|D_{T}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}+|\not{D}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{p}[D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi]:=

\displaystyle\>(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}\Omega^{2}|D_{X}D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}+(\Omega^{-1}r)^{\min\{p,0\}}(\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}|D_{T}D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}+|\not{D}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}+r^{-2}|D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}).

Note that
\mathcal{E}_{p}[D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi]
are gauge invariant, but
\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]
are not. When
A=\widehat{A}
, we can estimate for any
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\mathcal{E}_{p}[D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi]\sim\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi],

using that
D_{X}=X
,
D_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
and
D_{T}=T-iqr^{-1}\mathbf{1}
.

Theorem 2.5

(Weighted energy boundedness and integrated energy decay; Theorem 3.1 in
[
Gaj26
]
)
.

Let
\psi
be a solution to (
2.9
). Let
\epsilon,\delta,\eta,\kappa_{0}>0
,
N,\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
r_{+}<r_{H}<r_{I}<\infty
. Consider
1<p<\min\{1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell},2\}+\epsilon
and let
\upzeta
be a smooth function satisfying
\upzeta(r)=1
for
r\notin(r_{\sharp}-2\eta,r_{\sharp}+2\eta)
and
\upzeta(r)=0
for
r\in(r_{\sharp}-\eta,r_{\sharp}+\eta)
.

Assume that
0\leq\kappa_{+}\leq\kappa_{1}
or
|q|\leq q_{1}
. Then, for suitably small
\kappa_{1}
or
q_{1}
, there exist a constant

C=C(q_{1},\epsilon,\delta,\eta,\kappa_{1},p,r_{H},r_{I},\mathbbm{h},N)>0
, such that for any
\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
:

\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]}\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p-2\epsilon}[D_{T}^{k}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\,d\sigma dr+\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}=k}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}|D_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}D_{Y_{*}}^{k_{2}+1}D_{T}^{k_{3}}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+|D_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}D_{Y_{*}}^{k_{2}}D_{T}^{k_{3}}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\\
+\upzeta(r)(|D_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}D_{Y_{*}}^{k_{2}}D_{T}^{k_{3}+1}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+|D_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}+1}D_{Y_{*}}^{k_{2}}D_{T}^{k_{3}}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})r^{-1}(r^{-1}\rho_{+})^{2\epsilon}\mathcal{E}_{p}[D_{T}^{k}\psi_{\geq\ell}]+(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})r^{-3}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\delta-2}|D_{T}^{k+1}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[D_{T}^{k}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}+r_{+}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}r^{-1+2\epsilon},1\}r^{-2}|r^{3}D_{T}^{k}(G_{A})_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+(1-\upzeta)|D_{T}^{k+1}(G_{A})_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\,d\tau.

(2.19)

The estimate (
2.19
) remains valid when
\kappa_{+}>\kappa_{1}
and
|q|\geq q_{1}
under the assumption of Condition
1
below.

We state the following condition, motivated in
[
Gaj26
]
]§7.4]:

Condition 1

(Quantitative mode stability on the real axis away from extremality)
.

Let
\omega_{1}
and
\ell_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
be arbitrary. Let
(\omega,\ell)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}
, with
|\omega|\leq\omega_{1}
and
\ell\leq\ell_{1}
. Let
\kappa_{+}>\kappa_{1}
, with
\kappa_{1}
the constant from Theorem
2.5
.

Consider the ODE:

u^{\prime\prime}(r_{*})+(\omega^{2}-V_{\omega\ell}(r_{*}))u(r_{*})=0,

(2.20)

with

V_{\omega\ell}(r_{*}(r))=\ell(\ell+1)\Omega^{2}(r)r^{-2}-{q}^{2}\rho_{\infty}^{2}(r)+2{q}\omega\rho_{\infty}(r)+r^{-1}\Omega^{2}(r)\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}

Let
u_{+},u_{\infty}
be solutions to (
2.20
) that are normalized as follows:

\lim_{r_{*}\to-\infty}e^{i(\omega-qr_{+}^{-1})r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{+}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}u_{+}(r_{*})=\lim_{r_{*}\to\infty}e^{-i\omega r_{*}+iq\int_{0}^{r_{*}}\rho_{\infty}(r_{*}^{\prime})\,dr_{*}^{\prime}}u_{\infty}(r_{*})=1.

and consider their corresponding Wronskian:

\mathfrak{W}(\omega,\ell):=u_{+}(r_{*})\frac{du_{\infty}}{dr_{*}}(r_{*})-u_{\infty}(r_{*})\frac{du_{+}}{dr_{*}}(r_{*}).

Then there exists a constant
K=K(\omega_{1},\ell_{1},\kappa_{1})>0
such that:

|\mathfrak{W}|^{-1}\leq K.

Remark 2.1
.

Note that Condition
1
can be interpreted as a quantitative version of a
mode stability
statement on the real axis, i.e. the absence of purely oscillating mode solutions to (
2.7
) away from extremality; see also the discussion in §
1.1.7
.

2.8.
Further notation

We appeal to standard notational conventions regarding multiplications of uniform constants
C
and inequalities
\lesssim
,
\gtrsim
and
\sim
; we refer the reader
[
Gaj26
]
[§2.2] for a detailed overview.

3.
Stationary solutions

Before we give a precise formulation of the main theorem, we first introduce the notion of stationary (time-independent) solutions to (
2.9
) with
q\neq 0
,
A=\widehat{A}
and
G_{A}=0
, which play a key role in the main theorems.

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}
with
|m|\leq\ell
. Fix
r_{+}=1
. Let
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell},\mathfrak{w}^{\pm}_{\ell}:[r_{+},\infty)\to\mathbb{C}
, such that
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}Y_{\ell m}
and
\mathfrak{w}^{\pm}_{\ell}Y_{\ell m}
are solutions to (
2.9
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
G_{A}=0
. We write
\mathfrak{w}^{\pm}(r;q)
and
\mathfrak{w}(r;q)
when we need to emphasize the value of the corresponding charge parameter
q
.

Then:

(i)

We can fix
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell},\mathfrak{w}^{\pm}_{\ell}
uniquely by setting:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{+})=

\displaystyle\>1,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}^{-}_{\ell}(r)=

\displaystyle\>r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(1+O(r^{-1}))\quad(r>2),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}^{+}_{\ell}(r)=

\displaystyle\>r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(1+O(r^{-1}))\quad(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0)\quad(r>2),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}^{+}_{\ell}(r)=

\displaystyle\>r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}\log r(1+O(r^{-1}))\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0)\quad(r>2).

(ii)

We can express:

\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}={\alpha}_{+}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{+}+{\alpha}_{-}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-},

with
{\alpha}_{\pm}\in\mathbb{C}
, such that in the case
\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)
:

\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\log\left|\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}\right|=-\frac{\pi}{2}\left(\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}-\frac{1}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}{\cosh(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}\right)\right)\\
+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{\pi}{2}\left(\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}-\frac{1}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(\frac{|q|}{2}\frac{r_{-}+r_{+}}{r_{+}^{2}\kappa_{+}}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))\cosh^{2}(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}{\cosh(\pi(\frac{|q|}{2}\frac{r_{-}+r_{+}}{r_{+}^{2}\kappa_{+}}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))\cosh^{2}(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}\right)\right)\\
\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa_{+}\downarrow 0\\
\to\end{subarray}-\frac{\pi}{2}\left(\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}+\frac{1}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}{\cosh(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}\right)\right).

(3.1)

(iii)

Denote with
\alpha_{\pm}(\beta_{\ell})
the values of
\alpha_{\pm}
when
\beta_{\ell}\neq 0
and
q
is fixed. Then
\alpha_{-}(\beta_{\ell})=\alpha_{+}(-\beta_{\ell})
and we have that:

\lim_{\beta_{\ell}\to 0}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r)=\lim_{\beta_{\ell}\to 0}(\alpha_{+}\beta_{\ell})r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}\left[\log r+\frac{d}{d\beta_{\ell}}\bigg|_{\beta_{\ell}=0}\frac{\alpha_{-}(\beta_{\ell})}{\alpha_{+}(\beta_{\ell})}\right].

Therefore
\alpha_{\pm}
take the following values when
\beta_{\ell}=0
:

\displaystyle\alpha_{+}=

\displaystyle\>-\lim_{\beta_{\ell}\to 0}(\alpha_{+}(\beta_{\ell})\beta_{\ell}),

\displaystyle\alpha_{-}=

\displaystyle\>\alpha_{+}\frac{1}{i}\frac{d}{ds}\bigg|_{s=0}\frac{\alpha_{-}(is)}{\alpha_{+}(is)}.

In particular,

\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\textnormal{Im}\,\left(\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}\right)=-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\frac{d}{ds}\Big|_{s=0}\left(\frac{\alpha_{-}(is)}{\alpha_{+}(is)}\right)\right)=-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{\pi}{2}(1-\tanh(\pi|q|))\\
+\frac{\pi}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\left(1-\tanh\left(\pi\frac{|q|}{2}\frac{r_{-}+r_{+}}{r_{+}^{2}\kappa_{+}}\right)-2\tanh(\pi|q|)\right)\\
\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa_{+}\downarrow 0\\
\to\end{subarray}-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{\pi}{2}(1+\tanh(\pi|q|).

(3.2)

(iv)

The function
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}
is non-vanishing.

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle w_{\ell}:=

\displaystyle\>e^{-iq\int_{r^{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\,dr^{\prime}+iq\int_{r^{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})(\Omega^{2}-1+\mathbbm{h})(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell},

\displaystyle w_{\ell}^{\pm}:=

\displaystyle\>e^{-iq\int_{r^{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\,dr^{\prime}+iq\int_{r^{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})(\Omega^{2}-1+\mathbbm{h})(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{\pm}.

Then
w_{\ell}Y_{\ell m}
and
w_{\ell m}^{\pm}Y_{\ell m}
are solutions to (
2.7
) with
A=\widetilde{A}
and
G_{A}=0
. In other words, they are solutions to (
2.11
) with
\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}=\Omega^{-2}
.

Note that we can equivalently express:

\displaystyle w_{\ell}:=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{+}^{-1}r_{*}-iq\int_{r^{\sharp}}^{r}(\Omega^{-2}\rho_{+})(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}-iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell},

\displaystyle w_{\ell}^{\pm}:=

\displaystyle\>e^{iqr_{+}^{-1}r_{*}-iq\int_{r^{\sharp}}^{r}(\Omega^{-2}\rho_{+})(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}-iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{\pm}.

Then (
2.11
) with
\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}=\Omega^{-2}
is equivalent to:

\frac{d}{dr}\left(\Omega^{2}r^{2}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{-1}w_{\ell})\right)+\left[q^{2}r^{2}(r^{2}\Omega)^{-2}-\ell(\ell+1)\right]r^{-1}w_{\ell}=0.

(3.3)

Assume for convenience that
r_{+}=1
.

We first consider the case
\kappa_{+}=0
. By changing variables to
z=\frac{2iq}{r-1}
, we obtain the equation:

\frac{d^{2}(r^{-1}w_{\ell})}{dz^{2}}+\left[-\frac{1}{4}-\frac{iq}{z}+\frac{q^{2}-\ell(\ell+1)}{z^{2}}\right]r^{-1}w_{\ell}=0.

This is the
Whittaker equation
; see for example
[
NIS
]
[§13.4]. Using the asymptotic properties of Whittaker functions as
|z|\to\infty
, it follows that: we can express:

r^{-1}w_{\ell}(r)=c_{q}\cdot W_{-iq,\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}(2iq(r-1)^{-1}),

for some constant
c_{q}\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}
. The Whittaker function
W_{-iq,\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}(2iq(r-1)^{-1})
has the following asymptotics as
z\to 0
when
\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)
(see
[
NIS
]
[§13.14]):

c_{q}^{-1}w_{\ell}(r)=rW_{-iq,\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}(2iq(r-1)^{-1})=\frac{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\beta_{\ell}+iq)}(2iq)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}(1+O(r^{-1}))\\
+(2iq)^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}\frac{\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\beta_{\ell}+iq)}r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}(1+O(r^{-1}))\\
=\frac{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\beta_{\ell}+iq)}(2iq)^{\frac{1}{2}}|2q|^{\frac{i}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}e^{-\frac{\pi}{4}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}(1+O(r^{-1}))\\
+(2iq)^{\frac{1}{2}}|2q|^{-\frac{i}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}e^{\frac{\pi}{4}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}\frac{\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\beta_{\ell}+iq)}r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}(1+O(r^{-1})).

We therefore conclude that

\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}=|2q|^{-i\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}\frac{\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell})}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell})}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\beta_{\ell}+iq)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\beta_{\ell}+iq)}e^{-\frac{\pi}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q).\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}

From this, it follows that:

\log\left|\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}\right|=-\frac{\pi}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}+\frac{1}{2}\log\left|\frac{\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell})}{\Gamma(+\beta_{\ell})}\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\log\left|\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+iq)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+iq)}\right|^{2}\\
=-\frac{\pi}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}+\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}{\cosh(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}\right).

Now consider the case
\kappa_{+}>0
. Fix
r_{+}=1
. Write
2\kappa_{+}:=r_{+}-r_{-}
. Then we introduce the following change of variable and rescalings:

\displaystyle z=

\displaystyle\>\frac{r-r_{+}}{r-r_{-}},

\displaystyle r^{-1}w_{\ell}(r(z))=

\displaystyle\>z^{i\frac{q}{2}\kappa_{+}^{-1}}(1-z)^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta_{\ell}}F(z).

to obtain from (
3.3
) the following equation for
F
:

z(1-z)\frac{d^{2}F}{dz^{2}}+(c-(a+b+1)z)\frac{dF}{dz}-abF=0,

with
a=i\frac{q}{2}\kappa_{+}^{-1}(1-r_{-})+\frac{1-\beta_{\ell}}{2}
,
b=i\frac{q}{2}\kappa_{+}^{-1}(1+r_{-})+\frac{1-\beta_{\ell}}{2}
,
c=1+iq\kappa_{+}^{-1}
. The above ODE is a hypergeometric equation, see
[
NIS
]
[§15.10], and we can therefore write:

r^{-1}w_{\ell}(r)=c_{q,\kappa_{+}}\left(\frac{r-1}{r-r_{-}}\right)^{i\frac{q}{2}\kappa_{+}^{-1}}\left(\frac{1-r_{-}}{r-r_{-}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\beta_{\ell}}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(a,b,c\;\frac{r-1}{r-r_{-}}\right),

with
{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(a,b,c\;\frac{r-r_{+}}{r-r_{-}}\right)
the regularized Gauss hypergeometric function. It is straightforward to verify that
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}
is smooth at
r=1
.

Furthermore, by applying
[
NIS
]
[§15.8.4] we obtain the following large-
r
asymptotics (
z\uparrow 1
) :

\pi(\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell}))^{-1}c_{q,\kappa_{+}}^{-1}w_{\ell}(r)=\frac{r\left(\frac{r-r_{-}}{1-r_{-}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+i\frac{q}{2}(1+r_{-})\kappa_{+}^{-1})\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq)\Gamma(1-\beta_{\ell})}(1+O(r^{-1}))\\
-\frac{r\left(\frac{r-r_{-}}{1-r_{-}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+i\frac{q}{2}(1+r_{-})\kappa_{+}^{-1})\Gamma(1+\beta_{\ell})}(1+O(r^{-1})).

We therefore conclude that:

\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}=(1-r_{-})^{\beta_{\ell}}\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta_{\ell})}{\Gamma(1+\beta_{\ell})}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq)}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+i\frac{q}{2}(1+r_{-})\kappa_{+}^{-1})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+i\frac{q}{2}(1+r_{-})\kappa_{+}^{-1})}

From this, it follows that for
\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)
:

\log\left|\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}\right|=\frac{1}{2}\log\left|\frac{\Gamma(1-\beta_{\ell})}{\Gamma(1+\beta_{\ell})}\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\log\left|\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+iq)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+iq)}\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\log\left|\frac{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+i\frac{q}{2}(1+r_{-})\kappa_{+}^{-1})}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+i\frac{q}{2}(1+r_{-})\kappa_{+}^{-1})}\right|^{2}\\
=\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}{\cosh(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}\right)+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(\frac{|q|}{2}\frac{r_{-}+1}{\kappa_{+}}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}{\cosh(\pi(\frac{|q|}{2}\frac{r_{-}+1}{\kappa_{+}}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}\right)\\
=-\frac{\pi}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}-\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}{\cosh(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}\right)\\
+\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\left(\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}-\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(\frac{|q|}{2}\frac{r_{-}+1}{\kappa_{+}}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))\cosh^{2}(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}{\cosh(\pi(\frac{|q|}{2}\frac{r_{-}+1}{\kappa_{+}}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))\cosh^{2}(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}\right)\right).

Note that the last term vanishes in the extremal limit
\kappa_{+}\to 1
.

Now consider (iii). We apply Taylor’s theorem to obtain:

\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}{\cosh(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}\right)=-\frac{\pi}{2}\tanh(\pi|q|)\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}+O(|\beta|^{2}).

Therefore,

\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\frac{d}{ds}\Big|_{s=0}\left(\frac{\alpha_{-}(is)}{\alpha_{+}(is)}\right)\right)=-\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\frac{d}{ds}\Big|_{s=0}\log\left(\frac{\alpha_{-}(is)}{\alpha_{+}(is)}\right)\right)=-\frac{d}{ds}\Big|_{s=0}\log\left|\frac{\alpha_{-}(is)}{\alpha_{+}(is)}\right|\\
=+\frac{\pi}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)-\pi\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\tanh(\pi|q|)+\frac{1}{2}\pi\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\left(1-2\tanh\left(\pi\frac{|q|}{2}\frac{r_{-}+1}{\kappa_{+}}\right)-4\tanh(\pi|q|)\right).

We now rewrite for
s=\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}
:

\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r)=\frac{1}{i}\alpha_{+}(is)sr^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}\left[\frac{1}{s}e^{\frac{is}{2}\log r}+\frac{1}{s}\frac{\alpha_{-}(is)}{\alpha_{+}(is)}e^{-i\frac{s}{2}\log r}\right]\\
=\frac{1}{i}\alpha_{+}(is)sr^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}e^{i\frac{s}{2}\log r}\left[\frac{1}{s}(e^{+i\frac{s}{2}\log r}-1)-\frac{1}{s}(e^{-i\frac{s}{2}\log r}-1)+\frac{1}{s}\left(\frac{\alpha_{-}(is)}{\alpha_{+}(is)}-(-1)\right)e^{-i\frac{s}{2}\log r}\right]

By (ii), we have that
\lim_{\beta_{\ell}\to 0}\beta_{\ell}\alpha_{+}(\beta_{\ell})=-\lim_{\beta_{\ell}\to 0}\beta_{\ell}\alpha_{-}(\beta_{\ell})
is well-defined, so after taking the limit
s\downarrow 0
, we obtain:

\lim_{s\to 0}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r)=-\lim_{s\to 0}(\alpha_{+}(is)is)r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}\left[\log r+\frac{1}{i}\frac{d}{ds}\bigg|_{s=0}\frac{\alpha_{-}(is)}{\alpha_{+}(is)}\right].

Since this limit must correspond to a stationary solution in the
\beta_{\ell}=0
case, we conclude the identities for
\alpha_{\pm}
when
\beta_{\ell}=0
.

Finally, we turn to (iv). Write
w=w_{\ell}
. Then for
(\cdot)^{\prime}:=\frac{d}{dr_{*}}
, we have that
w^{\prime\prime}(r_{*})=V_{\ell}(r(r_{*}))w(r_{*})
, with

V_{\ell}(r)=\ell(\ell+1)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}-{q}^{2}r^{-2}+\Omega^{2}r^{-1}\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}.

This follows by
[
Gaj26
]
[Proposition 5.1]. Since
V_{\ell}
is real-valued, we obtain
\textnormal{Re}\,(i\overline{w}w^{\prime\prime})=0
, so:

\frac{d}{dr_{*}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(i\overline{w}w^{\prime}\right)=0.

Then

\lim_{r_{*}\to-\infty}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(i\overline{w}w^{\prime}\right)(r_{*})=-qr_{+}^{-1}|w|(-\infty)=-qr_{+}^{-1}.

Hence,
\textnormal{Re}\,\left(i\overline{w}w^{\prime}\right)(r_{*})=qr_{+}^{-1}\neq 0
for all
r_{*}\in\mathbb{R}
, which implies that
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}
is non-vanishing.
∎

Remark 3.1
.

For
q\neq 0
, the stationary solution
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-}Y_{\ell m}
is not smooth in
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{M,Q}
at
\mathcal{I}^{+}=\{x=0\}
. This stands in contrast with the
q=0
case, where
r^{-1}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-}(r)=Q_{\ell}(r_{+}^{-2}\kappa_{+}^{-1}(r-r_{+})+1)
is a Legendre polynomial of the second kind when
\kappa_{+}>0
and
r^{-1}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-}(r)=(r-r_{+})^{-1-\ell}
when
\kappa_{+}=0
, so
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{\pm}Y_{\ell m}
are both smooth at
\mathcal{I}^{+}
.

Corollary 3.2
.

When
\beta_{\ell}\in i[0,\infty)
, the following estimates hold for all
\kappa_{+}\geq 0
: there exists an
\eta_{0}>0
, such that:

\displaystyle\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\log\left|\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}\right|\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\pi}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}-\eta_{0}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\left|\frac{\cosh(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell})}{\cosh(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell})}\right|\right)\quad

\displaystyle(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),

(3.4)

\displaystyle\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\textnormal{Im}\,\left(\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}\right)\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\pi}{2}\left(1-\tanh(\pi|q|)\right)+\eta_{0}\quad

\displaystyle(\beta_{\ell}=0).

(3.5)

Proof.

Consider (
3.2
). Using the monotonicity of
\tanh
and the fact that
|q|\geq\frac{1}{2}
, we obtain the desired inequality by using that:

1-3\tanh\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)=\frac{2(2-e^{\pi})}{e^{\pi}+1}<0,

using that
e^{\pi}>e^{3}>e^{\log 2}
.

Now consider (
3.1
). Write
\beta_{\ell}=is
. Note that:

\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}-\frac{1}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(|q|\frac{r_{-}+1}{1-r_{-}}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))\cosh^{2}(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}{\cosh(\pi(|q|\frac{r_{-}+1}{1-r_{-}}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))\cosh^{2}(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}\right)\leq\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}-\frac{3}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}{\cosh(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}\right)\\
\to 0\quad\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}\to 0.

Furthermore,

\frac{d}{ds}\left(s-\frac{3}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}s))}{\cosh(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}s))}\right)\right)=1-\frac{3}{2}\tanh(\pi(|q|+s))-\frac{3}{2}\tanh(\pi(|q|-s))\\
\leq 1-3\tanh(\pi|q|)\leq 1-3\tanh(\frac{\pi}{2})<0,

where we used that
2|q|-|\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}|>0
when
\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}\neq 0
to arrive at the first inequality above.
Hence,

\left(\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}-\frac{3}{\pi}\log\left(\frac{\cosh(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}))}{\cosh(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}))}\right)\right)<0.\qed

4.
Precise statements of the main theorems

In this section, we give precise statements of the main results proved in the paper. Theorem
4.1
is the main result of the present paper and it establishes the precise, global late-time asymptotics of solutions
\psi
to (
2.7
). The theorem illustrates how the late-time asymptotics are dictated by a global function
\Psi
that have a null infinity contribution
\Psi^{\infty}
and, in the
|Q|=M
case, also an event horizon contribution
\Psi^{+}
and are closely related to solutions to (
2.10
).

In the
|Q|=M
case, when considering
\psi|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
,
\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}
dominate the late-time asymptotics, whereas for
\psi|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
,
\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}
dominate the late-time asymptotics. We also show that when considering instead
D_{T}\psi
, the reverse is true:
\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}
now dominate the late-time asymptotics of
D_{T}\psi|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
, whereas
\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}
dominate the late-time asymptotics of
D_{T}\psi|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}
.

Theorem 4.1

(Late-time pointwise asymptotics)
.

Let
\psi
be a solution to (
2.9
) with
\mathfrak{q}\neq 0
and

A=\widehat{A}=-Qr^{-1}d\tau
, and write
q=\mathfrak{q}Q
. Let
r_{H}>r_{+}
. Let
\beta_{\ell}=\sqrt{(2\ell+1)^{2}-4q^{2}}
. If
\kappa_{+}>\kappa_{1}
and
|q|>q_{1}
, assume moreover that Condition
1
holds.

(i)

Let
N_{1},N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
l\in\{0,1\}
and
\nu,\epsilon>0
. For arbitrary small
\nu>0
, there exists a constant
C=C(\nu,\epsilon,N_{1},N_{2})>0
, such that:

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}||D_{T}^{l}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}(\psi-\Psi)||_{L^{\infty}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}

(4.1)

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k\leq 2}\sqrt{E_{N_{1}+1,N_{2}+1+l,\epsilon}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\psi]}(\tau+1)^{-1+\frac{1}{2}\nu-N_{1}-\frac{l}{2}}(r^{-1}\Omega\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}},

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}||X^{l}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}(\psi-\Psi)||_{L^{\infty}(S^{2}_{\tau,r})}

(4.2)

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa_{+}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{k\leq 2}\sqrt{E_{N_{1}+1,N_{2}+1+l,\epsilon}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\psi]}(\tau+1)^{-1+\frac{1}{2}\nu-N_{1}-\frac{l}{2}}(r^{-1}\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}

\displaystyle+Ce^{-c\kappa_{+}\tau}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\sqrt{\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}X^{l}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\psi]\,d\sigma dr}\quad(|Q|<M),

(4.3)

with
\Psi=\Psi^{\infty}+\Psi^{+}
, where

\displaystyle\Psi^{\infty}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi):=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r;q)}{\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r;q)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}\\
|m|\leq\ell\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi](\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r;q)Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\varphi),

\displaystyle\Psi^{+}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi):=

\displaystyle\>e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}\left(r_{+}+\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{r-r_{+}};-q\right)}{\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}\left(r_{+}+\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{r-r_{+}};-q\right)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\>\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}\\
|m|\leq\ell\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi](\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}\left(\tau,r_{+}+\frac{r_{+}^{2}}{r-r_{+}};-q\right)Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\varphi),

where:

•

\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]
and
\mathfrak{h}_{\ell m}[\psi]
are integrals that are linear functionals of derivatives of initial data
(\psi,\partial_{\tau}\psi)|_{\tau=0}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{\ell m}[\psi]=0
for all data if
|Q|<M
; see the precise definitions in Proposition
8.1
and Corollary
8.2
,

•

The constants
\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]
and, in the case
|Q|=M
, also
\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]
, each vanish only for a codimension-1 subset of initial data,

•

the constants
E_{N_{1}+1,N_{2}+1+l,\epsilon}[\not{\nabla}^{k}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi]
are weighted, initial data energy norms that are defined in Proposition
9.1
,

•

the functions
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell,\pm}Y_{\ell m}
are
\tau
-independent solutions to (
2.7
) with
A=\widehat{A}
, satisfying
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}=\alpha_{+}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{+}+\alpha_{-}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-}
, with

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-}(r;q)=

\displaystyle\>r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(1+O(r^{-1}))\quad(r>2r_{+}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}^{+}_{\ell}(r;q)=

\displaystyle\>r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(1+O(r^{-1}))\quad(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0)\quad(r>2r_{+}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}^{+}_{\ell}(r;q)=

\displaystyle\>r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}\log r(1+O(r^{-1}))\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0)\quad(r>2r_{+}),

and
\alpha_{\pm}(q)\in\mathbb{C}
are constants such that
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r;q)={\alpha}_{+}(q)\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{+}(r;q)+{\alpha}_{-}(q)\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-}(r;q)
satisfies
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{+};q)=1
(see also Proposition
3.1
),

•

the functions
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell,\pm}^{0}Y_{\ell m}
are
\tau
-independent solutions to (
2.10
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
\mathbbm{h}=0
, satisfying:

\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}=\begin{cases}\alpha_{+}r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}+\alpha_{-}r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}\quad&(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0),\\
\alpha_{+}r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}\log r+\alpha_{-}r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}&(\beta_{\ell}=0).\end{cases}

•

The functions
(\Psi_{0})^{\infty}_{\ell m}Y_{\ell m}
, with
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}
, are solutions to (
2.10
) with
A=\widehat{A}
, with:

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r;q):=\begin{cases}\beta_{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-(n+\frac{1}{2})\beta_{\ell}+iq}\\
\times{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq-\left(n+\frac{1}{2}\right)\beta_{\ell};-\frac{\tau+1}{2r}\right),\quad\textnormal{when}\>\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1),\\
\mbox{}\\
\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\zeta^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-(n+\frac{1}{2})\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+iq}\\
\times{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq-\left(n+\frac{1}{2}\right)\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell};-\frac{\tau+1}{2r}\right),\\
\textnormal{when}\>\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty),\\
\mbox{}\\
(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tau+1)^{-ix}e^{-\eta x}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq-ix;-\frac{\tau+1}{2r}\right)\,dx,\\
\textnormal{when}\>\beta_{\ell}=0,\end{cases}

(4.4)

with
N_{\beta_{\ell}}=\lceil(\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell})^{-1}\rceil\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,b,c;\cdot)
denoting regularized Gauss hypergeometric functions, see for example
[
NIS
]
[§15.2] for a definition, and where
\zeta,\eta\in\mathbb{C}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\zeta(q):=

\displaystyle-\frac{2^{\beta_{\ell}}\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq)}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq)}\frac{\alpha_{-}(q)}{\alpha_{+}(q)},

\displaystyle\eta(q):=

\displaystyle\>i\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\left[\frac{\alpha_{-}(q)}{\alpha_{+}(q)}-\log 2+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\right],

where
\gamma_{\rm Euler}
is the Euler–Mascheroni constant.

(ii)

In particular, we obtain along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
:

(\Psi|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}})_{\ell m}(\tau)=\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]\begin{cases}\beta_{\ell}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\frac{\zeta^{n}(q)(1+\tau)^{-n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-(n+\frac{1}{2})\beta_{\ell}\right)}\\
+e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}O_{\infty}((\tau+1)^{-1-\beta_{\ell}}),\quad&(\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)),\\
\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n}(q)(1+\tau)^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-(n+\frac{1}{2})\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}\right)}\\
+e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}O_{\infty}((\tau+1)^{-1}),\quad&(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),\\
\frac{-i}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq\right)}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}\left[(\log(1+\tau))^{-1}+O((\log(1+\tau))^{-2})\right]\\
+e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}\frac{1}{\log(1+\tau)}O_{\infty}((\tau+1)^{-1}),\quad&(\beta_{\ell}=0).\end{cases}

Let
r_{0}>r_{+}
. Then we obtain along
\{r=r_{0}\}
:

\displaystyle(\Psi)_{\ell m}(\tau,r_{0})=

\displaystyle-2^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}(q)\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1-\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{0};q)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}(q)\frac{(1+\tau)^{-n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+iq)\Gamma(-(n+1)\beta_{\ell})}\right]

\displaystyle-2^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}(-q)\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1-\beta_{\ell}}e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{+}+r_{+}^{2}(r_{0}-r_{+})^{-1};-q)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}(-q)(\beta_{\ell})\frac{(1+\tau)^{-n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}-iq)\Gamma(-(n+1)\beta_{\ell})}\right]

\displaystyle+O_{\infty}\left((1+\tau)^{-2}\right)+e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}O_{\infty}\left((1+\tau)^{-2}\right),\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)),

\displaystyle(\Psi)_{\ell m}(\tau,r_{0})=

\displaystyle-2^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}(q)\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{0};q)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\zeta^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n}(q)\frac{(1+\tau)^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+iq)\Gamma(-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)(n+1)\beta_{\ell})}\right]

\displaystyle-2^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}(-q)\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{+}+r_{+}^{2}(r_{0}-r_{+})^{-1};–q)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\zeta^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n}(-q)\frac{(1+\tau)^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}-iq)\Gamma((n+1)\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell})}\right]

\displaystyle+O_{\infty}\left((1+\tau)^{-2}\right)+e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}O_{\infty}\left((1+\tau)^{-2}\right),\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),

\displaystyle(\Psi)_{\ell m}(\tau,r_{0})=

\displaystyle-\frac{2^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{0};q)\left[(\log(1+\tau))^{-2}(\tau+1)^{-1}+(\log(1+\tau))^{-3}O_{\infty}((\tau+1)^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{2^{\frac{1}{2}-iq}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-iq)}e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{+}+r_{+}^{2}(r_{0}-r_{+})^{-1};-q)

\displaystyle\times\left[(\log(1+\tau))^{-2}(\tau+1)^{-1}+(\log(1+\tau))^{-3}O_{\infty}((\tau+1)^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+O_{\infty}((1+\tau)^{-2})+e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}O_{\infty}((1+\tau)^{-2}),\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0).

We obtain along
\mathcal{H}^{+}
:

\displaystyle(\Psi)_{\ell m}(\tau,r_{+})=

\displaystyle\>\beta_{\ell}\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}-iq}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\frac{\zeta^{n}(-q)(1+\tau)^{-n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-iq-(n+\frac{1}{2})\beta_{\ell}\right)}

\displaystyle-2^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}(q)\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1-\beta_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}(q)\frac{(1+\tau)^{-n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+iq)\Gamma(-(n+1)\beta_{\ell})}\right]

\displaystyle+O_{\infty}\left((1+\tau)^{-2}\right),\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)),

\displaystyle(\Psi)_{\ell m}(\tau,r_{+})=

\displaystyle\>-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}-iq}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n}(-q)(1+\tau)^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-iq+(n+\frac{1}{2})\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}\right)}

\displaystyle-2^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}(q)\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\zeta^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n}(q)\frac{(1+\tau)^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+iq)\Gamma(-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)(n+1)\beta_{\ell})}\right]

\displaystyle+O_{\infty}\left((1+\tau)^{-2}\right),\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),

\displaystyle(\Psi)_{\ell m}(\tau,r_{+})=

\displaystyle\>\frac{-i}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-iq\right)}\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-iq}\left[(\log(1+\tau))^{-1}+O((\log(1+\tau))^{-2})\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{2^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]\left[(\log(1+\tau))^{-2}(\tau+1)^{-1}+(\log(1+\tau))^{-3}O_{\infty}((\tau+1)^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+O_{\infty}((1+\tau)^{-2}),\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0).

(iii)

We obtain moreover the following late-time asymptotics for
D_{T}\Psi
along
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and
\mathcal{I}^{+}
:

\displaystyle(D_{T}\Psi|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}})_{\ell m}(\tau)=

\displaystyle\>2^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}qr_{+}^{-1}\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}(-q)\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1-\beta_{\ell}}e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{+};-q)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}(-q)(\beta_{\ell})\frac{(1+\tau)^{-n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}-iq)\Gamma(-(n+1)\beta_{\ell})}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>O_{\infty}((\tau+r_{+})^{-\frac{3+\beta_{\ell}}{2}}),\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1),

\displaystyle(D_{T}\Psi|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}})_{\ell m}(\tau)=

\displaystyle\>2^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]qr_{+}^{-1}\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}(-q)\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{+};–q)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\zeta^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n}(-q)\frac{(1+\tau)^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}-iq)\Gamma((n+1)\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell})}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>O_{\infty}((\tau+r_{+})^{-\frac{3}{2}}),\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),

\displaystyle(D_{T}\Psi|_{\mathcal{I}^{+}})_{\ell m}(\tau)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{2^{\frac{1}{2}-iq}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-iq)}qr_{+}^{-1}e^{-iqr_{+}^{-1}\tau}\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{+}+r_{+}^{2}(r_{0}-r_{+})^{-1};-q)

\displaystyle\times\left[(\log(1+\tau))^{-2}(\tau+1)^{-1}+(\log(1+\tau))^{-3}O_{\infty}((\tau+1)^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>O_{\infty}((\tau+r_{+})^{-\frac{3}{2}}),\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0),

\displaystyle(D_{T}\Psi|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}})_{\ell m}(\tau)=

\displaystyle\>-2^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}qr_{+}^{-1}\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}(q)\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1-\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{+};q)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}(q)(\beta_{\ell})\frac{(1+\tau)^{-n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+iq)\Gamma(-(n+1)\beta_{\ell})}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>O_{\infty}((\tau+r_{+})^{-\frac{3+\beta_{\ell}}{2}}),\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1),

\displaystyle(D_{T}\Psi|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}})_{\ell m}(\tau)=

\displaystyle\>-2^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}qr_{+}^{-1}\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}(q)\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{+};q)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\zeta^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n}(q)\frac{(1+\tau)^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+iq)\Gamma(-(n+1)\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell})}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>O_{\infty}((\tau+r_{+})^{-\frac{3}{2}}),\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),

\displaystyle(D_{T}\Psi|_{\mathcal{H}^{+}})_{\ell m}(\tau)=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{2^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}qr_{+}^{-1}\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r_{+};q)

\displaystyle\times\left[(\log(1+\tau))^{-2}(\tau+1)^{-1}+(\log(1+\tau))^{-3}O_{\infty}((\tau+1)^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>O_{\infty}((\tau+r_{+})^{-\frac{3}{2}}),\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0).

Theorem
4.1
is proved in §
10.1
and follows from energy decay estimates, which encompass the following theorem:

Theorem 4.2

(Late time energy decay asymptotics)
.

Let
\psi
be as in Theorem
4.1
. If
\kappa_{+}>\kappa_{1}
and
|q|>q_{1}
, assume moreover that Condition
1
holds. Let
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
1<p<2
. For all
\nu>0
and
\epsilon>0
suitably small, there exists a constant
C=C(\nu,\epsilon,N_{1},N_{2},p)>0
such that for
|\gamma|\leq N_{2}
:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p-3\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}((1+\tau)TK)^{N_{1}}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\leq\begin{cases}CE_{N_{1},N_{2},\epsilon}[\psi](1+\tau)^{p-2-2\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0),\\
CE_{N_{1},N_{2},\epsilon}[\psi]\frac{(1+\tau)^{p-2}}{\log^{2}(1+\tau)}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0)\end{cases}

Furthermore, for
p\in[0,1-\epsilon]

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}((1+\tau)TK)^{N_{1}}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\leq\begin{cases}CE_{N_{1}+1,N_{2}+1,\epsilon}[\psi](1+\tau)^{p-2-2\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0),\\
CE_{N_{1}+1,N_{2}+1,\epsilon}[\psi]\frac{(1+\tau)^{p-2}}{\log^{2}(1+\tau)}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0)\end{cases}

and

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}((1+\tau)TK)^{N_{1}}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\leq\begin{cases}CE_{N_{1},N_{2}+2,\epsilon}[\psi](1+\tau)^{-2\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0),\\
CE_{N_{1},N_{2}+2,\epsilon}[\psi]\frac{1}{\log^{2}(1+\tau)}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0).\end{cases}

Theorem
4.2
is proved in §
9.1
.

Using the precise pointwise and energy decay estimates above, we also derive asymptotic instabilities in the form of pointwise blow-up of (
r
-weighted) transversal derivatives along
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, and the concentration of non-degenerate energies at
\mathcal{H}^{+}
in the
|Q|=M
case (and, in the case of
r
-weighted energies, also at
\mathcal{I}^{+}
in the
|Q|\leq M
case).

Theorem 4.3

(Instabilities and energy concentration)
.

Let
\psi
be as in Theorem
4.1
. If
\kappa_{+}>\kappa_{1}
and
|q|>q_{1}
, assume moreover that Condition
1
holds. Let
\nu,\epsilon,\delta>0
be arbitrarily small. Let
\mathfrak{c}_{k}\in\mathbb{C}
and
\mathfrak{c}_{\rm int}\in(0,\infty)
be the constants defined in Proposition
A.7
.

(i)

Let
r_{H}>r_{+}
and
r_{I}<\infty
be arbitrary. Then for
\kappa_{+}\leq\kappa_{0}
:

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}(1+\tau)^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr\geq

\displaystyle\>\mathfrak{c}_{\rm int}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}=\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}\\
|m|\leq\ell\end{subarray}}|\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]|^{2}\quad

\displaystyle(|Q|=M,\>\beta_{0}\neq 0),

(4.5)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}\log^{2}(1+\tau)\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr\geq

\displaystyle\>\mathfrak{c}_{\rm int}|\mathfrak{H}_{00}[\psi]|^{2}\quad

\displaystyle(|Q|=M,\>\beta_{0}=0),

(4.6)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}(1+\tau)^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr\geq

\displaystyle\>\mathfrak{c}_{\rm int}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}=\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}\\
|m|\leq\ell\end{subarray}}|\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]|^{2}\quad

\displaystyle(\beta_{0}\neq 0),

(4.7)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}\log^{2}(1+\tau)\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{I}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr\geq

\displaystyle\>\mathfrak{c}_{\rm int}|\mathfrak{I}_{00}[\psi]|^{2}\quad

\displaystyle(\beta_{0}=0).

(4.8)

Furthermore,
\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}\log^{2}(1+\tau)(1+\tau)^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi]\,d\sigma dr=0
for all
\beta_{0}\in[0,1]\cup i(0,\infty)
.

(ii)

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}
. Then there exists a constant
C_{k}>0
, such that for all

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}(1+\tau)^{-k-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}{2}}|(r^{2}X)^{k+1}\psi_{\ell m}|(\tau,r_{+})\geq

\displaystyle\>|\mathfrak{c}_{k}||\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]|\quad

\displaystyle(|Q|=M,\>\beta_{\ell}\neq 0),

(4.9)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}\log(1+\tau)(1+\tau)^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}|(r^{2}X)^{k+1}\psi_{\ell m}|(\tau,r_{+})\geq

\displaystyle\>|\mathfrak{c}_{k}||\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]|\quad

\displaystyle(|Q|=M,\>\beta_{\ell}=0),

(4.10)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}(1+\tau)^{-k-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}{2}}|(r^{2}X)^{k+1}\psi_{\ell m}|(\tau,\infty)\geq

\displaystyle\>|\mathfrak{c}_{k}||\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]|\quad

\displaystyle(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0),

(4.11)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}\log(1+\tau)(1+\tau)^{-k-\frac{1}{2}}|(r^{2}X)^{k+1}\psi_{\ell m}|(\tau,\infty)\geq

\displaystyle\>|\mathfrak{c}_{k}||\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]|\quad

\displaystyle(\beta_{\ell}=0).

(4.12)

Theorem
4.3
is proved in §
11.1
.

5.
Higher-order
(\Omega^{-1}r)^{p}
-weighted energy estimates

In this section, we show that we can commute (
2.19
) with the differential operators
\{(r-r_{+})X,T,\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\}
. Since commutation with
T^{k}
follows immediately from the Killing property of
T
and commutation with
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
is an immediate consequence of the fact that we can commute with angular momentum Killing vector fields, since the background spacetime is spherically symmetric, it remains to show that we can commute with
((r-r_{+})X)^{k}
.

By (
2.12
), with
\mathbbm{h}\equiv 0
, we obtain:

r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}}=\partial_{\rho_{\infty}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\partial_{\rho_{\infty}}\psi)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi+2\partial_{\rho_{\infty}}T\psi+2iq\rho_{\infty}\partial_{\rho_{\infty}}\psi-\left[r\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}-iq\right]\psi.

(5.1)

By (
2.13
), with
\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\equiv 0
, we obtain:

r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}}=\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi+2\partial_{\rho_{+}}K\psi-2iq\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi\\
-\left[r\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+iq\right]\psi.

(5.2)

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

(i)

Let
\psi
be a solution to (
5.1
). Denote
\psi^{(N)}=(\rho_{\infty}\partial_{\rho_{\infty}})^{N}\psi
. Then:

\rho_{\infty}^{-1}(\rho_{\infty}\partial_{\rho_{\infty}})^{N}(\rho_{\infty}r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})=\partial_{\rho_{\infty}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\partial_{\rho_{\infty}}\psi^{(N)})+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N)}+2\partial_{\rho_{\infty}}T\psi^{(N)}\\
+2iq\rho_{\infty}\partial_{\rho_{\infty}}\psi^{(N)}+N(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{\infty}))\rho_{\infty}\partial_{\rho_{\infty}}\psi^{(N)}+N\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N-1)}\\
-\left[r\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}-iq(1+2N)-\frac{1}{2}N(N+1)+O_{\infty}(\rho_{\infty})\right]\psi^{(N)}+N\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}O_{\infty}(\rho_{\infty}^{0})\psi^{(n)}\\
+N(N-1)\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}O_{\infty}(\rho_{\infty}^{0})\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(n)}

(5.3)

(ii)

Let
\psi
be a solution to (
5.2
). Denote
\psi^{(N)}=(\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}})^{N}\psi
. Then:

\rho_{+}^{-1}(\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}})^{N}(\rho_{+}r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})=\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N)})+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N)}+2\partial_{\rho_{+}}K\psi^{(N)}\\
-2iq\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N)}+N(1-6\kappa_{+}r_{+})(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}))\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N)}+N\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N-1)}\\
-\left[r\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+iq(1+2N)-\frac{1}{2}N(N+1)(1-6\kappa_{+}r_{+})+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+})\right]\psi^{(N)}+N\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}^{0})\psi^{(n)}\\
+N(N-1)\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}^{0})\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(n)}.

(5.4)

Proof.

We will prove (ii). The proof of (i) proceeds entirely analogously to (ii), with
\rho_{+}
replaced by
\rho_{\infty}
and
q
replaced by
-q
.

The proof of (ii) proceeds by induction. The
N=0
case follows directly from (
5.2
). Suppose that (
5.4
) holds for
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then:

(\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}})^{N+1}(\rho_{+}r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})=\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\rho_{+}^{-1}\psi^{(N+1)}))+\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\rho_{+}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N)})+2\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}K\psi^{(N+1)}\\
-2iq\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\rho_{+}\psi^{(N+1)})+N(1-6\kappa_{+}r_{+})(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}))\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\rho_{+}\psi^{(N+1)})+N\rho_{+}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N)}\\
-\left[r\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+iq(1+2N)-\frac{1}{2}N(N+1)(1-6\kappa_{+}r_{+})+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+})\right]\rho_{+}\psi^{(N+1)}+(N+1)\rho_{+}\sum_{n=0}^{N}O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}^{0})\psi^{(n)}\\
+N(N+1)\rho_{+}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}^{0})\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(n)}.

We can expand further by applying Lemma
2.1
:

\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\rho_{+}^{-1}\psi^{(N+1)}))=\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N+1)})+\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\rho_{+}^{-1})\psi^{(N+1)})\\
=\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N+1)})+\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\rho_{+}(1-6\kappa_{+}+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}))\psi^{(N+1)})\\
=\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N+1)})+\rho_{+}^{2}(1-6\kappa_{+}r_{+})(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}))\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N+1)}+\rho_{+}(1-6\kappa_{+}r_{+})(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}))\psi^{(N+1)}.

Combining the above results in (
5.4
) with
N
replaced by
N+1
.
∎

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and let
\psi
be a solution to (
2.9
). Let
1<r_{H}<r_{I}<\infty
,
q\in\mathbb{R}
,
\epsilon>0
,
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
1<p<\min\{1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell},2\}+\epsilon
. Then there exist a constant
C=C(\mathbbm{h},\epsilon,p,r_{H},r_{I},N)>0
, such that for suitably small
\epsilon>0
:

\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}\mathcal{E}_{p-2\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}\upzeta\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq\ell}]+|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})r^{-1}(r^{-1}\rho_{+})^{2\epsilon}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq\ell}]+(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})r^{-3}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\epsilon-2}|D_{T}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\sum_{n\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{p-2\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\,d\sigma dr+C\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}=n}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p+2\epsilon}r^{-1}\rho_{+}r^{-2}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}=n}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}r^{-1+2\epsilon},1\}r^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}}(r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+\Omega^{2}r^{-2}(1-\upzeta)|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{n_{1}}T^{n_{2}+1}(r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

(5.5)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\mathbbm{h}\equiv 0
in
\{r\geq r_{I}\}
and
\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\equiv 0
in
\{r\leq r_{H}\}
. Indeed, we can simply apply the local energy estimates in
[
Gaj26
]
[Theorem 2.6] afterwards to obtain (
5.5
) in the more general setting.

In view of the similarity between (
5.4
) and (
5.3
), we can also restrict without loss of generality to
\{r\leq r_{H}\}
. The estimates in
\{r\geq r_{I}\}
proceed analogously to the estimates in
\{r\leq r_{H}\}
with
\kappa_{+}=0
.

We multiply both sides of (
5.4
) with
\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}
and take the real part to obtain:

\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\textnormal{Re}\,\left[\rho_{+}^{-1}(\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}})^{N}(\rho_{+}r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})\partial_{\rho_{+}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\partial_{\rho_{+}}((\Omega^{2}r^{-2})^{2}|\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi|^{2})\\
-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{R_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\partial_{\rho_{+}}(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2})+{\rm div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)+T(\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\partial_{\rho_{+}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}|^{2})\\
+N(1-6\kappa_{+}r_{+})(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}))\rho_{+}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N)}|^{2}-N\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\left(\textnormal{Re}\,\left[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N-1)}\cdot\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}\right]\right)\\
+N\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\textnormal{Re}\,\left[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N-1)}\cdot\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}\right]\\
-\chi_{r_{H}}\left[r\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+iq(1+2N)-\frac{1}{2}N(N+1)(1-6\kappa_{+}r_{+})+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+})\right](r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\textnormal{Re}\,\left[\psi^{(N)}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}\right]\\
+N\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}^{0})\textnormal{Re}\,\left[\psi^{(n)}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}\right]-N(N-1)(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}^{0})\textnormal{Re}\,\left[\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(n)}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}\right]\\
=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\Bigg(\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{4-p}|\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{R_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\chi_{R_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}N(N+1)(1-6\kappa_{+}r_{+})|\psi^{(N)}|^{2}\\
-N\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\textnormal{Re}\,\left[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N-1)}\cdot\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}\right]\Bigg)+T(\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\partial_{\rho_{+}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}|^{2})+{\rm div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)\\
+\left[\frac{p}{4}r^{2}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})+N(1-6\kappa_{+}r_{+})(1+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}))\rho_{+}\right](r^{-2}\Omega^{2})^{2-p}|\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi|^{2}\\
+\left[\frac{2-p}{4}r^{2}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})+N(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2}\rho_{+}^{-1}\right](r^{-1}\Omega)^{p}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N)}|^{2}-\frac{2-p}{4}N(N-1)(1-6\kappa_{+}r_{+})r^{2}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p}|\psi^{(N)}|^{2}\\
+Nr^{2}\frac{2-p}{2}\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})\textnormal{Re}\,\left[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N-1)}\cdot\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}\right]\\
+N\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}^{0})\textnormal{Re}\,\left[\psi^{(n)}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}\right]-N(N-1)\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}^{0})\textnormal{Re}\,\left[\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(n)}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}\right]\\
-\chi_{r_{H}}\left[r\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}+iq(1+2N)+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+})\right](r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}\textnormal{Re}\,\left[\psi^{(N)}\partial_{\rho_{+}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}\right]\\
-\frac{r^{2}}{2}\frac{d\chi_{r_{H}}}{dr}\left[(r^{-1}\Omega)^{4-p}|\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N)}|^{2}-|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N)}|^{2}+N\frac{2-p}{2}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})\textnormal{Re}\,\left[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N-1)}\cdot\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\overline{\psi}^{(N)}\right]\right]

Using that
\frac{d}{dr}(r^{-2}\Omega^{2})=2\kappa_{+}+2(1-6\kappa_{+}r_{+})\rho_{+}+O_{\infty}(\rho_{+}^{2})
by Lemma
2.1
and integrating the above identity, we obtain the estimate:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N)}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}(\kappa_{+}+\rho_{+})\left[|\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N)}|^{2}+(2-p)(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi^{(N)}|^{2}\right]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}|\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N)}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr+C\sum_{k+n\leq N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\psi^{(n)}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\sum_{k+n\leq N}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{H}+\frac{1}{2}(r_{H}-r_{+})\}}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k+1}\psi^{(n)}|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\psi^{(n)}|^{2}\right]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\chi_{r_{H}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})^{-1}\rho_{+}^{-2}|(\rho_{+}\partial_{\rho_{+}})^{N}(\rho_{+}r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

(5.6)

Note that
|\psi^{(N)}|^{2}=\rho_{+}^{2}|\partial_{\rho_{+}}\psi^{(N-1)}|^{2}
. Hence, we can estimate via a proof by induction. In the
N=1
case, the spacetime integrals on the RHS of (
5.6
) involving
\psi
and its derivatives can be estimated by (
2.19
). Suppose that (
5.6
) without the spacetime integrals involving
\psi
on the RHS holds for
N
replaced with
n
, where
n\leq N-1
. Then we can apply (
5.6
) to conclude the induction step. The spacetime integrals supported in
\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{H}+\frac{1}{2}(r_{H}-r_{+})\}
can finally be absorbed using (
2.19
).
∎

Corollary 5.3
.

Let
\gamma\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\epsilon>0
. Let
1<p<\min\{1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell},2\}+\epsilon
. Then

\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{2}}}\mathcal{E}_{p-2\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}\upzeta\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq\ell}]+|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{I}\}}(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})r^{-1}(r^{-1}\rho_{+})^{2\epsilon}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq\ell}]+(\rho_{+}+\kappa_{+})r^{-3}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\epsilon-2}|D_{T}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq C\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\sum_{n\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq\ell}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}r^{-1+2\epsilon},1\}r^{-2}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})_{\geq\ell}|^{2}+\Omega^{2}r^{-2}(1-\upzeta)|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T(r^{3}G_{\widehat{A}})_{\geq\ell}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

(5.7)

6.
Energy time-decay of time derivatives

In this section, we establish decay for the energies corresponding to the energy densities
\mathcal{E}_{p}[D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\psi]
(assuming appropriate decay for
G_{\widehat{A}}
), with
|\gamma|\leq N_{2}
and
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
,
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
arbitrary. While we do not establish energy time-decay for
\mathcal{E}_{p}[D_{\mathbf{Z}}^{\gamma}\psi]
, we establish time-decay after commuting with the operator
T\circ K
and show that the decay rate can be improved as we apply
T\circ K
additional times.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
,
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
q_{1}\in[0,\infty)
. Let
\psi
be a solution to (
2.9
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
|q|\leq q_{1}
. For all
\nu>0
, there exists an
\epsilon_{0}>0
, such that for all
0\leq\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0}
and
1+\epsilon\leq p\leq 1+\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q_{1}^{2}}+\epsilon
, there exists a constant
C=C(\mathbbm{h},\nu,\epsilon_{0},p,N_{1},N_{2},q_{1})>0
such that:

(1+\tau)^{2N_{1}-\nu}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\sum_{m\leq N_{1}}\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq N_{1}+N_{2}}\sum_{j\leq 2N_{1}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\sup_{\tau\in[0,\infty)}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{2-\epsilon+2\max\{m-1,0\}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p}r^{-2}|r^{3}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j}(TK)^{\max\{m-1,0\}}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{2m}\Big[\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}r^{-1+2\epsilon},1\}r^{-2}|r^{3}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j}(TK)^{m}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\\
+\Omega^{2}(1-\upzeta)|r\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j+1}(TK)^{m}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\Bigg]\,d\sigma drd\tau.

(6.1)

Proof.

We set
A=\widehat{A}
. For the sake of notational convenience, we write
G=G_{\widehat{A}}
. We first consider the case
N_{2}=0
and
N_{1}=1
. Let
0\leq\sigma<1
and define
\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\tau_{A}}:=\{\rho_{+}\leq\frac{1}{2}(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma}\}\cap\{r\geq 4(1+\tau_{A})^{\sigma}\}
. Let
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
. Then we can split the following integral:

\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau=\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau+\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\lesssim\overbrace{\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\tau_{A}}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}r^{2}|XTK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{=:J_{1}}+\overbrace{\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\tau_{A}}}|TTK\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}TK\psi|^{2}+|TK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{=:J_{2}}\\
+\overbrace{\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus(\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\tau_{A}}\cup\{r^{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r^{\sharp}+\eta\})}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{=:J_{3}}+\overbrace{\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r_{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r_{\sharp}+\eta\}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau}^{=:J_{4}}.

We will estimate each of the terms
J_{i}
separately.

Take
1+\epsilon^{\prime}\leq p^{\prime}\leq 1+\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}+\epsilon^{\prime}
, where
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
will be chosen suitably small.

To estimate
J_{1}
, we use that by (
2.11
) and (
2.13
)

r^{2}|XTK\psi|^{2}\lesssim\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{0}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]+r^{-2}|r^{3}TG|^{2}+r^{-2}|r^{3}G|^{2}.

(6.2)

Hence,

J_{1}\lesssim\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\tau_{A}}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}\left[\mathcal{E}_{0}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{0}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]\right]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}r^{-2}(|r^{3}TG|^{2}+|r^{3}G|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\lesssim(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1+p-2\epsilon-p^{\prime})}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\tau_{A}}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{1-p+2\epsilon}\left[\mathcal{E}_{0}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{0}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]\right]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1+p-p^{\prime})}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{\sigma(1+p-p^{\prime})}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}(|rTG|^{2}+|rG|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\lesssim(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1+p-2\epsilon-p^{\prime})}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p-1-2\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{p-1+2\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1+p-2\epsilon-p^{\prime})}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{\sigma(1+p-p^{\prime}-2\epsilon)}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}r^{-2}(|r^{3}TG|^{2}+|r^{3}G|^{2})\,d\sigma dr

We now apply (
5.7
) with
p=p^{\prime}
,
\epsilon=2\epsilon^{\prime}
and
|\gamma|=1
to obtain:

J_{1}\lesssim(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p^{\prime}}\rho_{+}^{1-4\epsilon^{\prime}}r^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}},1\}r^{-2}(|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(r^{3}G)|^{2}+|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(r^{3}TG)|^{2})\\
+(1-\upzeta)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{2}(r^{3}G)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}r^{-2}(|r^{3}TG|^{2}+|r^{3}G|^{2})\,d\sigma dr.

We can estimate
J_{2}
as follows:

J_{2}\lesssim(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-2\epsilon)}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\tau_{A}}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{-1+2\epsilon}\left[|TTK\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}TK\psi|^{2}+|TK\psi|^{2}\right]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\lesssim(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-2\epsilon)}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,\tau_{A}}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-1+2\epsilon}(|D_{T}T^{2}\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}D_{T}T\psi|^{2}+|D_{T}T\psi|^{2})\\
+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-1+2\epsilon}\rho_{+}^{2}(|T\psi|^{2}+|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}T\psi|^{2}+|T^{2}\psi|^{2})\,d\sigma drd\tau.

We apply (
5.5
) with
|\gamma|=0
,
p=p^{\prime}
,
\epsilon=2\epsilon^{\prime}
, to
T\psi
and
T^{2}\psi
to obtain:

J_{2}\lesssim C(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[T\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[T^{2}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+C(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p^{\prime}}\rho_{+}^{1-4\epsilon^{\prime}}r^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}},1\}r^{-2}(|r^{3}TG|^{2}+|r^{3}T^{2}G|^{2})\\
+(1-\upzeta)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|T^{3}(r^{3}G)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

We estimate
J_{3}
:

J_{3}\lesssim(1+\tau_{A})^{\sigma(1+4\epsilon^{\prime})}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{r_{\sharp}-\eta\leq r\leq r_{\sharp}+\eta\}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}-1-4\epsilon^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau.

We apply (
5.5
) with
|\gamma|=0
,
p=p^{\prime}
and
\epsilon=2\epsilon^{\prime}
to
TK\psi
to obtain:

J_{3}\lesssim C(1+\tau_{A})^{\sigma(1+4\epsilon^{\prime})}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+C(1+\tau_{A})^{\sigma(1+4\epsilon^{\prime})}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p^{\prime}}\rho_{+}^{1-4\epsilon^{\prime}}r^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}},1\}r^{-2}|r^{3}T^{j}TKG|^{2}+(1-\upzeta)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|r^{3}T^{j+1}TKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Finally, we can estimate
J_{4}
by directly applying (
5.7
) with
|\gamma|=0
,
p=1+\epsilon^{\prime}
and
\epsilon=2\epsilon^{\prime}
to obtain:

J_{4}\lesssim C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon^{\prime}}[TK\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon^{\prime}}[T^{2}K\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-1-\epsilon^{\prime}}\rho_{+}^{1-4\epsilon^{\prime}}r^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}},1\}r^{-2}|r^{3}TKG|^{2}+(1-\upzeta)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|T(r^{3}TKG)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

We conclude that:

\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim(1+\tau_{A})^{\sigma(1+4\epsilon^{\prime})}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[T^{2}K\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p^{\prime}}\rho_{+}^{1-4\epsilon^{\prime}}r^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}},1\}(1+\tau)^{2\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}r^{-2}|r^{3}T^{j}TKG|^{2}\\
+(1-\upzeta)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}(1+\tau)^{2\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}|r^{3}T^{j+1}TKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p^{\prime}}\rho_{+}^{1-4\epsilon^{\prime}}r^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}},1\}r^{-2}(|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(r^{3}G)|^{2}+|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(r^{3}TG)|^{2})\\
+(1-\upzeta)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|\mathbf{Z}T^{2}(r^{3}G)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}r^{-2}(|r^{3}TG|^{2}+|r^{3}G|^{2})\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq(1+\tau_{A})^{\sigma(1+4\epsilon^{\prime})}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[T^{2}K\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]\,d\sigma dr+(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\mathcal{G}[G],

(6.3)

with

\mathcal{G}[G]:=\sum_{j\leq 1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p^{\prime}}\rho_{+}^{1-4\epsilon^{\prime}}r^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}},1\}(1+\tau)^{2\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}r^{-2}|r^{3}T^{j}TKG|^{2}\\
+(1-\upzeta)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}(1+\tau)^{2\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}|r^{3}T^{j+1}TKG|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p^{\prime}}\rho_{+}^{1-4\epsilon^{\prime}}r^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}},1\}r^{-2}|(\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(r^{3}G)|^{2}+|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(r^{3}TG)|^{2})+(1-\upzeta)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{2}(r^{3}G)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\sup_{\tau\in[0,\infty)}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})+1+\epsilon^{\prime}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}r^{-2}(|r^{3}TG|^{2}+|r^{3}G|^{2})\,d\sigma dr.

Step 1:
\tau^{-1+2\epsilon^{\prime}}

Let
\{\tau_{j}^{(1)}\}
be a dyadic sequence. Taking
\sigma=0
,
\tau_{A}=\tau_{j}^{(1)}
and
\tau_{B}=\tau_{j+1}^{(1)}
in (
6.3
) and applying the mean-value theorem (“pigeonhole principle”), we obtain along a dyadic subsequence
\{\tau_{j}^{(2)}\}
of
\{\tau_{j}^{(1)}\}
:

(1+\tau_{j}^{(2)})\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{j}^{(2)}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[T^{2}K\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]\,d\sigma dr+\mathcal{G}[G]=:D_{0,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi].

(6.4)

Applying (
5.7
) with
|\gamma|=0
,
p=p^{\prime}
and
\epsilon=2\epsilon^{\prime}
, we in fact obtain for all
\tau\geq 0
:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}-4\epsilon^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim(1+\tau)^{-1}D_{0,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi].

Now we apply an interpolation argument. We split

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}r^{2}|XTK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}-4\epsilon^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r^{-1}\rho_{+}\leq(1+\tau)^{-1}\}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}r^{2}|XTK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r^{-1}\rho_{+}\geq(1+\tau)^{-1}\}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}r^{2}|XTK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}-4\epsilon^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau

and then apply (
6.2
) to further estimate the second term on the RHS, so that we obtain:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim(1+\tau)^{4\epsilon^{\prime}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}-4\epsilon^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+(1+\tau)^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}-4\epsilon^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}-4\epsilon^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+(1+\tau)^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{1-4\epsilon^{\prime}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}r^{-2}(|r^{3}TG|^{2}+|r^{3}G|^{2})\,d\sigma dr.

By applying (
5.5
) with
|\gamma|=1
to
\psi
and
T\psi
,
p=p^{\prime}
and
\epsilon=2\epsilon^{\prime}
once more, we conclude the first global-in-
\tau
decay result:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim(1+\tau)^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}}D_{0,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi].

(6.5)

Note that the above interpolation argument can also be used to conclude the
N_{1}=0
case of the theorem.

Step 2:
\tau^{-\frac{3}{2}}
decay

We will now improve the decay rate. We apply (
6.3
) again and we moreover apply (
6.5
) to estimate the first two integrals on the RHS. We obtain:

\int_{\tau_{A}}^{\tau_{B}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim(1+\tau_{A})^{\sigma-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}}D_{0,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi]+(1+\tau_{A})^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}}D_{0,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[T\psi]\\
+(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{A}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]\,d\sigma dr+(1+\tau_{A})^{-\sigma(1-4\epsilon^{\prime})}\mathcal{G}[G].

(6.6)

Let
\{\tau_{j}^{(1)}\}
be a dyadic sequence. Taking
\sigma=\frac{1}{2}
,
\tau_{A}=\tau_{j}^{(1)}
and
\tau_{B}=\tau_{j+1}^{(1)}
in (
6.6
) and applying the mean-value theorem, we obtain along a dyadic subsequence
\{\tau_{j}^{(2)}\}
of
\{\tau_{j}^{(1)}\}
:

(1+\tau_{j}^{(2)})^{\frac{3}{2}-4\epsilon^{\prime}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{j}^{(2)}}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim D_{0,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi]+(1+\tau_{j}^{(2)})^{-\frac{1}{2}}D_{0,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[T\psi]\\
+\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]\,d\sigma dr+\mathcal{G}[G]\\
=:D_{1,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi]+(1+\tau_{j}^{(2)})^{-\frac{1}{2}}D_{0,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[T\psi].

(6.7)

We repeat the interpolation argument at the end of Step 1 by considering
\{r^{-1}\rho_{+}\leq(1+\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\}
and
\{r^{-1}\rho_{+}\geq(1+\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\}
to conclude that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma dr\lesssim\tilde{D}_{1,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi](1+\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}+8\epsilon^{\prime}},

with

\tilde{D}_{1,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi]:=D_{1,p,\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi]+D_{0,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[T\psi].

Step 3:
\tau^{-2+\nu}
decay

Note that the only term that does not decay at least like
\tau^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}}
on the RHS of (
6.6
) when
\sigma=1
is
(1+\tau_{A})^{\sigma-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}}D_{0,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi]
.

By Step 2, we have that (
6.6
) still holds with
(1+\tau_{A})^{\sigma-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}}D_{0,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi]
replaced by

(1+\tau_{A})^{\sigma-\frac{3}{2}+4\epsilon^{\prime}}\tilde{D}_{1,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi]
.

We can therefore take
\sigma=\frac{3}{4}
and conclude that:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim\tilde{D}_{1,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi](1+\tau)^{-\frac{7}{4}+12\epsilon^{\prime}}.

Continuing this procedure iteratively, taking
\sigma=1-\frac{1}{2^{k}}
, we can show:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim\tilde{D}_{1,p^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}}[\psi](1+\tau)^{2-\frac{1}{2k}+4k\epsilon^{\prime}}.

We conclude that for all
\nu>0
, there exists a suitably small
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
, such that for
1+\epsilon^{\prime}\leq p^{\prime}\leq 1+\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}+\epsilon^{\prime}
:

(1+\tau)^{2-\nu}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau\lesssim\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq 1}\sum_{j\leq 2}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{p^{\prime}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{2-4\epsilon^{\prime}}\left[\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p^{\prime}}\rho_{+}^{1-4\epsilon^{\prime}}r^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}},1\}r^{-2}|r^{3}T^{j}(TK)G|^{2}+(1-\upzeta)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|r^{3}T^{1+j}TKG|^{2}\right]\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p^{\prime}}\rho_{+}^{1-4\epsilon^{\prime}}r^{-1+4\epsilon^{\prime}},1\}r^{-2}(|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(r^{3}T^{j}G)|^{2}+|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(r^{3}T^{j+1}G)|^{2})\\
+(1-\upzeta)\Omega^{2}r^{-2}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{2+j}(r^{3}G)|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sup_{\tau\in[0,\infty)}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{2-2\epsilon^{\prime}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p^{\prime}}r^{-2}(|r^{3}T^{j+1}G|^{2}+|r^{3}T^{j}G|^{2})\,d\sigma dr.

Step 4: general
N_{1},N_{2}

Now we consider the general
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
,
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
case. We repeat Steps 1 and 2 above but we generalize (
6.2
) as follows:

\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N-1}r^{2}|XTK\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}\lesssim\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{0}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]+r^{-2}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N-1}|r^{3}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}TG|^{2}+|r^{3}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}G|^{2}.

We apply the above inequality
N_{1}
times.
∎

Corollary 6.2
.

Let
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
,
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
q_{1}\in[0,\infty)
. Let
|q|\leq q_{1}
. Let
1+\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q_{1}^{2}}<p<2+\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q_{1}^{2}}
. For all
\nu>0
and
0<\epsilon<p-1-\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q_{1}^{2}}<1
, there exists a constant

C=C(\mathbbm{h},\nu,\epsilon,N_{1},N_{2},p,q_{1})>0
such that:

(1+\tau)^{1+\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4\tilde{q}^{2}}-p+\epsilon-\nu+2N_{1}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\sum_{m\leq N_{1}}\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq N_{1}+N_{2}}\sum_{j\leq 2N_{1}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4\tilde{q}^{2}}+\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\sup_{\tau\in[0,\infty)}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{2-\epsilon+2\max\{m-1,0\}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p}r^{-2}|r^{3}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j}(TK)^{\max\{m-1,0\}}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{2m}\Big[\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-1-\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4\tilde{q}^{2}}-\epsilon}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}r^{-1+2\epsilon},1\}r^{-2}|r^{3}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j}(TK)^{m}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\\
+\Omega^{2}(1-\upzeta)|r\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j+1}(TK)^{m}G_{\widehat{A}}|^{2}\Big]\,d\sigma drd\tau.

(6.8)

Proof.

We will prove the case
N_{1}=1
,
N_{2}=0
. The general case proceeds entirely analogously. We apply an interpolation argument. Split:
