\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma dr=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r^{-1}\rho_{+}\leq(1+\tau)^{-1}\}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[TK\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r^{-1}\rho_{+}>(1+\tau)^{-1}\}}\mathcal{E}_{p}TK\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r^{-1}\rho_{+}\leq(1+\tau)^{-1}\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}r^{2}|XTK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+(1+\tau)^{p-(1+\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4\tilde{q}^{2}}+\epsilon)}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4\tilde{q}^{2}}+\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\psi]\,d\sigma dr.

We use that by (
2.11
) and (
2.13
):

r^{2}|XTK\psi|^{2}\lesssim\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{0}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]+|rTG|^{2}+|rG|^{2}

(6.9)

to estimate:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r^{-1}\rho_{+}\leq(1+\tau)^{-1}\}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}r^{2}|XTK\psi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\lesssim(1+\tau)^{-3-\sqrt{1-4\tilde{q}^{2}}+\epsilon+\nu+p}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\sum_{j\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\sqrt{1-4\tilde{q}^{2}}-\epsilon-\nu}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
+(1+\tau)^{-2+\epsilon}\sum_{j\leq 1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{2-\epsilon}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{2-p}[|rTG|^{2}+|rG|^{2}]\,d\sigma dr.

The first integral on the RHS above can then be estimated by (
5.7
) applied to
\psi
and
T\psi
.
∎

7.
Approximate solutions

In this section, we will set
r_{+}=1
for convenience. We define the following integral operators:

\displaystyle(T^{-1}f)(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)=

\displaystyle-\int_{\tau}^{\infty}f(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\varphi)\,d\tau^{\prime},

\displaystyle(K^{-1}f)(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)=

\displaystyle-e^{-iq\tau}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{iq\tau^{\prime}}f(\tau^{\prime},r,\theta,\varphi)\,d\tau^{\prime}.

It is straightforward to check that for
q\neq 0
:

TK\left(\frac{1}{-iq}\left(K^{-1}f-T^{-1}f\right)\right)=f,

so we can express:

(TK)^{-1}(f)=\frac{1}{-iq}\left(K^{-1}f-T^{-1}f\right).

We will apply the energy decay estimates of Proposition
6.1
and Corollary
6.2
to
(TK)^{-1}(\widehat{\psi})=(TK)^{-1}(\psi-\Psi)
, where
\psi
is a solution to (
2.7
) with
G_{A}
and
\Psi
is a function for which
(g^{-1}_{M,e})^{\mu\nu}D^{\widehat{A}}_{\mu}D^{\widehat{A}}_{\nu}\Psi
decays suitably in
\tau
and
r
.

The structure of this section is as follows:

•

We define
\Psi
here and establish in §
7.2
decay estimates for
(g^{-1}_{M,e})^{\mu\nu}\>{}^{\widehat{A}}D_{\mu}^{\widehat{A}}D_{\nu}(r^{-1}\Psi)
. The function
\Psi
will depend on constants
\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]
and
\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]
, which will be determined by initial data for
\psi
, with
\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]=0
when
|Q|<M
.

•

In §
8
, we interpret
(TK)^{-1}(\widehat{\psi})
as solutions to the inhomogeneous equation:

(g^{-1}_{M,e})^{\mu\nu}\>{}^{\widehat{A}}D_{\mu}\>^{\widehat{A}}D_{\nu}(r^{-1}(TK)^{-1}\widehat{\psi})=-(TK)^{-1}((g^{-1}_{M,e})^{\mu\nu}\>{}^{\widehat{A}}D_{\mu}^{\widehat{A}}D_{\nu}(r^{-1}\Psi))

arising from initial data
((TK)^{-1}(\widehat{\psi}),T(TK)^{-1}(\widehat{\psi}))|_{\Sigma_{0}}
that we construct explicitly. In this step, we fix
\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]
and
\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]
.

7.1.
Definition of tail functions

We define the
null infinity tail function

\Psi^{\infty}:[0,\infty)_{\tau}\times[r_{+},\infty)_{r}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}\to\mathbb{C}
as follows: fix
r_{+}=1
, then

\displaystyle\Psi^{\infty}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi):=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}(\tau,r)Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\varphi),

\displaystyle\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}(\tau,r):=

\displaystyle\>\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]\frac{\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r)}{\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{\ell}(r)}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r;q),

where

\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{\ell}(r;q):=\begin{cases}{\alpha}_{+}r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}+{\alpha}_{-}r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0),\\
{\alpha}_{+}r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}\log r+{\alpha}_{-}r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0),\end{cases}

with
{\alpha}_{\pm}\in\mathbb{C}
the constants appearing in the following asymptotics as
r\to\infty
:

\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r;q)=\begin{cases}{\alpha}_{+}r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(1+O(r^{-1}))+{\alpha}_{-}r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(1+O(r^{-1}))\quad(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0),\\
{\alpha}_{+}r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}\log r(1+O(r^{-1}))+{\alpha}_{-}r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}(1+O(r^{-1}))\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0);\end{cases}

see Proposition
3.1
, and where

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r;q):=\begin{cases}\beta_{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-(n+\frac{1}{2})\beta_{\ell}+iq}\\
\times{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq-\left(n+\frac{1}{2}\right)\beta_{\ell};-\frac{\tau+1}{2r}\right)\quad\textnormal{when}\>\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)),\\
\mbox{}\\
\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\zeta^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-(n+\frac{1}{2})\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+iq}\\
\times{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq-\left(n+\frac{1}{2}\right)\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell};-\frac{\tau+1}{2r}\right)\\
\textnormal{when}\>\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty),\\
\mbox{}\\
(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tau+1)^{-ix}e^{-\eta x}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq-ix;-\frac{\tau+1}{2r}\right)\,dx\\
\textnormal{when}\>\beta_{\ell}=0,\end{cases}

with
N_{\beta_{\ell}}=\lceil(\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell})^{-1}\rceil\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, with
{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,b,c;\cdot)
denoting regularized Gauss hypergeometric functions, see for example
[
NIS
]
[§15.2] for a definition, and
\zeta,\eta\in\mathbb{C}
defined as follows:

\displaystyle\zeta:=

\displaystyle-\frac{2^{\beta_{\ell}}\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq)}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq)}\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}},

\displaystyle\eta:=

\displaystyle\>i\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\left[\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}-\log 2+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\right],

where
\gamma_{\rm Euler}
is the Euler–Mascheroni constant.

Note that
(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\cdot,\cdot;q)Y_{\ell m}
are solutions to (
2.10
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
\mathbbm{h}=0
, that are smooth with respect to
(\tau,\rho_{\infty},\theta,\varphi)
; see Appendix
A
.

In the
|Q|=M
case, we also define the
event horizon tail function
:

\displaystyle\Psi^{+}(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi;q):=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}(\tau,r)Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\varphi),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\ell m}^{+}(\tau,r):=

\displaystyle\>\frac{\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(1+(r-1)^{-1};-q)}{\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(1+(r-1)^{-1};-q)}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,1+(r-1)^{-1};q),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]\in

\displaystyle\>\mathbb{C}.

In the case
|Q|<M
, we simply set
\mathfrak{h}_{\ell m}[\psi]=0
. We define

\Psi:=\Psi^{\infty}+\Psi^{+}.

We also introduce:

G^{(1)}:=\frac{1}{iq}(K^{-1}-T^{-1})((g^{-1})^{\mu\nu}D_{\mu}D_{\nu}(r^{-1}\Psi)).

From the estimates in §
7.2
, it will follow that the above integral is in fact well-defined.

For later convenience, we introduce the following notation:

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{I}[\psi]|^{2}:=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}\\
|m|\leq\ell\end{subarray}}|\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]|^{2},

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{H}[\psi]|^{2}:=

\displaystyle\>\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}\\
|m|\leq\ell\end{subarray}}|\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]|^{2}.

7.2.
Tail functions as approximate solutions

Define

G[f]:=-(g^{-1}_{M,Q})^{\mu\nu}\>(^{\widehat{A}}D_{\mu})(^{\widehat{A}}D_{\nu})(r^{-1}f).

In this section, we show that
G[\Psi^{\infty}]
and
G[\Psi^{+}]
decay suitably fast in
r
and
\tau
and these decay rates are preserved under commutation with either
(\tau+1)T
or
(\tau+1)K
and
(r-r_{+})X
. In this sense, the tail functions
\Psi^{+}
and
\Psi^{\infty}
may each be considered approximate solutions to (
2.11
).

Proposition 7.1
.

Fix
r_{+}=1
. Let
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(n_{1},n_{2},\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}})>0
such that:

\left|r^{3}((r-1)X)^{n_{1}}((\tau+1)T)^{n_{2}}G[\Psi^{\infty}]\right|(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)\leq C((\tau+1)r^{-1}+2)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}.

(7.1)

and

\left|r^{3}((r-1)X)^{n_{1}}((\tau+1)K)^{n_{2}}G[\Psi^{+}]\right|(\tau,r,\theta,\varphi)\leq C((\tau+1)(1-r^{-1})+2)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}.

(7.2)

Proof.

In view of (
2.13
), the estimates in (
7.2
) follow immediately from the estimates (
7.1
), after replacing
q
with
-q
,
T
with
K
and
r=s_{\infty}+1
with
s_{+}+1=\frac{1}{r-1}+1
.

We will first prove (
7.1
) with
n_{1}=n_{2}=0
.

Denote
f(\tau,r)=(\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r;q))^{-1}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r)
,
\mathfrak{w}(r)=\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r;q)
and
\mathfrak{w}^{0}(r)=\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r;q)
. Then we can write
\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}=\mathfrak{w}\cdot f
,so

r^{3}(g^{-1})^{\mu\nu}D_{\mu}D_{\nu}(r^{-1}\mathfrak{w}fY_{\ell m})Y_{\ell m}^{-1}=2r^{2}\frac{d(\Omega^{2}\mathfrak{w})}{dr}Xf-2r^{2}(1-\mathbbm{h})X(\mathfrak{w}Tf)\\
+\mathfrak{w}r^{2}\left[\Omega^{2}X^{2}f-2iq(1-\mathbbm{h})r^{-1}Xf-\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T^{2}f+\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)Tf\right]\\
=\Omega^{2}\frac{\mathfrak{w}}{\mathfrak{w}^{0}}\left[2r^{2}\frac{d\mathfrak{w}^{0}}{dr}Xf-2r^{2}X(\mathfrak{w}^{0}Tf)+\mathfrak{w}^{0}r^{2}\left(X^{2}f-2iqr^{-1}Xf\right)\right]\\
+2r^{2}\mathfrak{w}^{0}\frac{d(\Omega^{2}\mathfrak{w}(\mathfrak{w}^{0})^{-1})}{dr}Xf-2r^{2}(1-\Omega^{2})\mathfrak{w}XTf-2r^{2}\mathfrak{w}^{0}X(\mathfrak{w}(\mathfrak{w}^{0})^{-1})Tf+2r^{2}\mathbbm{h}X(\mathfrak{w}Tf)\\
+2iq\mathfrak{w}r(1-\Omega^{2}-\mathbbm{h})Xf-\mathfrak{w}r^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T^{2}f+\mathfrak{w}r^{2}\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)Tf\\
=2r^{2}\mathfrak{w}^{0}\frac{d(\Omega^{2}\mathfrak{w}(\mathfrak{w}^{0})^{-1})}{dr}Xf-2r^{2}(1-\Omega^{2})\mathfrak{w}XTf-2r^{2}\mathfrak{w}^{0}X(\mathfrak{w}(\mathfrak{w}^{0})^{-1})Tf\\
+2r^{2}\mathbbm{h}X(\mathfrak{w}Tf)-2iq\mathfrak{w}r(1-\Omega^{2}-\mathbbm{h})Xf-\mathfrak{w}r^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T^{2}f+\mathfrak{w}r^{2}\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)Tf,

(7.3)

where we arrived at the final equality by using that
\mathfrak{w}^{0}=(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}
is a solution to (
2.11
) with
M=0
by Propositions
A.2
,
A.4
and
A.5
, so:

2r^{2}\frac{d\mathfrak{w}^{0}}{dr}Xf-2r^{2}X(\mathfrak{w}^{0}Tf)+\mathfrak{w}^{0}r^{2}\left(X^{2}f-2iqr^{-1}Xf\right)=0.

From Propositions
A.2
,
A.4
and
A.5
it follows moreover that:

\displaystyle r^{2}|\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\mathfrak{w}T^{2}f|\lesssim

\displaystyle\>r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}},

\displaystyle r^{2}|\mathfrak{w}\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}+2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)Tf|\lesssim

\displaystyle\>r^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}},

\displaystyle 2r^{2}\left|\mathfrak{w}^{0}\frac{d(\Omega^{2}\mathfrak{w}(\mathfrak{w}^{0})^{-1})}{dr}Xf\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\>r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+2r)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}},

\displaystyle 2r^{2}\left|(1-\Omega^{2})\mathfrak{w}XTf\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\>r^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+2r)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}},

\displaystyle 2r^{2}\left|\mathfrak{w}^{0}X(\mathfrak{w}(\mathfrak{w}^{0})^{-1})Tf\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\>r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}},

\displaystyle 2r^{2}|\mathbbm{h}X(\mathfrak{w}Tf)|\lesssim

\displaystyle\>r^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}

\displaystyle+2\left|iq\mathfrak{w}r(1-\Omega^{2}-\mathbbm{h})Xf\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\>r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}.

We therefore have that:

|r^{3}(g^{-1})^{\mu\nu}D_{\mu}D_{\nu}(r^{-1}\mathfrak{w}f)|\lesssim r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\textnormal{Re}\,\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}.

The case
n_{1}+n_{2}>0
follows from the fact that the above estimates are all preserved under commutation with
(\tau+1)T
and
rX
.
∎

Corollary 7.2
.

Let
r_{+}=1
and
s_{+}=(r-1)^{-1}
. The following constants are well-defined:

\displaystyle B_{\infty,\ell m}:=

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r;q)e^{2iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})(1-\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime}))\,dr^{\prime}}\Bigg[rT^{-1}(G_{\ell m}[\Psi^{\infty}])(0,r)

(7.4)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(2(1-\mathbbm{h})X\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}+\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}-\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}\right)(0,r)\Bigg]\,dr,

\displaystyle B_{+,\ell m}:=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(s_{+}+1;-q)e^{-2iq\int_{1}^{s_{+}}(s^{\prime}+1)^{-1}\Omega^{-2}(s^{\prime}+1)(1-\mathbbm{h}^{+}(s^{\prime}+1))\,ds^{\prime}}\Bigg[rK^{-1}(G_{\ell m}[\Psi^{+}])(0,s_{+})

(7.5)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(2(1-\mathbbm{h}^{+})\partial_{s_{+}}\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}+\mathbbm{h}^{+}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}^{+}K\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}-\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}^{+}}{ds_{+}}-2iq(s_{+}+1)^{-1}\mathbbm{h}^{+}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}^{+}\right)\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}\right)(0,s_{+})\Bigg]\,ds_{+}.

Proof.

From the decay estimates in Proposition
7.1
, it follows that
rT^{-1}(G_{\ell m}[\Psi^{\infty}])(0,r)
and
rK^{-1}(G_{\ell m}[\Psi^{+}])(0,s_{+})
and decay suitably fast in
r
and
s_{+}
for the integrals in (
7.4
) and (
7.5
) to be well-defined. Note moreover that
B_{+,\ell m}
can be obtained from
B_{\infty,\ell m}
after expressing
B_{\infty,\ell m}
as an integral with respect to the variable
r-1
and then mapping
T
to
K
,
r-1
to
s_{+}
,
q
to
-q
,
\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}
to
\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}
and
rT^{-1}(G_{\ell m}[\Psi^{\infty}])
to
rK^{-1}(G_{\ell m}[\Psi^{+}])
.
∎

Remark 7.1
.

In Corollary
10.2
, we will show that
B_{\infty,\ell m}\neq 0
. Furthermore, in the case
|Q|=M
, it then follows immediately that
B_{+,\ell m}\neq 0
if we choose
\mathbbm{h}:(0,\infty)_{s_{+}}\to[0,\infty)
so that
\mathbbm{h}^{+}:(0,\infty)_{s_{\infty}}\to[0,\infty)
satisfies
\mathbbm{h}(s_{+})=\mathbbm{h}^{+}(s_{\infty})
.

8.
Initial data for time integrals

We define the following difference function:

\widehat{\psi}=\psi-\Psi.

Then
(g^{-1})^{\mu\nu}D_{\mu}D_{\nu}(r^{-1}\widehat{\psi})=TKG^{(1)}
.

In this section, we construct the time integrals
(TK)^{-1}(\widehat{\psi})
. This construction fixes
\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]
and also
\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]
in the
|Q|=M
case.

8.1.
Fixed spherical harmonic modes

In this section, we consider the difference function
\psi-\Psi^{\infty}
and we will determine the values of
\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]
that are required to make sense of
T^{-1}(\psi-\Psi^{\infty})
.

We will first construct
T^{-1}(\psi-\Psi^{\infty})|_{\Sigma_{0}}
via the renormalized quantities:

T^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell}:=\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-1}T^{-1}(\psi-\Psi^{\infty})|_{\Sigma_{0}}.

Indeed, the requirement that
T(T^{-1}\psi_{\ell})|_{\Sigma_{0}}=(\psi_{\ell})|_{\Sigma_{0}}
and (
2.11
) holds for
T^{-1}\widehat{\psi}
result in the equation:

-rT^{-1}(G_{\ell m}[\Psi^{\infty}])(0,\cdot)=\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}X(\Omega^{2}XT^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell m})+2\Omega^{2}\frac{d\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}}{dr}XT^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell m}-2iq\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(1-\mathbbm{h})r^{-1}XT^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell m}\\
-2(1-\mathbbm{h})X\widehat{\psi}_{\ell m}-\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T\widehat{\psi}_{\ell m}+\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)\widehat{\psi}_{\ell m}.

(8.1)

Equivalently, we can express:

-\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}e^{-2iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})(1-\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime}))\,dr^{\prime}}rT^{-1}(G_{\ell m}[\Psi^{\infty}])(0,\cdot)\\
=X(\Omega^{2}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{2}e^{-2iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})(1-\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime}))\,dr^{\prime}}XT^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell m})\\
+\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}e^{-2iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})(1-\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime}))\,dr^{\prime}}\left[-2(1-\mathbbm{h})X\widehat{\psi}_{\ell m}-\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T\widehat{\psi}_{\ell m}+\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)\widehat{\psi}_{\ell m}\right].

(8.2)

We define the following constant:

Proposition 8.1
.

Fix
r_{+}=1
. Suppose that
B_{\infty,\ell m}\neq 0
and fix the constants
\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]
appearing in the definition of
\Psi^{\infty}
as follows:

\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]:=B_{\infty,\ell m}^{-1}\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r;q)e^{-2iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})(1-\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime}))\,dr^{\prime}}\\
\times\left[2(1-\mathbbm{h})X{\psi}_{\ell}+\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T{\psi}_{\ell}-\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right){\psi}_{\ell}\right](0,r)\,dr.

Then there exists a unique solution
T^{-1}(\psi_{\ell m}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m})|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in C^{\infty}([r_{+},\infty))
to (
8.1
) such that

\displaystyle\Omega^{2}XT^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell})|_{\Sigma_{0}}(r_{+},\cdot)\equiv

\displaystyle\>0,

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to\infty}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-1}(r;q)T^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell})|_{\Sigma_{0}}(r,\theta,\varphi)=

\displaystyle\>0.

We denote with

T^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell})

the solution to (
2.7
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
G=G[\Psi^{\infty}]
, arising from the initial data:

\left(T^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell})|_{\Sigma_{0}},(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell})|_{\Sigma_{0}}\right).

Then
T(T^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell}))=\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell}
.

Proof.

For the sake of notational convenience, we will drop the
m
in the subscripts
\psi_{\ell m}
. We can write (
8.2
) as follows:

X(\Omega^{2}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{2}e^{-2iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})(1-\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime}))\,dr^{\prime}}XT^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell})(0,r)=r\widetilde{G}_{\ell},

(8.3)

with

r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}:=-\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}e^{-2iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})(1-\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime}))\,dr^{\prime}}rT^{-1}(G_{\ell m}[\Psi^{\infty}])(0,\cdot)\\
+\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}e^{-2iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})(1-\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime}))\,dr^{\prime}}\left[2(1-\mathbbm{h})X\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}+\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}-\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}\right].

Since (
8.3
) is a second-order ODE, we need to impose two boundary conditions to obtain a unique solution. We take as our first boundary condition
(\Omega^{2}XT^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell})(1)=0
, which together with the boundedness of
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}
at
r=1
implies that:

XT^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell}(r)=e^{2iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})(1-\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime}))\,dr^{\prime}}\Omega^{-2}(r)\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-2}(r)\int_{1}^{r}r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}.

Since
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}
is non-vanishing, the above expression is well-defined for all
r>1
.

Note that for
r\leq 2
and
\delta>0
, we can estimate:

\displaystyle|XT^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell}(r)|^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\>\Omega^{-3-\delta}(r-1)\int_{1}^{r}\Omega^{-1+\delta}|r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}(r^{\prime})|^{2}\,dr^{\prime},

\displaystyle|T^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell}(r)|^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\>(r-1)^{-\delta}\int_{1}^{r}\Omega^{-1+\delta}|r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}(r^{\prime})|^{2}\,dr^{\prime}+|T^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell}(2)|^{2}.

The second boundary condition is
\lim_{r\to\infty}T^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell}(r)=0
, which implies that

T^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell}(r)=-\int_{r}^{\infty}e^{2iq\int_{r_{0}}^{r^{\prime}}r^{\prime\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime\prime})(1-\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime\prime}))\,dr^{\prime\prime}}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-2}(r^{\prime})\int_{1}^{r^{\prime}}r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}(r^{\prime\prime})\,dr^{\prime\prime}dr^{\prime}.

For
\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}<1
,
r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}
is integrable for suitably decaying
{\psi}_{\ell}
so we can choose
\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]
such that

\int_{1}^{\infty}r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}=0.

(8.4)

We can guarantee (
8.4
) by taking:

\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]=B_{\infty,\ell m}^{-1}\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}e^{-2iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r}r^{\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})(1-\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime}))\,dr^{\prime}}\\
\times\left[2(1-\mathbbm{h})X{\psi}_{\ell}+\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T{\psi}_{\ell}-\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right){\psi}_{\ell}\right](0,r)\,dr,

where we made use of the fact that
B_{\infty,\ell m}\neq 0
.

In that case, we obtain:

T^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell}(r)=\int_{r}^{\infty}e^{2iq\int_{r_{\sharp}}^{r^{\prime}}r^{\prime\prime-1}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime\prime})(1-\mathbbm{h}(r^{\prime\prime}))\,dr^{\prime\prime}}\Omega^{-2}(r^{\prime})\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-2}(r^{\prime})\int_{r^{\prime}}^{\infty}r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}(r^{\prime\prime})\,dr^{\prime\prime}dr^{\prime}

Hence, by applying Cauchy–Schwarz and taking
0<\delta<1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}
, we obtain in the case
\beta_{\ell}\neq 0
:

|T^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell}(r)|\lesssim\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime-1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\cdot r^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}{2}+\frac{\delta}{2}}\sqrt{\int_{r^{\prime}}^{\infty}r^{\prime\prime 2-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta}|r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}|^{2}(r^{\prime\prime})\,dr^{\prime\prime}}\\
\lesssim r^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}{2}+\frac{\delta}{2}}\sqrt{\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime\prime 2-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta}|r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}|^{2}(r^{\prime\prime})\,dr^{\prime\prime}}.

In the case
\beta_{\ell}=0
, we obtain instead:

|T^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell}(r)|\lesssim\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime-1}\cdot(\log(r^{\prime}+1))^{-2}r^{\prime-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\delta}{2}}\sqrt{\int_{r^{\prime}}^{\infty}r^{\prime\prime 2-\delta}|r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}|^{2}(r^{\prime\prime})\,dr^{\prime\prime}}\\
\lesssim r^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\delta}{2}}(\log(r+1))^{-1}\sqrt{\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime\prime 2-\delta}|r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}|^{2}(r^{\prime\prime})\,dr^{\prime\prime}}

Hence,

\displaystyle|T^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m})|_{\Sigma_{0}}|^{2}(r)\lesssim r^{\delta}\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime 2-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta}|r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}|^{2}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime},

\displaystyle|XT^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m})|_{\Sigma_{0}}|^{2}(r)\lesssim r^{-2+\delta}\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime 2-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}-\delta}|r\widetilde{G}_{\ell}|^{2}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}.

Now suppose that
\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}>1
. Then we estimate instead in the region
\{r\geq 2\}
:

|T^{-1}\widecheck{\psi}_{\ell}(r)|\lesssim\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime-1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\int_{1}^{r^{\prime}}\left|r\tilde{G}_{\ell}(r^{\prime\prime})\right|\,dr^{\prime\prime}\lesssim\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime-1+\delta-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\sqrt{\int_{1}^{r^{\prime}}r^{\prime\prime 1-\delta}\left|r\tilde{G}_{\ell}(r^{\prime\prime})\right|^{2}\,dr^{\prime\prime}}\,dr^{\prime}\\
\lesssim r^{-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\delta}\sqrt{\int_{1}^{\infty}r^{\prime 1-\delta}\left|r\tilde{G}_{\ell}(r^{\prime})\right|^{2}\,dr^{\prime}}.

Hence, we can estimate for
r\geq 2
:

\displaystyle|T^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m})|_{\Sigma_{0}}|^{2}(r)\lesssim

\displaystyle\>r^{1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\delta}\int_{1}^{\infty}r^{\prime 1-\delta}|r^{\prime}\widetilde{G}_{\ell}|^{2}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime},

\displaystyle|XT^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m})|_{\Sigma_{0}}|^{2}(r)\lesssim

\displaystyle\>r^{-1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}+\delta}\int_{1}^{\infty}r^{\prime 1-\delta}|r^{\prime}\widetilde{G}_{\ell}|^{2}(r^{\prime})\,dr^{\prime}.\qed

Corollary 8.2
.

Let
|Q|=M
. Fix
r_{+}=1
. Suppose that
B_{+,\ell m}\neq 0
and fix:

\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi]:=B_{+,\ell m}^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(1+s_{+};-q)e^{2iq\int_{r_{\sharp}+1}^{s_{+}}(s^{\prime}+1)^{-1}\Omega^{-2}(s^{\prime}+1)(1-\mathbbm{h}^{+}(s^{\prime}+1))\,ds^{\prime}}\\
\times\left[2(1-\mathbbm{h}^{+})\partial_{s_{+}}{\psi}_{\ell}+\mathbbm{h}^{+}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}^{+}K{\psi}_{\ell}-\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}^{+}}{dr}+2iq(s_{+}+1)^{-1}\mathbbm{h}^{+}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}^{+}\right){\psi}_{\ell}\right](0,s_{+})\,ds_{+}.

Then there exists a unique solution
K^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{+}_{\ell})|_{\Sigma_{0}}\in C^{\infty}([0,r_{+}^{-1})_{\rho_{\infty}})
to (
8.1
) such that

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to\infty}XK^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{+}_{\ell})|_{\Sigma_{0}}(r,\theta,\varphi)\equiv

\displaystyle\>0,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{-1}\left(1+\frac{1}{r-1};-q\right)K^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{+}_{\ell})|_{\Sigma_{0}}(r_{+},\cdot)\equiv

\displaystyle\>0.

We denote with

K^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{+}_{\ell})

the solution to (
2.7
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
G=G[\Psi^{+}]
, arising from the initial data:

\left(K^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{+}_{\ell})|_{\Sigma_{0}},(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{+}_{\ell})|_{\Sigma_{0}}-iqK^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{+}_{\ell})|_{\Sigma_{0}}\right).

Then
K(K^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell}))=\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{+}_{\ell}
.

Proof.

We use the symmetry present in (
2.14
) under the exchanges:
\rho_{\infty}\leftrightarrow\rho_{+}
,
s_{+}\leftrightarrow s_{\infty}=r-1
,
T\leftrightarrow K
,
q\leftrightarrow-q
,
\mathbbm{h}\leftrightarrow\mathbbm{h}^{+}
and
\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\leftrightarrow\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}^{+}
, so that we can simply repeat the proof of Proposition
8.1
.
∎

Write

\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}:=-\frac{1}{iq}\left(K^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{+}_{\ell})-T^{-1}(\psi_{\ell}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell})\right).

Then
TK(\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell})=\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}=\psi_{\ell}-\Psi
.

8.2.
Elliptic estimates

In this section, we will show that we can sum the functions
\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}
over
\ell
to define:

\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}(\tau,\cdot):=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}(\tau,\cdot)

as a function in an appropriate Sobolev space on
\Sigma_{\tau}
.

Then

(\widehat{\psi}^{(1)})_{\ell m}(\tau,r)=\langle\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}(\tau,r,\cdot),Y_{\ell m}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}.

8.2.1.
Elliptic estimates for large angular frequencies

The key estimates that will allow us to sum over
\ell
are elliptic-type estimates for
\psi_{\geq L_{0}}
, with
L_{0}>0
suitably large. Recall that
\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}=\psi_{\ell}
for
\ell
satisfying
\ell(\ell+1)\geq q^{2}
. We will take
r_{+}=1
in this section.

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
L_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\tau\geq 0
and let
\phi
be a solution to (
2.7
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
G_{A}\equiv 0
. Let
1<p<3
. Then for suitable large
L_{0}>0
, depending on
q
and
p
, there exists a constant
C=C(p,q,L_{0})>0
such that for all
\tau\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p-2}[\psi_{\geq L_{0}}]\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T\psi_{\geq L_{0}}]\,d\sigma dr,

(8.5)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p-2}[\psi_{\geq L_{0}}]\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[K\psi_{\geq L_{0}}]\,d\sigma dr\quad\textnormal{if $|Q|=M$}.

(8.6)

Proof.

Restrict to
\psi_{\ell}
, with
\ell\geq L_{0}
, where
L_{0}>0
will be chosen suitably large. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\psi
is smooth any compactly supported. The more general case follows from a standard density argument. For the sake of notational convenience, we drop the subscript
\geq L_{0}
in
\psi_{\geq L_{0}}
in the estimates below. Rearranging (
2.11
) then gives:

X(\Omega^{2}X\psi)+r^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi-\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}r^{-1}\psi+2iq(1-\mathbbm{h})r^{-1}X\psi+\left[-iqr^{-1}\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}+q^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}r^{-2}+iqr^{-2}(1-\mathbbm{h})\right]\psi\\
=2(1-\mathbbm{h})XT\psi-\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}T^{2}\psi-\left(\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-2iqr^{-1}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}\right)T\psi=:r\tilde{G}[T\psi].

(8.7)

We use that

rX(\Omega^{2}X\psi)+r^{-1}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi-r\frac{d\Omega^{2}}{dr}r^{-1}\psi=X(r^{2}\Omega^{2}X\phi)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi

and rearrange (
8.7
) further:

X(r^{2}\Omega^{2}X\phi)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi=-2iq(1-\mathbbm{h})rX\phi+\left[-iqr\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}-q^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}-3iq(1-\mathbbm{h})\right]\phi+r^{2}\tilde{G}[T\psi].

(8.8)

Multiplying both sides of (
8.8
) with
r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}X\phi
, taking the real parts of the products and applying the Leibniz rule, we obtain;

\frac{1}{2}r^{4-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}+2}X(|X\phi|^{2})+2(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}r^{-s}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{2}\Omega^{2})|X\phi|^{2}+\textnormal{Re}\,(r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}\overline{X\phi}\cdot\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi)\\
+r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\overline{X\phi}\cdot\left[iqr\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}+q^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}+3iq(1-\mathbbm{h})\right]\phi\right)\\
=r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\overline{X\phi}\cdot r^{2}\tilde{G}[T\psi]\right).

We can further write:

\frac{1}{2}r^{4-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}+2}X(|X\phi|^{2})+2r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{2}\Omega^{2})|X\phi|^{2}\\
=X\left(\frac{1}{2}r^{4-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}+2}|X\phi|^{2}\right)+\left[2r^{-s}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{2}\Omega^{2})-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{4-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}+2})\right]|X\phi|^{2}\\
=X\left(\frac{1}{2}r^{4}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}+2}|X\phi|^{2}\right)+\left[\left(3-\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}\right)(r-M)+\left(p^{\prime}+\frac{s}{2}\right)r\Omega^{2}\right]r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}|X\phi|^{2}.

Furthermore,

\textnormal{Re}\,(r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}\overline{X\phi}\cdot\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi)=-\ell(\ell+1)\textnormal{Re}\,(r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}\overline{X\phi}\cdot\phi)=-\frac{1}{2}\ell(\ell+1)X(r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}|\phi|^{2})\\
+\frac{1}{4}\left[p^{\prime}\frac{d}{dr}(\Omega^{2}r^{-2})-2sr^{-3}\Omega^{2}\right]\ell(\ell+1)r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}-2}|\phi|^{2}\\
=-\frac{1}{2}\ell(\ell+1)X(r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}|\phi|^{2})\\
+\frac{1}{4}\left[2p^{\prime}(r-M)+2(-s-2p^{\prime})(r-2M+r^{-1}e^{2})\right]\ell(\ell+1)r^{-s-4}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}-2}|\phi|^{2}.

Combining the above estimates and integrating over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, we therefore obtain:

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left[\left(3-\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}\right)(r-M)+\left(p^{\prime}+\frac{s}{2}\right)r\Omega^{2}\right]r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}|X\phi|^{2}\\
+\frac{1}{2}\left[(-s-p^{\prime})(r-M)+(2p^{\prime}+s)(M-r^{-1}Q^{2})\right]r^{-s-4}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma\\
=X\left[\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{1}{2}r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega^{2}r^{2}|X\phi|^{2}-|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2})\,d\sigma\right]\\
-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\overline{X\phi}\cdot\left[-iqr\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}+q^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}+3iq(1-\mathbbm{h})\right]\phi\right)\\
-r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\overline{X\phi}\cdot r^{2}\tilde{G}[T\psi]\right)\,d\sigma.

(8.9)

The terms on the very LHS are non-negative definite if
p^{\prime}>0
,
s\leq 0
and
p^{\prime}<|s|<2p^{\prime}
and
p^{\prime}<6
.
By Young’s inequality, we can estimate:

\left|r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\overline{X\phi}\cdot\left[-iqr\frac{d\mathbbm{h}}{dr}+q^{2}\mathbbm{h}\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}+3iq(1-\mathbbm{h})\right]\phi\right)\right|\leq\delta r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}(r-M)|X\phi|^{2}\\
+\frac{Cq^{2}}{4\delta}r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}(r-M)^{-1}|\phi|^{2}

Note that

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r-M)r^{-s-4}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma\\
\geq L_{0}(L_{0}+1)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{(r-M)^{2}}{(r-M)^{2}-(M^{2}-Q^{2})}(r-M)^{-1}r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}|\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma\\
\geq L_{0}(L_{0}+1)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}(r-M)^{-1}|\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma.

We apply Young’s inequality again to estimate:

\left|r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}\textnormal{Re}\,\left(\overline{X\phi}\cdot r^{2}\tilde{G}[T\psi]\right)\right|\lesssim\delta r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}(r-M)|X\phi|^{2}+\frac{1}{4\delta}r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}r^{2}(r-M)^{-1}|r\tilde{G}[T\phi]|^{2}.

Finally, we integrate (
8.9
) in
r
and observe that the boundary terms vanish by the boundary assumptions in the proposition and we take
L_{0}(L_{0}+1)\geq Cq^{2}\delta^{-1}
to obtain:

\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left[\left(3-\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}-2\delta\right)(r-M)+\left(p^{\prime}+\frac{s}{2}\right)r\Omega^{2}\right]r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}|X\phi|^{2}\\
+\frac{1}{4}\left[(-s-p^{\prime})(r-M)+(2p^{\prime}+s)(M-r^{-1}Q^{2})\right]r^{-s-4}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}-2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}}r^{2}(r-M)^{-1}|r\tilde{G}[T\phi]|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\int_{1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-s}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p^{\prime}-1}(|XT\psi|^{2}+r^{-4}|T^{2}\psi|^{2}+r^{-6}|T\psi|^{2})\,d\sigma dr.

(8.10)

Now (
8.5
) follows by applying (
8.10
) with the choices
p^{\prime}=3-p
and
s=1-p^{\prime}-p=-2
, which implies that
1<p<3
.

In the case
|Q|=M
, (
8.6
) simply follows by repeating the above argument with
T
replaced by
K
,
q
replaced by
-q
and
r
replaced by
s_{+}+1
.
∎

Corollary 8.4
.

Let
L_{0},N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
\tau\geq 0
and let
\phi
be a solution to (
2.7
) with
G\equiv 0
.

Let
1<p<3
. Then, for suitably large
L_{0}>0
depending on
q
and
p
, there exists a constant
C=C(p,q,L_{0},N)>0
such that for all
\tau\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p-2}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq L_{0}}]\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\psi_{\geq L_{0}}]\,d\sigma dr,

(8.11)

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p-2}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi_{\geq L_{0}}]\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}K\psi_{\geq L_{0}}]\,d\sigma dr\quad\textnormal{if $|Q|=M$}.

(8.12)

Proof.

We can immediately commute (
8.7
) with angular momentum operators and
T
. To conclude (
8.11
) we therefore only need to commute (
8.7
) with
(r-1)X
. It is straightforward to show that the highest order terms in (
8.7
) have the same structure after commutation with
rX
and non-negative constants can only get larger. See for example
[
Gaj23b
]
[Lemma 9.2, Corollary 9.4].
∎

Corollary 8.5
.

Let
r>1
,
\delta>0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then

\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}|_{\Sigma_{0}}(r,\cdot)=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}(r,\cdot)

is well-defined as an element of the Sobolev space
H^{N}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, provided

\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi]+\mathcal{E}_{2}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{N}\psi]\,d\sigma dr<\infty.

Proof.

We first recall that

\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}=-\frac{1}{iq}\left(K^{-1}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}-T^{-1}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}\right).

Hence, we can apply (
8.11
) and (
8.12
) with
\tau=0
,
\gamma_{1}=N
,
\gamma_{2}=\gamma_{3}=0
and
p=2
to conclude that for all
\ell\geq L
:

\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}]\,d\sigma dr\leq 2q^{-2}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}T^{-1}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}]+\mathcal{E}_{0}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}K^{-1}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}]\,d\sigma dr,

where
C>0
is uniform in
\ell
.

By the fundamental theorem of calculus in the
r
-direction and a Poincaré inequality on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, it then follows that for all
r>1
:

||\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}||^{2}_{H^{N}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\leq C\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{N}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}]+\mathcal{E}_{0}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{N}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}]\,d\sigma dr.

Convergence of the sum over
\ell
then follows by showing that the sums
\sum_{\ell=L}^{M}\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}(r,\cdot)
are Cauchy sequences in
M
, which in turn follows from the above uniform estimate and the convergence of the sum
\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{N}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}]+\mathcal{E}_{0}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{N}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}]\,d\sigma dr
, which we assumed.
∎

8.2.2.
Elliptic estimates for bounded angular frequencies

We complement the elliptic estimates from Corollary
8.4
with additional elliptic estimates for bounded angular frequencies, which follow from the construction in Proposition
8.1
.

Proposition 8.6
.

Let
N,L_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
\tau\geq 0
and let
\phi
be a solution to (
2.7
). Let
2-\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}<p<3
and
\epsilon>0
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(p,q,L_{0},\epsilon)>0
such that for all
\tau\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p-2-\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\widehat{\psi}_{\leq L_{0}-1}]\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T\widehat{\psi}_{\leq L_{0}-1}]+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p+2}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(G_{\widehat{A}})_{\leq L_{0}-1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr,

(8.13)

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p-2-\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\widehat{\psi}_{\leq L_{0}-1}]\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}K\widehat{\psi}_{\leq L_{0}-1}]+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-p+2}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(G_{\widehat{A}})_{\leq L_{0}-1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr,

(8.14)

where (
8.14
) only holds for
|Q|=M
.

Proof.

Suppose first that
N=0
. We apply the estimates in the proof of Proposition
8.1
with
T^{-1}(\psi_{\ell m}-\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m})|_{\Sigma_{0}}
replaced by
\psi|_{\Sigma_{\tau}}
and with
\delta=3-p
to obtain in the case
\beta_{\ell}\neq 0
:

\displaystyle|\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}(\tau,r)\leq

\displaystyle\>(r^{-1}\Omega)^{p-3}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T\widehat{\psi}_{\leq L-1}]+(r^{\prime-1}\Omega)^{2-p+2}|(G_{\widehat{A}})_{\leq L-1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr^{\prime},

\displaystyle|X\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}(\tau,r)\leq

\displaystyle\>\Omega^{p-6}(r-1)r^{-p}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[T\widehat{\psi}_{\leq L-1}]+(r^{\prime-1}\Omega)^{2-p+2}|(G_{\widehat{A}})_{\leq L-1}|^{2}\,d\sigma dr^{\prime}.

The condition
0<\delta<1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}
from the proof of Proposition
8.1
then implies that
2-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}<p<3
.

In the
\beta_{\ell}=0
case, we see an additional logarithmic term inside the integrand on the RHS, due to the estimate
|\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}(r)|^{2}\leq(\log(1+r))^{2}r
in this case.

The estimate (
8.13
) then follows by integrating

(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-(p^{\prime}-2)}r^{-2}|\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-(p^{\prime}-2)}\Omega^{2}|X\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}

and taking
p^{\prime}<p
to ensure convergence of the integrals. The derivation of (
8.14
) proceeds entirely analogously, with the roles of
r-1
and
(r-1)^{-1}
reversed, applying Corollary
8.2
instead of Proposition
8.1
.

The case
N=1
then follows by applying the elliptic estimates in Proposition
8.3
and using (
8.13
) and (
8.13
) with
N=0
to control the lower-order orders, instead of the assumption
\ell\geq L_{0}
. The general
N\geq 1
case can similarly be estimated inductively.
∎

9.
Energy estimates for
\widehat{\psi}

In this section, we will derive energy estimates for
\widehat{\psi}
by using that
\widehat{\psi}=TK\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}
and applying Corollary
6.2
.

In order to bound the energy norms for
\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}
on the RHS of (
6.8
) by initial energy norms of
\widehat{\psi}
, we would need to apply the elliptic estimates of Corollary
8.4
and Proposition
8.6
with
p>3
. Since these elliptic estimates are in general only valid for
2<p<3
, we cannot apply them directly.

Instead, will first multiply the initial data
(\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}|_{\Sigma_{0}},T\widehat{\psi}|_{\Sigma_{0}})
with smooth cut-off functions, so that they they are compactly supported and supported away from
r=r_{+}
. We will then apply energy estimates for the cut off data before deriving energy estimates for the original data via an interpolation argument.

In the remainder of this section, we will set
r_{+}=1
. Let
\chi_{\rm initial}(r)=1
for all
1+2(r_{\rm initial}-1)\leq r\leq\frac{1}{2(r_{\rm initial}-1)}
and
\chi_{\rm initial}(r)=0
for all
r<r_{\rm initial}
and
r>\frac{1}{r_{\rm initial}-1}
. Let
\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}
be a solution to (
2.7
) with inhomogeneity
G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}
, defined as follows.

G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}=\xi_{\tau_{*}}(TK)^{-1}G_{\widehat{A}}[\Psi],

with
\xi_{\tau_{*}}:[0,\infty)_{\tau}\to[0,1]
a smooth cut-off function such that
\xi_{\tau_{*}}(\tau)=1
for
\tau\leq\tau_{*}
and
\xi_{\tau_{*}}(\tau)=0
for
\tau\geq\tau_{*}+1
.

We consider the following corresponding initial data for
\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}
:

\chi_{\rm initial}(\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}|_{\Sigma_{0}},(T\widehat{\psi}^{(1)})|_{\Sigma_{0}}).

Then by finite speed of propagation, we have that

\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}\equiv\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}\quad\textnormal{in $D^{+}\left(\Sigma_{0}\cap\left\{1+2(r_{\rm initial}-1)\leq r\leq\frac{1}{2(r_{\rm initial}-1)}\right\}\right)\cap\{\tau\leq\tau_{*}\}$}

Given
\tau_{*}\geq 0
and
\tilde{R}>1
, we can therefore find
r_{\rm initial}(\tau_{*},\tilde{R})
such that

\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}|_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{1+\tilde{R}^{-1}\leq r\leq\tilde{R}\}}\equiv\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}|_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{1+\tilde{R}^{-1}\leq r\leq\tilde{R}\}}

for all
\tau\leq\tau_{*}
.

Furthermore, it is straightforward to show that we can choose
r_{\rm initial}(\tau_{*},\tilde{R})
, such that

(r_{\rm initial}(\tau_{*},\tilde{R})-1)^{-1}\lesssim\tilde{R}+(1+\tau_{*}).

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
1<p<2
. For all
\nu>0
and
\epsilon>0
suitably small, there exists a constant
C=C(\nu,\epsilon,N_{1},N_{2},p)>0
such that:

(1+\tau)^{3-p+2N_{1}-2\epsilon-\nu}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p-3\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\sum_{m\leq N_{1}+1}\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq N_{1}+N_{2}+1}\sum_{j\leq 2(N_{1}+1)}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{3-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr+C(|\mathfrak{I}[\psi]|^{2}+|\mathfrak{H}[\psi]|^{2})\\
=:CE_{N_{1},N_{2},\epsilon}[\psi]

(9.1)

and additionally,

(1+\tau)^{-2\epsilon}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1-5\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{-1}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\leq CE_{-1,N_{2},\epsilon}[\psi].

(9.2)

Furthermore, for
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\cup\{-1\}
,
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

\displaystyle(1+\tau)^{3+\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}+2N_{1}-8\epsilon-\nu}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{\epsilon-\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>CE_{N_{1}+1,N_{2}+1,\epsilon}[\psi],

(9.3)

\displaystyle(1+\tau)^{3+\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}+2N_{1}-8\epsilon-\nu}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{2+\epsilon-\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}}[D_{T}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\leq

\displaystyle\>CE_{N_{1}+1,N_{2}+2,\epsilon}[\psi].

(9.4)

Proof.

With the above choice of
r_{\rm initial}(\tau_{*},\tilde{R})
, we obtain the following estimate:

\sum_{m\leq N_{1}}\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq N_{1}+N_{2}}\sum_{j\leq 2N_{1}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}]\,d\sigma dr\\
\lesssim\left[\tilde{R}^{2\epsilon}+(1+\tau_{*})^{2\epsilon}\right]\sum_{m\leq N_{1}}\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq N_{1}+N_{2}}\sum_{j\leq 2N_{1}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}]\,d\sigma dr

We apply the elliptic estimates in Corollary
8.4
and Proposition
8.6
along
\Sigma_{0}
to further estimate the RHS in terms of
\widehat{\psi}
and obtain:

\sum_{m\leq N_{1}}\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq N_{1}+N_{2}}\sum_{j\leq 2N_{1}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}_{\textrm{✂}}^{(1)}]\,d\sigma dr\\
\lesssim\left[\tilde{R}^{2\epsilon}+(1+\tau_{*})^{2\epsilon}\right]\sum_{m\leq N_{1}}\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq N_{1}+N_{2}}\sum_{j\leq 2N_{1}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{3-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\left[\tilde{R}^{2\epsilon}+(1+\tau_{*})^{2\epsilon}\right]\sum_{m\leq N_{1}}\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq N_{1}+N_{2}}\sum_{j\leq 2N_{1}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{-1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}|r\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}(TK)^{-1}G_{\widehat{A}}[\psi]|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

First, we we apply (
5.7
) to obtain for
p=1+\epsilon
:

\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{*}}\cap\{1+\tilde{R}^{-1}\leq r\leq\tilde{R}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{-1}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\leq\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{*}}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N}\sum_{n\leq N}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-1-\epsilon}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}r^{-1+2\epsilon},1\}r^{-2}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(r^{3}G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}})|^{2}+\Omega^{2}r^{-2}(1-\upzeta)|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T(r^{3}G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}})|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
\leq\left[\tilde{R}^{2\epsilon}+(1+\tau_{*})^{2\epsilon}\right]\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq N_{1}+N_{2}}\sum_{j\leq 2N_{1}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{3-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\sum_{m\leq N_{1}}\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq N_{1}+N_{2}}\sum_{j\leq 2N_{1}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{-1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}|r\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}(TK)^{-1}G_{\widehat{A}}[\psi]|^{2}\,d\sigma dr\\
+C\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-1-\epsilon}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}r^{-1+2\epsilon},1\}r^{-2}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(r^{3}G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}})|^{2}+\Omega^{2}r^{-2}(1-\upzeta)|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T(r^{3}G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}})|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau.

Note first of all that all the terms on the RHS above involving
G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}
are finite, by the estimates in Proposition
7.1
and can be estimated by
|\mathfrak{I}[\psi]|^{2}+|\mathfrak{H}[\psi]|^{2}
, using the definition of
\Psi
appearing in
\widehat{\psi}=\psi-\Psi
. By including a factor
(r^{-1}\Omega)^{4\epsilon}
into the left hand side, replacing
\tilde{R}
with a dyadic sequence
\tilde{R}_{j}
and summing over
j
, we obtain:

\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{*}}}\mathcal{E}_{1-5\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{-1}\psi]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C(1+\tau_{*})^{2\epsilon}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\left[\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{3-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr+|\mathfrak{I}[\psi]|^{2}+|\mathfrak{H}[\psi]|^{2}\right].

Since
\tau_{*}
can be taken arbitrarily large, we conclude (
9.2
).

Now suppose that
N_{1}\geq 1
. Now we apply Corollary
6.2
with
q_{1}>\frac{1}{2}
to
\psi^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}
to obtain for
1<p<2
and
0<\epsilon<p-1
:

(1+\tau)^{1-p+2N_{1}-\nu}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\psi^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\sum_{m\leq N_{1}}\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq N_{1}+N_{2}}\sum_{j\leq 2N_{1}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}\psi^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\sup_{\tau\in[0,\infty)}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{2-\epsilon+2\max\{m-1,0\}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p}r^{-2}|r^{3}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j}(TK)^{\max\{m-1,0\}}G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{2m}\Big[\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-1-\epsilon}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}r^{-1+2\epsilon},1\}r^{-2}|r^{3}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j}(TK)^{m}G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}|^{2}\\
+(1-\upzeta)\Omega^{2}|r\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j+1}(TK)^{m}G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}|^{2}\Big]\,d\sigma drd\tau.

and hence:

(1+\tau_{*})^{1-p+2N_{1}-\nu}\left[\tilde{R}^{2\epsilon}+(1+\tau_{*})^{2\epsilon}\right]^{-1}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau_{*}}\cap\{1+\tilde{R}^{-1}\leq r\leq\tilde{R}\}}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}-1}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\\
\lesssim\sum_{m\leq N_{1}}\sum_{|\gamma|+m\leq N_{1}+N_{2}}\sum_{j\leq 2N_{1}}\int_{\Sigma_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{3-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{m}T^{j}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\left[\tilde{R}^{2\epsilon}+(1+\tau_{*})^{2\epsilon}\right]^{-1}\Bigg[\sup_{\tau\in[0,\infty)}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{2-\epsilon+2\max\{m-1,0\}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{2-p}r^{-2}|r^{3}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j}(TK)^{\max\{m-1,0\}}G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau\\
+\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(1+\tau)^{2m}\Big[\max\{(r^{-1}\Omega)^{-1-\epsilon}\rho_{+}^{1-2\epsilon}r^{-1+2\epsilon},1\}r^{-2}|r^{3}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j}(TK)^{m}G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}|^{2}\\
+(1-\upzeta)\Omega^{2}|r\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T^{j+1}(TK)^{m}G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}|^{2}\Big]\,d\sigma drd\tau\Bigg].

We can remove the factor
(\Omega^{-1}r)^{2\epsilon}
on the LHS at the expanse of introducing a factor
(\Omega r^{-1})^{4\epsilon}
inside the integral by applying the above energy estimate in dyadic
r
-intervals
[\tilde{R}_{j},\tilde{R}_{j+1}]\cup[1+\tilde{R}_{j+1}^{-1},1+\tilde{R}_{j}^{-1}]
and then summing over
j
.

All the terms on the RHS above involving
G^{(1)}_{\textrm{✂}}
are finite, by the estimates in Proposition
7.1
and can be estimated by
|\mathfrak{I}[\psi]|^{2}+|\mathfrak{H}[\psi]|^{2}
, using the definition of
\Psi
appearing in
\widehat{\psi}=\psi-\Psi
. Furthermore, the constants in the estimates do not depend on
\tau_{*}
so we can take
\tau_{*}
arbitrarily large and we conclude (
9.1
).

To derive (
9.3
), we apply the elliptic estimates in Corollary
8.4
and Proposition
8.6
along
\Sigma_{\tau}
with
p=2-\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}+\epsilon
to obtain for
\tau\leq\tau_{*}
:

\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{\epsilon-\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\lesssim\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2-\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}+\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}-\epsilon}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(rG)|^{2}\,d\sigma dr.

Let
N_{1}\geq 0
. We then split the integral over
\Sigma_{\tau}
into a region where
\Omega r^{-1}\leq(1+\tau)^{-1}
and
\Omega r^{-1}\geq(1+\tau)^{-1}
to obtain:

\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2-\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}+\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\\
\lesssim(1+\tau)^{-1-\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}+1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\widehat{\psi}]+\mathcal{E}_{1+\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}T(TK)^{N_{1}}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\\
+(1+\tau)^{-1-\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}+1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}(\Omega r^{-1})^{1-\epsilon}(|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}(rG)|^{2}+|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}(rG)|^{2})\,d\sigma dr\\
+(1+\tau)^{1-\textnormal{Re}\,\sqrt{1-4q^{2}}}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{1-\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}+1}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr.

Then we apply (
9.1
) with
N_{1}
replaced by
N_{1}+1
and separately with
N_{2}
replaced by
N_{2}+1
to conclude (
9.3
). The
N_{1}=-1
case proceeds analogously.

Finally, to obtain (
9.4
), we note that

\displaystyle|D_{T}X\psi|\lesssim

\displaystyle\>|KX\psi|+(r-1)|X\psi|\quad r\leq R,

\displaystyle|D_{T}X\psi|\lesssim

\displaystyle\>|TX\psi|+r^{-1}|X\psi|\quad r>R.

Hence, it follows from (
2.12
) and (
2.13
) that

\mathcal{E}_{p}[D_{T}\psi]\lesssim\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\mathcal{E}_{p-2}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi].

We then apply (
9.3
) with
N_{2}
replaced by
N_{2}+1
to obtain the desired result.
∎

9.1.
Proof of Theorem
4.2

First, note that

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}((1+\tau)TK)^{N_{1}}\Psi^{\infty}]\,d\sigma dr\\
\sim\sum_{\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z},|m|\leq\ell}|\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]|^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}((1+\tau)TK)^{N_{1}}((\Psi_{0}^{\infty})_{\ell m}Y_{\ell m})]\,d\sigma dr.

We can therefore apply Proposition
A.7
to obtain for
0\leq p\leq 2
:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p-3\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}((1+\tau)TK)^{N_{1}}\Psi^{\infty}]\,d\sigma dr\leq\begin{cases}C|\mathfrak{I}[\psi]|^{2}(1+\tau)^{p-2-2\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0),\\
C|\mathfrak{I}[\psi]|^{2}\frac{(1+\tau)^{p-2}}{\log^{2}(1+\tau)}\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0)\end{cases}

(9.5)

When
|Q|=M
, we similarly obtain:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{p-3\epsilon}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}((1+\tau)TK)^{N_{1}}\Psi^{+}]\,d\sigma dr\leq\begin{cases}C|\mathfrak{H}[\psi]|^{2}(1+\tau)^{p-2-2\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0),\\
C|\mathfrak{H}[\psi]|^{2}\frac{(1+\tau)^{p-2}}{\log^{2}(1+\tau)}\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0).\end{cases}

(9.6)

By Young’s inequality, we have for any
\delta>0
that:

(1-(1+\tau)^{-\delta})\mathcal{E}_{p}[\Psi]-((1+\tau)^{\delta}-1)\mathcal{E}_{p}[\widehat{\psi}]\leq\mathcal{E}_{p-3\epsilon}[\psi]\leq(1+(1+\tau)^{-\delta})\mathcal{E}_{p}[\Psi]+(1+(1+\tau)^{\delta})\mathcal{E}_{p}[\widehat{\psi}].

Hence, we conclude the estimates for
1<p<2
by combining the above with (
9.1
) and for
0\leq p<1-\epsilon
by applying additionally (
9.3
) and then performing a standard interpolation argument.

To obtain the
p=2
estimate, we apply (
2.11
) applied to
(TK)^{-1}\widehat{\psi}
, to estimate:

\mathcal{E}_{2}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\widehat{\psi}]\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{1}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\widehat{\psi}]+\sum_{|\gamma|\leq 2}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}-1}\widehat{\psi}]+r^{-2}|\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}(r^{3}G^{(1)})|^{2}.

Hence, by interpolating between (
9.2
) and (
9.3
), we obtain:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}((1+\tau)TK)^{N_{1}}\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\leq CE_{N_{1},N_{2}+2,\epsilon}[\psi](1+\tau)^{-1+8\epsilon+\nu}.

(9.7)

We conclude the proof by combining the above with (
9.5
) and (
9.6
).

10.
Late-time tails

In this section, we prove the main result of the present paper, which can be interpret as a determination of the precise late-time asymptotics of solutions
\phi
to (
2.7
) with
G_{A}=0
.

Proposition 10.1
.

Let
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\cup\{-1\}
,
N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Let
l\in\{0,1\}
. For arbitrarily small
\nu>0
and
\epsilon>0
, there exists a constant
C=C(\nu,\epsilon,N_{1},N_{2},p)>0
such that:

\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}||D_{T}^{l}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}(\psi-\Psi)||_{L^{\infty}}\\
\leq C\sum_{k\leq 2}\sqrt{E_{N_{1}+1,N_{2}+1+l,\epsilon}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\psi]}(\tau+1)^{-1+\frac{1}{2}\nu+4\epsilon-N_{1}-\frac{l}{2}}(r^{-1}\Omega\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}.

(10.1)

If
|Q|<M
, we moreover have for all
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}||X^{l}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}(\psi-\Psi^{\infty})||_{L^{\infty}}\\
\leq C\kappa_{+}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{k\leq 2}\sqrt{E_{N_{1}+1,N_{2}+l+1,\epsilon}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\psi]}(\tau+1)^{-1+\frac{1}{2}\nu+4\epsilon-N_{1}}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}(r^{-1}\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}\\
+Ce^{-c\kappa_{+}\tau}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N_{2}}\sqrt{\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}X^{l}\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}\psi]\,d\sigma dr}.

(10.2)

Proof.

We will derive
L^{\infty}
-estimates for
\widehat{\psi}
by using the energy estimates of Proposition
9.1
.

Consider first the region
\{r\geq r_{H}\}
with
r_{H}>r_{+}
. Then we can estimate via the fundamental theorem of calculus:

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-1}|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\cdot),d\sigma\lesssim\int_{\Sigma\cap\{r\geq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr.

Furthermore, for all
r\geq r_{H}
, we have:

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\cdot),d\sigma\lesssim\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{1+\delta}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr+\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r_{H},\cdot),d\sigma.

From (
9.1
), it then follows that for
r\geq r_{+}
and arbitrarily small
\nu
:

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\cdot)\lesssim C(1+\tau)^{-2+\delta+5\epsilon+\nu}E_{0,0,\epsilon}[\psi].

We moreover that by the fundamental theorem of calculus:

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}+1}|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\cdot),d\sigma\leq\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{\epsilon-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr.

We then apply (
9.3
) to conclude that:

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r^{-1}\Omega)^{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}+1}|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\cdot)\lesssim C(1+\tau)^{-3-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}+8\epsilon+\nu}E_{1,1,\epsilon}[\psi].

(10.3)

To obtain an estimate for
D_{T}\psi
, we instead apply (
9.4
).
We combine the above estimates and apply a standard Sobolev inequality on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
to conclude (
10.1
) with
N_{1}=N_{2}=0
. The general
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
cases follows entirely analogously.

In the
|Q|<M
case, we can improve the above estimates by removing the factor
\Omega
in (
10.3
) and by making use of the red-shift effect.

From a standard red-shift energy estimate in
\{r\leq r_{H}\}
with
r_{H}-r_{+}>0
suitably small, see for example
[
DR13
]
[§7] for a general analysis and
[
Gaj23b
]
[Proposition 6.7], it follows that there exists a numerical constant
c>0
, such that:

c\kappa_{+}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\\
\leq C\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{H}+\frac{1}{2}(r_{H}-r_{+})\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}+C\int_{\tau}^{\infty}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}+\frac{1}{2}(r_{H}-r_{+})\}}|G_{\widehat{A}}[\widehat{\psi}]|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}.

Let
f(\tau):=\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr
, then this implies that:

\frac{d}{d\tau}\left[e^{c\kappa_{+}\tau}f(\tau)\right]\leq Ce^{c\kappa_{+}\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{H}+\frac{1}{2}(r_{H}-r_{+})\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}\\
+Ce^{c\kappa_{+}\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}+\frac{1}{2}(r_{H}-r_{+})\}}|G_{\widehat{A}}[[\Psi]|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}

Hence,

f(\tau)\leq Ce^{-c\kappa_{+}\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr+C\kappa_{+}^{-1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}\cap\{r_{H}\leq r\leq r_{H}+\frac{1}{2}(r_{H}-r_{+})\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}\\
+C\kappa_{+}^{-1}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}+\frac{1}{2}(r_{H}-r_{+})\}}|G_{\widehat{A}}[[\Psi]|^{2}\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}.

By (
9.3
), we therefore have that:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr\leq C\kappa_{+}^{-1}(1+\tau)^{-3-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}+\epsilon+\nu}E_{1,1,\epsilon}[\psi]+Ce^{-c\kappa_{+}\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr.

Then we can apply the fundamental theorem of calculus in
\{r\leq r_{H}\}
to conclude that:

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}-1+\epsilon}|\widehat{\psi}|^{2}(\tau,r,\cdot)\lesssim C\kappa_{+}^{-1}(1+\tau)^{-3-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}+\epsilon+\nu}E_{1,1,\epsilon}[\psi]+Ce^{-c\kappa_{+}\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{0}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\widehat{\psi}]\,d\sigma dr.

The above improvement applies also after commuting the above estimates with
\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}(TK)^{N_{1}}
as well as
X^{l}
and we conclude (
10.2
) after applying a standard Sobolev inequality on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.
∎

Corollary 10.2
.

B_{\infty,\ell m}\neq 0
and
B_{+,\ell m}\neq 0
for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}
.

Proof.

Note first of all that
K^{-1}\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}
is well-defined. Suppose that
B_{\infty,\ell m}=0
. Then, in the case of zero initial data for
\psi_{\ell m}
, we have by Proposition
8.1
that
T^{-1}\Psi_{\infty}
is also well-defined. Hence,
\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(1)}=iq\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}(T^{-1}\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}-K^{-1}\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m})Y_{\ell m}
is well-defined. Since
TK\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(1)}=\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}Y_{\ell m}
, the decay estimates in (
10.1
) imply decay for
\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}
and hence also
(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}
that is faster than what follows from Proposition
A.6
, which is a contradiction. We must therefore have
B_{\infty,\ell m}\neq 0
.

In the
|Q|=M
case, an analogous contradiction argument with
B_{+,\ell m}
taking the role of
B_{\infty,\ell m}
and
\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}
replacing
\Psi^{\infty}_{\ell m}
leads to the statement:
B_{+,\ell m}\neq 0
.
∎

10.1.
Proof of Theorem
4.1

Theorem
4.1
(i) follows immediately from Proposition
10.1
and Theorem
4.1
(ii) follows from Proposition
A.6
.

11.
Instabilities and energy concentration

In this section, we establish lower bound estimates for
r^{2}X\psi
and the energies
\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi]
, following from the leading-order behaviour of
r^{2}X\Psi
.

11.1.
Proof of Theorem
4.3

We first consider (i). We apply (
9.7
) in combination with Proposition
A.7
(ii) to conclude the desired statements in the region
r\geq r_{I}
. The region
r\leq r_{H}
, then follows immediately after transforming
r-1\mapsto(r-1)^{-1}
and
q\mapsto-q
.

Now consider (ii). Again, without loss of generality, we can consider
(\tau,\infty)
. Let
k=0
. Then we apply (
2.12
) at
\rho_{\infty}=0
with
\mathbbm{h}\equiv 0
when
r\geq r_{I}
to obtain:

-2(r^{2}X\widehat{\psi})(\tau,\infty,\cdot)=\int_{0}^{\tau}r^{3}(G_{\widehat{A}})(\tau^{\prime},\infty)+(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}+iq)\widehat{\psi}(\tau^{\prime},\infty,\cdot)\,d\tau^{\prime}

By (
10.1
) and (
7.1
), we therefore have that:

|(r^{2}X\widehat{\psi})(\tau,\infty)|\leq\sqrt{E_{1,1,\epsilon}[\psi]}(\tau+1)^{\frac{1}{2}\nu+4\epsilon}.

(11.1)

Note that

r^{2}X\psi_{\ell m}=r^{2}X\Psi_{\ell m}+r^{2}X\widehat{\psi}_{\ell m}=\mathfrak{I}_{\ell m}[\psi]r^{2}X(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}+\ldots,

where the terms in
\ldots
can be bounded by
(\tau+1)^{\frac{1}{2}\nu+4\epsilon}
by Proposition
10.1
.

Taking the
\limsup
and applying Proposition
A.7
(i) then results in the
k=0
estimates in (ii).

To obtain the
k\geq 1
case, we consider
\partial_{\rho_{\infty}}^{k}r^{3}(G_{\widehat{A}})
and we establish estimates for
\partial_{\rho_{\infty}}^{k+1}\widehat{\psi}
inducively. Then we apply Proposition
A.7
(i).

11.2.
Transient instabilities

The estimates in the present paper are uniform in
\kappa_{+}
, which allows us to obtain lower bounds on the size of
X
-derivatives along the event horizon, which may be interpreted as
transient instabilities
.

Proposition 11.1
.

Let
\psi
be a solution to (
2.7
) with
G_{A}=0
and
A=-\frac{Q}{r}d\tau
and
\kappa_{+}>0
. Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Fix the initial data for
\psi
independently of
\kappa_{+}
. Let
\psi_{M}
be a solution arising from the same data, but with
\kappa_{+}=0
. Then, there exists suitably small
0<\delta_{1}<\delta_{2}
, depending only on the initial data for
\psi
and on
k
, such that for
r_{+}\kappa_{+}\leq\delta_{1}
and
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{\delta_{1}\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\tau\leq\delta_{2}\kappa_{+}^{-1}}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-k+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}|X^{k+1}\psi_{\ell m}|(\tau,r_{+})\geq

\displaystyle\>\frac{|\mathfrak{c}_{k}|}{2}|\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi_{M}]|\quad

\displaystyle(\beta_{0}\neq 0),

\displaystyle\sup_{\delta_{1}\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\tau\leq\delta_{2}\kappa_{+}^{-1}}\log(1+\tau)\tau^{-\frac{1}{2}-k}|X^{k+1}\psi_{\ell m}|(\tau,r_{+})\geq

\displaystyle\>\frac{|\mathfrak{c}_{k}|}{2}|\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi_{M}]|\quad

\displaystyle(\beta_{0}=0).

Proof.

Fix
Q
and let
\psi^{M}
denote a solution to (
2.7
) with
G_{A}=0
and
M=|Q|
, such that
(\psi^{M}|_{\Sigma_{0}},\partial_{\tau}\psi^{M}|_{\Sigma_{0}})=(\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}},\partial_{\tau}\psi|_{\Sigma_{0}})
. Then

(g^{-1}_{M,M})^{\mu\nu}(^{\widehat{A}}D)_{\mu}(^{\widehat{A}}D)_{\nu}(r^{-1}(\psi-\psi^{M}))=(r_{-}-r_{+})X((r-r_{+})r^{-2}X\psi)+2(Q^{2}-M^{2})r^{-4}\psi=:F_{M,Q}.

Then we can estimate:
|F_{M,Q}|^{2}\leq\kappa_{+}^{2}r^{-6}\sum_{|\gamma|=1}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]
, so by standard local energy estimates, we obtain:

\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[0,\tau]}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi-\psi^{M}]\,d\sigma dr\leq C\kappa_{+}\sum_{|\gamma|=1}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi-\psi^{M}]}\sqrt{\mathcal{E}_{2}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]}\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}\\
\leq C\kappa_{+}\sqrt{\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi-\psi^{M}]\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}}\sqrt{\sum_{|\gamma|=1}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]\,d\sigma drd\tau^{\prime}}\\
\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[0,\tau]}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi-\psi^{M}]\,d\sigma dr+\frac{C^{2}}{2}(\kappa_{+}\tau)^{2}\sum_{|\gamma|=1}\sup_{\tau^{\prime}\in[0,\tau]}\int_{\Sigma_{\tau^{\prime}}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\mathbf{Z}^{\gamma}\psi]\,d\sigma dr.

By applying Theorem
4.2
, we therefore have that for
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small:

\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi-\psi^{M}]\,d\sigma dr\leq C(\kappa_{+}\tau)^{2}E_{0,3,\epsilon}[\psi](1+\tau)^{-2\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}.

We now apply the fundamental theorem of calculus in the
r
-direction together with Theorem
4.2
to estimate:

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\psi-\psi_{M}|^{2}(\tau,r_{+},\cdot)\,d\sigma\leq C\sqrt{\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{H}\}}\mathcal{E}_{2}[\psi-\psi_{M}]\,d\sigma dr}\sqrt{\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq r_{I}\}}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi-\psi_{M}]\,d\sigma dr}\\
\leq C(\kappa_{+}\tau)E_{1,3,\epsilon}[\psi](1+\tau)^{-1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}.

In particular, we obtain:

|(\psi_{\ell m}-(\psi_{M})_{\ell m})(\tau,r_{+})|\\
\leq C(\kappa_{+}\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{2}E_{1,3,\epsilon}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\psi]}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}(1+\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0})}.

Now let
k=0
. From (
11.1
), it follows in particular that:

|(r^{2}X((\psi_{M})_{\ell m}-\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}))(\tau,r_{+})|\leq C\sqrt{E_{1,1,\epsilon}[\psi]}(\tau+1)^{\frac{1}{2}\nu+4\epsilon}.

We apply (
2.13
) with
\widetilde{\mathbbm{h}}=0
in
r\leq r_{H}
to obtain:

2T(r^{2}X(\psi-\psi_{M}))(\tau,r_{+},\cdot)=\kappa_{+}r^{2}X\psi(\tau,r_{+})+(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-iq)(\psi-\psi_{M}).

We can rewrite the above as follows:

2T(e^{\kappa_{+}\tau}r^{2}X(\psi-\psi_{M}))(\tau,r_{+},\cdot)=\kappa_{+}e^{\kappa_{+}\tau}r^{2}X\psi_{M}(\tau,r_{+})+e^{\kappa_{+}\tau}(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-iq)(\psi-\psi_{M}).

Integrating the above therefore gives for
\kappa_{+}\tau<1
:

2|r^{2}X(\psi-\psi_{M})_{\ell m}|(\tau,r_{+},\cdot)\leq C(\kappa_{+}\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{E_{1,3,\epsilon}[\psi]}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0})}

and hence, we obtain:

2|r^{2}X(\psi-\Psi^{+})_{\ell m}|(\tau,r_{+},\cdot)\leq C(\kappa_{+}\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{E_{1,3,\epsilon}[\psi]}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0})}+C\sqrt{E_{1,1,\epsilon}[\psi]}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}\nu+4\epsilon}

Now, let
\delta_{2}>0
be suitably small, so that for
r_{+}\delta_{2}^{-1}\leq\tau\leq\delta_{2}\kappa_{+}^{-1}
:

(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}{2}}|r^{2}X(\psi-\Psi^{+})_{\ell m}|(\tau,r_{+})\leq\frac{|\mathfrak{c}_{0}|}{4}|\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi_{M}]|.

If
\beta_{0}\in[0,\infty)
, we have that

(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{0}}{2}}(1+\delta_{\beta_{0}0}\log(1+\tau))|r^{2}X\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}|(\tau,r_{+})\geq|\mathfrak{c}_{0}||\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi_{M}]|,

so the stated estimates follow immediately.

Suppose that
\beta_{0}\in i(0,\infty)
. Note that for
\sigma=\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}\log(r_{+}+\tau)
, we have by the mean value theorem that for
\delta_{1}<\delta_{0}
:

\sup_{\delta_{1}\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\tau\leq\delta_{2}\kappa_{+}^{-1}}(r_{+}+\tau)^{-1}|r^{2}X\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}|^{2}(\tau,r_{+})\\
\geq\frac{1}{\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}\frac{1}{\log(\frac{r_{+}+\delta_{2}\kappa_{+}^{-1}}{r_{+}+\delta_{1}\kappa_{+}^{-1}})}\int_{\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}\log(r_{+}+\delta_{1}\kappa_{+}^{-1})}^{\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}\log(r_{+}+\delta_{2}\kappa_{+}^{-1})}(r_{+}+\tau(\sigma))^{-1}|r^{2}X\Psi^{+}_{\ell m}|^{2}(\tau(\sigma),r_{+})\,d\sigma\\
\geq\mathfrak{c}_{0}^{2}|\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi_{M}]|^{2}-C|\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi_{M}]|^{2}\frac{1}{\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}\frac{1}{\log(1+\frac{\frac{\delta_{2}}{\delta_{1}}-1}{1+\delta_{1}^{-1}\kappa_{+}r_{+}})}\geq\frac{9}{16}\mathfrak{c}_{0}^{2}|\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi_{M}]|^{2},

where we applied (
A.34
) to arrive at the second inequality above and we assumed that
\frac{\delta_{2}}{\delta_{1}}
is suitably large and
\kappa_{+}\delta_{1}^{-1}\leq r_{+}^{-1}
to arrive at the last inequality.

We conclude that there exist
0<\delta_{1}<\delta_{2}
suitably small with
\kappa_{+}\leq\delta_{1}r_{+}^{-1}
, such that:

\sup_{\delta_{1}\kappa_{+}^{-1}\leq\tau\leq\delta_{2}\kappa_{+}^{-1}}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|r^{2}X\psi_{\ell m}|(\tau,r_{+})\geq\frac{|\mathfrak{c}_{0}|}{2}|\mathfrak{H}_{\ell m}[\psi_{M}]|.

To obtain the the desired estimates for
k^{\prime}\geq 1
, we proceed by induction: we commute (
2.13
) with
\partial_{\rho_{+}}^{k^{\prime}}
and then integrate in
\tau
, applying the estimates for
k\leq k^{\prime}-1
.
∎

Appendix A
Global tail functions on Minkowski

In this section, we will construct solutions to (
2.10
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
\mathbbm{h}=0
, that will play an important role in the late-time asymptotics when
M\neq 0
.

It will be useful to work in
(\tau,\varrho,\theta,\varphi)
coordinates, where

\varrho=\frac{\tau+1}{2r}.

Note that
\varrho
is preserved by the conformal Killing vector field
S+T
, with
S=u\partial_{u}+r\partial_{r}
and
T=\partial_{u}
in
(u,r,\theta,\varphi)
coordinates.

Denote
\mathfrak{X}:=\partial_{\varrho}
and
\mathfrak{T}:=\partial_{\tau}
with respect to
(\tau,\varrho,\theta,\varphi)
coordinates. Then we can express:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}:=

\displaystyle-\frac{\tau+1}{2\varrho^{2}}X,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}:=

\displaystyle\>T+\frac{1}{2\varrho}X,

or equivalently,

\displaystyle X=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\varrho^{2}}{\tau+1}\mathfrak{X},

\displaystyle T=

\displaystyle\>\mathfrak{T}+\frac{\varrho}{\tau+1}\mathfrak{X}.

We will be considering solutions to (
2.10
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
\mathbbm{h}=0
of the form:

(\tau+1)^{-s}F(\varrho)Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\varphi),

where
F
is a function to be determined.

Note that (
2.10
) commutes with the vector field
S+T
and the solutions of the above form are eigenfunctions of the operator
S+T
:

(S+T)((\tau+1)^{-s}F(\varrho)Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\varphi))=-s\cdot(\tau+1)^{-s}F(\varrho)Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\varphi).

Lemma A.1
.

(i)

Let
s\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{Z}
. The function
(\tau+1)^{s}F(\varrho)Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\varphi)
is a solution to (
2.10
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
\mathbbm{h}=0
that is smooth in
\varrho
at
\varrho=0
if and only if

F(\varrho)=B\cdot{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,b,c;-\varrho),

with
{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,b,c;\cdot)
the regularized Gauss hypergeometric function,
B\in\mathbb{C}
and

\displaystyle a=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}\pm\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,

\displaystyle b=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}\mp\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,

\displaystyle c=

\displaystyle\>s+1.

In particular, we obtain the following simplifications for particular choices of
s
: let
a=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq
, then

\displaystyle(\tau+1)^{s}F(\varrho)=

\displaystyle\>B(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}(1+\varrho)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}-iq}\quad(s=b-1),

\displaystyle(\tau+1)^{s}F(\varrho)=

\displaystyle\>B(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}(1+\varrho)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}-iq}\quad(s=a-1).

(ii)

Let
\beta_{\ell}=0
and define

H(\tau,\varrho):=(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}(1+\varrho)^{-\frac{1}{2}-iq}(\log(\varrho+1)+\log(\tau+1)).

Then
H(\tau,\varrho)Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\varphi)
is a solution to (
2.10
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
\mathbbm{h}=0
that is smooth in
\varrho
at
\varrho=0
.

Proof.

Let
\psi
be a solution to (
2.10
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
\mathbbm{h}=0
and with
\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\psi=-\ell(\ell+1)\psi
. Then:

0=X^{2}\psi-2(T+iqr^{-1})X\psi+(iq-\ell(\ell+))r^{-2}\psi\\
=4\varrho^{3}(\varrho+1)(\tau+1)^{-2}\mathfrak{X}^{2}\psi+4\varrho^{2}(\tau+1)^{-1}\mathfrak{T}\mathfrak{X}\psi+4\varrho^{2}(\tau+1)^{-2}(2(1+iq)\varrho+1)\mathfrak{X}\psi+(iq-\ell(\ell+1))4\varrho^{2}(\tau+1)^{-2}\psi.

Equivalently,

0=\varrho(\varrho+1)\mathfrak{X}^{2}\psi+(\tau+1)\mathfrak{T}\mathfrak{X}\psi+(2(1+iq)\varrho+1)\mathfrak{X}\psi+(iq-\ell(\ell+1))\psi.

(A.1)

Now let
\psi=(\tau+1)^{s}F(\varrho)
. Then
F
satisfies the following ODE:

\varrho(\varrho+1)\frac{d^{2}F}{d\varrho^{2}}+((2+2iq)\varrho+1+s)\frac{dF}{d\varrho}+(iq-\ell(\ell+1))F=0.

By taking
z=-\varrho
we obtain a hypergeometric equation:

z(1-z)\frac{d^{2}F}{dz^{2}}+(-(2+2iq)z+1+s)\frac{dF}{dz}-(iq-\ell(\ell+1))F=0.

The corresponding solutions that are smooth at
z=0
for non-integer
c
are multiples of regularized Gauss hypergeometric functions
{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,b,c;-\varrho)
with

\displaystyle a+b+1=

\displaystyle\>2+2iq,

\displaystyle ab=

\displaystyle\>iq-\ell(\ell+1),

\displaystyle c=

\displaystyle\>s+1,

see for example
[
NIS
]
[§15.10]. We can easily solve for
a
and
b
to obtain the expressions in the statement of the theorem.

The simplifications in the cases
s=a-1
and
s=b-1
follow from
[
NIS
]
[§15.4].

Finally, it can easily be verified that
H(\tau,\varrho)
solves (
A.1
). Alternatively, we can take the
s
-derivative of
(\tau+1)^{s}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,a,s+1;-\varrho)
evaluate at
s=a-1
and add a constant multiple of
(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}(1+\varrho)^{-\frac{1}{2}-iq}
to obtain
H(\tau,\varrho)
. Indeed,
\frac{d}{ds}((\tau+1)^{s}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,a,s+1;-\varrho))
must be a solution since the equation (
A.1
) does not depend on
s
, the corresponding difference quotient is a solution, and
{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,a,a;-\varrho)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(a)}(1+\varrho)^{-a}
.
∎

Proposition A.2
.

Let
\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)
and
\alpha_{+},\alpha_{-}\in\mathbb{C}
. Let
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r)=\alpha_{+}r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}+\alpha_{-}r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}
and let
r\geq R
, so that
\mathfrak{w}_{0}(r)\neq 0
. Let
N_{\beta_{\ell}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, such that
N_{\beta_{\ell}}\beta_{\ell}>1
.

Define

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r(\tau,\varrho)):=\beta_{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}(1+\tau)^{-a+2iq-n\beta_{\ell}}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(a,b,c_{n};-\varrho\right),

(A.2)

with

\displaystyle a=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,

\displaystyle b=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,

\displaystyle c_{n}=

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{2}+iq-(n+\frac{1}{2})\beta_{\ell},

\displaystyle\zeta=

\displaystyle\>-\frac{2^{\beta_{\ell}}\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(a)}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(b)}\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}.

Then
\Psi^{\infty}_{0}=(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}Y_{\ell m}
is smooth with respect to the coordinate chart
(\tau,\varrho_{\infty},\theta,\varphi)
(where
\varrho_{\infty}=\frac{1}{r}
) and it is a solution to (
2.10
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
\mathbbm{h}=0
.

Furthermore,
f(\tau,r):=(\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{\ell})^{-1}(r)(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r)
satisfies: for all
k,l\leq N

\displaystyle|(rX)^{k}((\tau+1)T)^{l}f|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{N}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}},

(A.3)

\displaystyle|(rX)^{k}((\tau+1)T)^{l}Tf|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{N}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}},

(A.4)

\displaystyle|(rX)^{k}((\tau+1)T)^{l}Xf|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{N}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}.

(A.5)

Proof.

Note first of all that by Lemma
A.1
and linearity of (
2.10
),
\Psi^{\infty}_{0}
is a solution to (
2.10
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
\mathbbm{h}=0
. Furthermore,
T^{m_{1}}(r^{2}X)^{m_{2}}\Psi^{\infty}_{0}
is well-defined for all
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

Let
\varrho_{0}>0
be arbitrarily large and suppose that
\varrho\leq\varrho_{0}
. Then (
A.3
) –(
A.5
) follow immediately, using that the decay rates of
(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r(\tau,\varrho))
are preserved under commutation with
(\tau+1)\mathfrak{T}
and
\varrho\partial_{\varrho}
.

Now suppose that
\varrho>\varrho_{0}
. The hypergeometric functions
{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,b,2-b-n\beta_{\ell};-\varrho)
satisfy the following asymptotics when
