\varrho\to\infty
(combine example
[
NIS
]
[§15.8] with
[
NIS
]
[§15.2]): for
n\geq 1
:

\frac{-\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}{\pi}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,b,1-a+2iq-n\beta_{\ell};-\varrho)=\frac{\varrho^{-a}}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(b)\Gamma(-(n+1)\beta_{\ell})}(1+O_{\infty}(\varrho^{-1}))\\
-\frac{\varrho^{-b}}{\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(a)\Gamma(-n\beta_{\ell})}(1+O_{\infty}(\varrho^{-1}))

(A.6)

On the other hand, we obtain for
n=0
:

\frac{-\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}{\pi}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,b,1-a+2iq;-\varrho)=\frac{-\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}{\pi}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,b,b;-\varrho)=\frac{-\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}{\pi\Gamma(b)}(1+\varrho)^{-a}\\
=\frac{(1+\varrho)^{-a}}{\Gamma(b)\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell})},

(A.7)

where we applied Euler’s reflection formula to arrive at the last equality.

From the above, it follows that (
A.3
) and (
A.4
) hold, since decay rates are preserved under commutation with
(\tau+1)T
and
\varrho\partial_{\varrho}
and
T\mathfrak{w}_{0}^{-1}=0
.

In order to conclude (
A.5
), we will need to choose the constant
\zeta
appropriately, since
X\mathfrak{w}_{0}^{-1}\neq 0
.

Consider (
A.6
). Note that we can rewrite the terms with a factor
\varrho^{-b}
as follows:

-\frac{\varrho^{-b}(1+\tau)^{-a+2iq}}{\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(a)}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}\frac{(1+\tau)^{-n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(-n\beta_{\ell})}\\
=-\zeta\frac{(2r)^{b}(1+\tau)^{-a+2iq}(1+\tau)^{-b}}{\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(a)}\sum_{m=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}+1}\zeta^{m}\frac{(1+\tau)^{-(m+1)\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(-(m+1)\beta_{\ell})}\\
=-\zeta\frac{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(b)}{\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(a)}(2r)^{b}\left[(1+\tau)^{-a}\sum_{m=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}+1}\zeta^{m}\frac{(1+\tau)^{-a+2iq-m\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(b)\Gamma(-(m+1)\beta_{\ell})}\right].

Hence, using that
N_{\beta_{\ell}}\beta_{\ell}>1
, we obtain:

2^{-a}\frac{-\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}{\pi}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}(1+\tau)^{-a+2iq-n\beta_{\ell}}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,b,1-a+2iq-n\beta_{\ell};-\varrho)\\
=\left[r^{a}-\zeta\frac{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(b)}{2^{\beta}\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(a)}r^{b}\right]\cdot\left[\sum_{m=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{m}\frac{(1+\tau)^{-1-(m+1)\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(b)\Gamma(-(m+1)\beta_{\ell})}\right]\\
+(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}O_{\infty}(\varrho^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}).

Now choose
\zeta
so that

\zeta\frac{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(b)}{2^{\beta}\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(a)}=-\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha+}.

Then

2^{-a}\alpha_{+}\frac{-\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}{\pi\beta_{\ell}}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r)=\mathfrak{w}_{0}(r)\cdot\left[\sum_{m=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{m}\frac{(1+\tau)^{-1-(m+1)\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(b)\Gamma(-(m+1)\beta_{\ell})}\right]\\
+(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}O_{\infty}\left(\left(\frac{\tau}{2r}\right)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}\right).

Finally, we divide the above expression by
\mathfrak{w}_{0}
and apply
X
to conclude that (
A.5
) holds.
∎

When
\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)
, we instead consider an infinite sum over
n
. We first prove a lemma that characterizes the necessary properties of the relevant hypergeometric functions.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
\beta\in i[0,\infty),q\in\mathbb{R}
and
x\in(0,\infty)
. Define
F_{x}(\varrho;\beta):={}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta}{2}+i(q+x);-\varrho)
.

(i)

Let
\varrho_{0}>0
. Then for all
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exists a constant
C_{N,\varrho_{0},q}>0
, such that for all
0\leq m\leq N
:

\left|((\varrho\partial_{\varrho})^{m}\left(\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta}{2}+iq-ix\right)F_{x}(\varrho;x)-(1+\varrho)^{-a}\right)\right|\leq C_{q,\varrho_{0},N}\varrho(1+\varrho)^{-\frac{3}{2}},

(A.8)

with

\left|\frac{1}{e^{\frac{\pi}{2}x}|\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta}{2}+iq-ix)|}+1\right|\leq C_{q,\beta,N}(x+1)^{-2}e^{-\frac{\pi}{2}x}.

(A.9)

(ii)

Let
\varrho\geq\varrho_{0}
and let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrarily small. Then for
\varrho_{0}
suitably small and for all
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exists a constant
C_{N,q,\varrho_{0},\epsilon}>0
, such that for all
0\leq m,l\leq N
:

\displaystyle\Bigg|(x\partial_{x})^{l}(\varrho\partial_{\varrho})^{m}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\sin(\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\beta)}{\pi}F(\varrho;x)-\frac{\varrho^{-a}}{\Gamma(b)\Gamma(c_{x}-a)}+\frac{\varrho^{-b}}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(c_{x}-b)}\right)\Bigg|

(A.10)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{N,q,\varrho_{0},\epsilon}\varrho^{-1}e^{(\frac{\pi}{2}+\epsilon)x}\quad(\beta\in i(0,\infty)),

\displaystyle\Bigg|(x\partial_{x})^{l}(\varrho\partial_{\varrho})^{m}

\displaystyle\left(F(\varrho;x)-\frac{\varrho^{-a}}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(c_{x}-a)}\left(\log\varrho+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(a)}{\Gamma(a)}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(c_{x}-a)}{\Gamma(c_{x}-a)}\right)\right)\Bigg|

(A.11)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{N,q,\varrho_{0},\epsilon}\varrho^{-1}\log\varrho e^{(\frac{\pi}{2}+\epsilon)x}\quad(\beta=0).

Proof.

Let
c_{x}:=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta}{2}+iq-ix
,
a=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta}{2}+iq
and
b=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta}{2}+iq
. Note that
\textnormal{Re}\,a=\textnormal{Re}\,b=\textnormal{Re}\,c_{x}=\frac{1}{2}
. Note that
c_{x}-b=-ix
.

We can express:

F(\varrho;x)=(1+\varrho)^{-a}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(a,c_{x}-b,c_{x};\frac{\varrho}{1+\varrho}\right)\\
=\frac{(1+\varrho)^{-a}}{\Gamma(c_{x})}\left(1+\frac{\Gamma(c_{x})}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(c_{x}-b)}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(a+k)\Gamma(c_{x}+k-b)}{\Gamma(c_{x}+k)\Gamma(k+1)}\left(\frac{\varrho}{1+\varrho}\right)^{k}\right),

see for example
[
NIS
]
[§15.8.1].

By the Stirling approximation formula, we can alternatively estimate for
z\in\mathbb{C}
, with
z=re^{i\theta}
, with
r>0
and
\theta=\arg(z)\in(-\pi+\epsilon_{0},\pi-\epsilon_{0})
for some
\epsilon_{0}>0
:

\log\Gamma(z)=z\log z-z+\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi z^{-1})+O_{\infty}(z^{-1})\\
=\left[(\textnormal{Re}\,z)(\log r-1)-\theta\textnormal{Im}\,z+\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi r^{-1})\right]+i(\textnormal{Im}\,z\log r+\theta\textnormal{Re}\,z-\frac{\theta}{2})+O_{\infty}(z^{-1})

Hence, for
z=c_{x}+k-b
,
\textnormal{Re}\,z=k
,
\textnormal{Im}\,z=-x
,
r=(k^{2}+x^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\theta=-\arctan(xk^{-1}):=\theta_{k}
, so

\textnormal{Re}\,\log\Gamma(c_{x}+k-b)=\frac{k}{2}\left(\log(k^{2}+x^{2})-2\right)+\theta_{k}x-\frac{1}{4}\log(4\pi^{2}(k^{2}+x^{2}))+O_{\infty}((x^{2}+k^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

On the other hand, for
z=c_{k}+k
, we have
\textnormal{Re}\,z=k+\frac{1}{2}
,
\textnormal{Im}\,z=-x+q-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta
,
r=((k+\frac{1}{2})^{2}+(x+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q)^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}
and
\theta=-\arctan((x+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q)(k+\frac{1}{2})^{-1}):=\tilde{\theta}_{k}
, so

\textnormal{Re}\,\log\Gamma(c_{x}+k)=\frac{k+\frac{1}{2}}{2}\left(\log\left((k+\frac{1}{2})^{2}+(x+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q)^{2}\right)-2\right)+\tilde{\theta}_{k}(x+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q)\\
-\frac{1}{4}\log(4\pi^{2}((k+\frac{1}{2})^{2}+(x+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q)^{2})+O_{\infty}((x^{2}+k^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

We therefore obtain:

\textnormal{Re}\,\log\Gamma(c_{x}+k-b)-\textnormal{Re}\,\log\Gamma(c_{x}-b)-\textnormal{Re}\,\log\Gamma(c_{x}+k)=\left(\theta_{k}-\tilde{\theta}_{k}+\frac{\pi}{2}\right)x+\frac{k}{2}\log(1+O((k^{2}+x^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}))\\
-\frac{1}{4}\log\left((x^{-2}k^{2}+1)(1+O((k^{2}+x^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}))\right)+\frac{1}{4}\log\left(1+O((k^{2}+x^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)+O_{\infty}(1)\\
=\left(\theta_{k}-\tilde{\theta}_{k}+\frac{\pi}{2}\right)x-\frac{1}{4}\log(x^{-2}k^{2}+1)+O_{\infty}(1)

Furthermore,

-\log\Gamma(c_{x})=-\frac{1}{4}\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{4}+(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q)^{2}\right)-2\right)+(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q)\arctan(4(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q))\\
+\frac{1}{4}\log\left(4\pi^{2}\left(\frac{1}{4}+(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q)^{2}\right)\right)-i\left(-(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta)\log x-\frac{1}{2}\arctan(4(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q))\right)\\
+O_{\infty}(x^{-1}),

so

\frac{1}{\Gamma(c_{x})}=-e^{\frac{1}{2}(\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q-1)}e^{\frac{\pi}{2}x(1+O_{\infty}(x^{-2}))}e^{+i(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta)\log x}.

Finally, we can estimate as above the following
x
-independent factor:

\left|\frac{\Gamma(a+k)}{\Gamma(k+1)}\right|\lesssim e^{\frac{k}{2}\log\left(\frac{(k+1)^{2}+(\textnormal{Im}\,a)^{2}}{(k+1)^{2}}\right)}(k+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim(k+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Putting the above together, we obtain the following estimate, which is uniform in
k,n
:

\left|\frac{\Gamma(c_{x})}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(c_{x}-b)}\frac{\Gamma(a+k)\Gamma(c_{x}+k-b)}{\Gamma(c_{x}+k)\Gamma(k+1)}\right|\leq C_{q}(k+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Hence, for
\varrho\leq\varrho_{0}
:

\left|\Gamma(c_{x})F(\varrho;x)-(1+\varrho)^{-a}\right|\leq C_{q,\varrho_{0}}\varrho(1+\varrho)^{-\frac{1}{2}-1}.

It is straightforward to commute the above estimates with
(\varrho\partial_{\varrho})^{m}
, which introduces harmless polynomial factors in
k
, in order to conclude (
A.8
).

Now consider the region
\varrho\geq\varrho_{0}
. First, consider the case
\beta\in i(0,\infty)
.

Then we instead apply
[
NIS
]
[§15.8.2] to express:

-\frac{\sin(\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\beta)}{\pi}F(\varrho;x)=\varrho^{-a}\frac{{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,a-c_{x}+1,a-b+1;-\varrho^{-1})}{\Gamma(b)\Gamma(c_{x}-a)}-\varrho^{-b}\frac{{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(b,b-c_{x}+1,b-a+1;-\varrho^{-1})}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(c_{x}-b)}.

We can write:

\frac{{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}(a,a-c_{x}+1,a-b+1;-\varrho^{-1})}{\Gamma(b)\Gamma(c_{x}-a)}=\frac{1}{\Gamma(b)\Gamma(c_{x}-a)\Gamma(a)\Gamma(a-c_{x}+1)}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(a+k)\Gamma(a-c_{x}+1+k)}{\Gamma(a-b+1+k)\Gamma(k+1)}(-\varrho)^{-k}\\
=\frac{-i\sinh(\pi(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))}{\pi}\frac{1}{\Gamma(b)\Gamma(a)}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(a+k)\Gamma(a-c_{x}+1+k)}{\Gamma(a-b+1+k)\Gamma(k+1)}(-\varrho)^{-k},

where we applied Euler’s reflection formula to arrive at the last equality.

We apply Stirling’s formula, as above, to obtain:

\left|\frac{\Gamma(a-c_{x}+1+k)}{\Gamma(a-b+1+k)}\right|\lesssim e^{\frac{1}{2}(k+\frac{1}{2})\log[\frac{(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q)^{2}+(k+1)^{2}}{(\textnormal{Im}\,\beta)^{2}+(k+1)^{2}}]}e^{-(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q)\arctan(\frac{x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q}{k+1})}\\
=e^{\frac{x}{2}\frac{k+\frac{1}{2}}{x}\log[\frac{(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q)^{2}+(k+1)^{2}}{(\textnormal{Im}\,\beta)^{2}+(k+1)^{2}}]}e^{-(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q)\arctan(\frac{x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta-q}{k+1})}.

Suppose that
k+1\leq\delta x
for
\delta>0
. Then using that for
x\leq\delta
,
x\log(1+x^{-1})\leq\delta\log(1+\delta^{-1})
, we obtain for
\delta
suitably small:

\sinh(\pi(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))\left|\frac{\Gamma(a-c_{x}+1+k)}{\Gamma(a-b+1+k)}\right|\lesssim e^{C\delta x}e^{\frac{\pi}{2}x}e^{-\frac{\pi}{2}(1-C\delta)x}\leq e^{C\delta x}.

Now suppose that
k+1>\delta x
. Then

\sinh(\pi(x+\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}))\left|\frac{\Gamma(a-c_{x}+1+k)}{\Gamma(a-b+1+k)}\right|\lesssim e^{\frac{\pi}{2}x}e^{Ck}\leq e^{C\delta^{-1}k}.

By repeating the above estimates with the roles of
a
and
b
reversed and taking
\varrho_{0}
suitably large, depending on
\delta
, we obtain:

\Bigg|\frac{\sin(\pi\textnormal{Im}\,\beta)}{\pi}F(\varrho;x)-\frac{\varrho^{-a}}{\Gamma(b)\Gamma(c_{x}-a)}+\frac{\varrho^{-b}}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(c_{x}-b)}\Bigg|\leq C_{\epsilon,q}\varrho^{-1}e^{(\frac{\pi}{2}+\epsilon)x}.

It is straightforward to apply
(\varrho\partial_{\varrho})^{m}
and
(x\partial_{x})^{l}
to the above estimates to conclude (
A.10
).

Now consider the case
\beta=0
. We apply
[
NIS
]
[§15.8.8] together with Euler’s reflection formula to express:

F(\varrho;x)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(a)^{2}}\varrho^{-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(a+k)}{\Gamma(k+1)^{2}\Gamma(c_{x}-a-k)}\varrho^{-k}\left(\log\varrho+2\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(k+1)}{\Gamma(k+1)}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(a+k)}{\Gamma(a+k)}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(c_{x}-a-k)}{\Gamma(c_{x}-a-k)}\right)\\
=-i\frac{\pi\sinh(\pi x)}{\Gamma(a)^{2}}\varrho^{-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(a+k)\Gamma(a-c_{x}+k+1)}{\Gamma(k+1)^{2}}(-\varrho)^{-k}\left(\log\varrho+2\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(k+1)}{\Gamma(k+1)}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(a+k)}{\Gamma(a+k)}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(c_{x}-a-k)}{\Gamma(c_{x}-a-k)}\right).

The terms in the
k
-sum can be estimated as in the
\textnormal{Im}\,\beta>0
case above to conclude:

\Bigg|F(\varrho;x)-\frac{\varrho^{-a}}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(c_{x}-a)}\left(\log\varrho+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(a)}{\Gamma(a)}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(c_{x}-a)}{\Gamma(c_{x}-a)}\right)\Bigg|\leq C_{\epsilon,q}\varrho^{-1}\log\varrho e^{(\frac{\pi}{2}+\epsilon)x}.

It is straightforward to apply
(\varrho\partial_{\varrho})^{m}
and
(x\partial_{x})^{l}
to the above estimates to conclude (
A.11
).
∎

Proposition A.4
.

Let
\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)
and
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r)=\alpha_{+}(\beta_{\ell})r^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}+\alpha_{-}(\beta_{\ell})r^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}
, with
\alpha_{\pm}(is)\in\mathbb{C}
for
s\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
\alpha_{-}(is)=\alpha_{+}(-is)
.

Define

\zeta(\beta_{\ell}):=-\frac{2^{\beta_{\ell}}\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq)}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq)}\frac{\alpha_{-}(\beta_{\ell})}{\alpha_{+}(\beta_{\ell})},

so that
\zeta(-\beta_{\ell})=\frac{1}{\zeta(\beta_{\ell})}
, and assume that there exists a
\eta_{0}=\eta(|q|)>0
, such that

\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\log\left|\frac{\alpha_{-}(\beta_{\ell})}{\alpha_{+}(\beta_{\ell})}\right|\leq-\frac{\pi}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}-\eta_{0}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\left|\frac{\cosh(\pi(|q|-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell})}{\cosh(\pi(|q|+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell})}\right|^{2}\right).

(A.12)

Then the following sum is a well-defined smooth function with respect to
(\tau,\varrho_{\infty},\theta,\varphi)
:

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r(\tau,\varrho)):=\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(\zeta(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}))^{n}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-(n+\frac{1}{2})\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+iq}\\
\times{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq-(n+\frac{1}{2})\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell};-\varrho\right).

(A.13)

The function
\Psi^{\infty}_{0}=(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}Y_{\ell m}
is a solution to (
2.10
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
\mathbbm{h}=0
.

Furthermore,
f(\tau,r):=(\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{\ell})^{-1}(r)(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r)
satisfies: for all
k,l\leq N

\displaystyle|(rX)^{k}((\tau+1)T)^{l}f|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{N}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(A.14)

\displaystyle|(rX)^{k}((\tau+1)T)^{l}Tf|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{N}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(A.15)

\displaystyle|(rX)^{k}((\tau+1)T)^{l}Xf|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{N}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(A.16)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
q>0
. If
q<0
, we simply repeat the argument with
\beta_{\ell}
replaced by
-\beta_{\ell}
. We can express:

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r(\tau,\varrho))=\beta_{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\zeta^{n}(1+\tau)^{-a+2iq-n\beta_{\ell}}F_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\varrho;\beta_{\ell}),

with
F_{x}(\varrho;\beta)
defined in Lemma
A.3
.

By assumption on
\zeta
, (
A.8
) and (
A.9
), we can estimate:

\log\zeta+\frac{1}{n}\log|F_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})|\leq-\eta_{0}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}+\frac{\log C_{\varrho,q}}{n}.

Hence,
|\zeta|F_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})|^{\frac{1}{n}}|<e^{-\eta_{0}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}<1
, so for
\varrho\leq\varrho_{0}
:

|(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r(\tau,\varrho))|\leq C_{\varrho,q}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\beta_{\ell}|\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\eta_{0}\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}\leq C_{\varrho,q}\eta_{0}.

This means that the series converges. Commutation with
\mathfrak{T}
and
\varrho\mathfrak{X}
is straightforward and we conclude that
(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}
is smooth.

Since
\Psi^{\infty}_{0}
is a well-defined limit of a sum of solutions to (
2.11
) for
M=0
by Lemma
A.1
, we conclude by linearity that
\Psi^{\infty}_{0}
must itself by a solution.

The remaining estimates in the statement of the proposition follow by repeating the steps in the proof of Proposition
A.2
with
N_{\beta_{\ell}}
replaced by
\infty
, using (
A.10
).
∎

Finally, we consider the case
\beta_{\ell}=0
. We could interpret the limit of (
A.2
) as
\beta_{\ell}\to 0
as the limit of a Riemann sum, evaluated along
x=n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta
, to obtain an integral over
(0,\infty)
. In the proposition below, we will instead directly define
(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}
as an integral.

Proposition A.5
.

Let
\beta_{\ell}=0
and
\alpha_{+},\alpha_{-}\in\mathbb{C}
. Let
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r)=r^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}(\alpha_{+}\log r+\alpha_{-})
.

Define

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r(\tau,\varrho)):=(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tau+1)^{-ix}e^{-\eta x}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq-ix;-\varrho\right)\,dx.

(A.17)

with

\eta:=i\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\left[\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}-\log 2+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\right],

and with
\gamma_{\rm Euler}
the Euler–Mascheroni constant. Assume that there exists a
\eta_{0}=\eta(|q|)>0
, such that

\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\textnormal{Im}\,\left(\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}\right)\leq-\frac{\pi}{2}\left(1-\tanh(\pi|q|)\right)+\eta_{0}.

(A.18)

Then
\Psi^{\infty}_{0}=(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}Y_{\ell m}
is well-defined and smooth with respect to the coordinate chart
(\tau,\varrho_{\infty},\theta,\varphi)
and it is a solution to (
2.10
) with
A=\widehat{A}
and
\mathbbm{h}=0
.

Furthermore,
f(\tau,r):=(\mathfrak{w}^{0}_{\ell})^{-1}(r)(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r)
satisfies: for all
k,l\leq N

\displaystyle|(rX)^{k}((\tau+1)T)^{l}f|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{N}(\log r)^{-1}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(A.19)

\displaystyle|(rX)^{k}((\tau+1)T)^{l}Tf|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{N}(\log r)^{-1}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(A.20)

\displaystyle|(rX)^{k}((\tau+1)T)^{l}Xf|\leq

\displaystyle\>C_{N}(\log r)^{-1}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\tau+1+2r)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(A.21)

Proof.

We can express:

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,\varrho)=(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tau+1)^{-ix}e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\eta x}F_{x}(\varrho;0)\,dx,

with
F_{x}(\varrho;\beta)
defined in Lemma
A.3
.

Without loss of generality assume that
q>0
. In order to conclude that the above integral is well-defined, we will verify that the integrand decays exponentially in
x
. Note that

\textnormal{Re}\,\eta=-\textnormal{Im}\,\left(\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}\right)+\textnormal{Im}\,\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}.

Furthermore,

\textnormal{Im}\,\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}=\textnormal{Re}\,\frac{d}{dq}\log\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq\right)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dq}\log\left|\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq\right)\right|^{2}=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{d}{dq}\log(\cosh(\pi q))=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\tanh(\pi q).

Hence, by assumption on
\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}

\textnormal{Re}\,\eta\geq\frac{\pi}{2}+\eta_{0}

Applying (
A.8
) and (
A.9
), then implies that for
\varrho\leq\varrho_{0}
:

\left|e^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\eta x}F_{x}(\varrho;0)\right|\leq e^{-\eta_{0}x}.

The absolute value of the integrand is therefore exponentially decaying, so the integral is well-defined. Furthermore, by Lemma
A.1
, the linearity of (
A.1
) and the dominated convergence theorem,
\Psi^{\infty}_{0}
must be a solution to (
A.1
). Since we can freely commute with
\varrho\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathfrak{T}
, we moreover have that
\Psi^{\infty}_{0}
is a smooth solution, so
(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r)Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\varphi)
is a smooth solution to (
2.10
).

It remains to consider the function
f=\mathfrak{w}_{0}^{-1}\Psi^{\infty}_{0}
for
\varrho\geq\varrho_{0}
. Note first of all that by (
A.11
), we can write:

F(\varrho;x)=\frac{\varrho^{-a}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)\Gamma(-ix)}\left(\log\varrho+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(-ix)}{\Gamma(-ix)}\right)+\log\varrho e^{(\frac{\pi}{2}+\epsilon)x}R(\varrho,x),

(A.22)

where
(x\partial_{x})^{\ell}R(\varrho,x)=O_{\infty}(\varrho^{-1})
. We split

\log\varrho=\log(1+\tau)-\log r-\log 2

can integrate by parts in
x
, taking
\epsilon<\eta_{0}
to estimate:

\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+\tau)^{-ix}e^{-(\eta-\frac{\pi}{2}-\epsilon)x}\log(1+\tau)R(\varrho,x)\,dx=-\frac{1}{i}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d}{dx}(e^{-ix\log(1+\tau)})e^{-(\eta-\frac{\pi}{2}-\epsilon)x}R(\varrho,x)\,dx\\
=-iR(\varrho,0)-i\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-ix\log(1+\tau)}\frac{d}{dx}\left(e^{-(\eta-\frac{\pi}{2}-\epsilon)x}R(\varrho,x)\right)\,dx.

The right-hand side can be bounded by
\varrho^{-\frac{3}{2}}
and after applying
X
, we conclude that:

\left|X\left((\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0})^{-1}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+\tau)^{-ix}e^{-(\eta-\frac{\pi}{2}-\epsilon)x}\log(1+\tau)R(\varrho,x)\,dx\right)\right|\leq C(1+\tau)^{-2}(\log r)^{-1},

as desired. It is straightforward to commute with
rX
and
(\tau+1)T
.

We will now consider the remaining term in (
A.22
) and show that we can factor out
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r)
, by choosing
\eta
appropriately. As above, we first split
\log\varrho=\log(1+\tau)-\log r-\log 2
and integrate by parts the
\log(1+\tau)
contribution. We obtain:

\frac{\varrho^{-\frac{1}{2}-iq}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+\tau)^{-ix}e^{-\eta x}\log(1+\tau)\frac{1}{\Gamma(-ix)}\,dx=-\frac{\varrho^{-\frac{1}{2}-iq}}{i\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d}{dx}(e^{-ix\log(1+\tau)})e^{-\eta x}\frac{1}{\Gamma(-ix)}\,dx\\
=\frac{\varrho^{-\frac{1}{2}-iq}}{i\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left[-\eta+i\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(-ix)}{\Gamma(-ix)}\right]\frac{1}{\Gamma(-ix)}(1+\tau)^{-ix}e^{-\eta x}\,dx,

where we used that the boundary term at
x=0
vanishes since
\frac{1}{\Gamma(-ix)}
vanishes at
x=0
.

The full integral then becomes:

-\frac{\varrho^{-\frac{1}{2}-iq}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+\tau)^{-ix}\frac{e^{-\eta x}}{\Gamma(-ix)}\left[\log r+\log 2-2\gamma_{\rm Euler}+\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}-i\eta\right]\,dx.

Now take

-i\eta=\frac{\alpha_{-}}{\alpha_{+}}-\log 2+2\gamma_{\rm Euler}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}.

Then we can write the integral as follows.

-2^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}\frac{(\tau+1)^{-1}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r)\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+\tau)^{-ix}\frac{e^{-\eta x}}{\Gamma(-ix)}\,dx.

After dividing by
\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r)
, the above expression is independent of
r
and hence, the
X
-derivative vanishes. We conclude (
A.19
)–(
A.21
) for
\varrho\geq\varrho_{0}
.

We integrate by parts and use that
{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq;-\varrho\right)=\frac{(1+\varrho)^{-\frac{1}{2}-iq}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}
to rewrite:

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r(\tau,\varrho))=i((\log(1+\tau))^{-1}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}\\
\times\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d}{dx}((\tau+1)^{-ix})e^{-\eta x}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq-ix;-\varrho\right)\,dx\\
=-i((\log(1+\tau))^{-1}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}\frac{(1+\varrho)^{-\frac{1}{2}-iq}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\\
-i((\log(1+\tau))^{-1}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}\\
\times\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tau+1)^{-ix}\frac{d}{dx}\left(e^{-\eta x}{}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq,\frac{1}{2}+iq-ix;-\varrho\right)\right)\,dx

We therefore obtain:

|(rX)^{l}((1+\tau)T)^{m}\Psi^{\infty}_{0}|\lesssim(\log(1+\tau))^{-1}((1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}.

For
\varrho\leq\varrho_{0}
,
r^{-1}\lesssim(1+\tau)^{-1}
, so (
A.19
)–(
A.21
) then follow from the above estimates.
∎

Proposition A.6
.

The functions
(\Psi_{0}^{\infty})_{\ell m}(\tau,r)
, defined in Propositions
A.2
,
A.4
and
A.5
satisfy the following late-time asymptotics:

(i)

In the limit
r\to\infty
:

\displaystyle(\Psi_{0}^{\infty})_{\ell m}(\tau,\infty)=

\displaystyle\>\beta_{\ell}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\frac{\zeta^{n}(\beta_{\ell})(1+\tau)^{-n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-(n+\frac{1}{2})\beta_{\ell}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\>\frac{\beta_{\ell}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}\right)}+O((1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\beta_{\ell}}{2}}),\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)),

\displaystyle(\Psi_{0}^{\infty})_{\ell m}(\tau,\infty)=

\displaystyle\>\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\zeta^{n}(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell})(1+\tau)^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-(n+\frac{1}{2})\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}\right)},\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),

\displaystyle(\Psi_{0}^{\infty})_{\ell m}(\tau,\infty)=

\displaystyle\>\frac{-i}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq\right)}(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}(\log(1+\tau))^{-1}+(\log(1+\tau))^{-2}O_{\infty}((1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}),\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0).

(ii)

At
r=r_{0}>0
:

\displaystyle(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r_{0})=

\displaystyle-2^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1-\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}(\beta_{\ell})\frac{(1+\tau)^{-n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+iq)\Gamma(-(n+1)\beta_{\ell})}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>O_{\infty}\left((1+\tau)^{-2}\right)=-2^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+iq)\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell})}\mathfrak{w}_{0}(r)(1+\tau)^{-1-\beta_{\ell}}

\displaystyle+O_{\infty}\left((1+\tau)^{-1-2\beta_{\ell}}\right),\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)),

\displaystyle(\Psi_{0}^{\infty})_{\ell m}(\tau,\infty)=

\displaystyle\>-2^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{-}\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\zeta^{n}(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell})\frac{(1+\tau)^{-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}+iq)\Gamma(-(n+1)\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell})}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>O_{\infty}\left((1+\tau)^{-2}\right),\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),

\displaystyle(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r_{0})=

\displaystyle-\frac{2^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r)\left[\log(1+\tau))^{-2}(\tau+1)^{-1-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}+(\log(1+\tau))^{-3}O_{\infty}((\tau+1)^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>O_{\infty}((1+\tau)^{-2}),\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0).

Proof.

We first consider the case
\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)
. Then

(\Psi_{0}^{\infty})_{\ell m}(\tau,\infty)=\beta_{\ell}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\frac{\zeta^{n}(1+\tau)^{-n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-(n+\frac{1}{2})\beta_{\ell}\right)}=\frac{\beta_{\ell}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}\right)}+O((1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\beta_{\ell}}{2}}).

Let
r_{0}>0
be arbitrary. Then:

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r_{0})=-2^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})}(1+\tau)^{-1-\beta_{\ell}}\mathfrak{w}_{0}(r)\cdot\left[\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}\frac{(1+\tau)^{-n\beta_{\ell}}}{\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+iq)\Gamma(-(n+1)\beta_{\ell})}\right]\\
+O_{\infty}\left((1+\tau)^{-2}\right)\\
=-2^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}}\frac{\pi\beta_{\ell}}{\alpha_{+}\sin(\pi\beta_{\ell})\Gamma(\beta_{\ell}+1)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{\ell}+iq)\Gamma(-\beta_{\ell})}\mathfrak{w}_{0}(r)(1+\tau)^{-1-\beta_{\ell}}+O_{\infty}\left((1+\tau)^{-1-2\beta_{\ell}}\right).

Now let
\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)
. Then the expressions from the
\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)
case remain valid, but with
\beta_{\ell}
replaced by
\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}
and
N_{\beta_{\ell}}
replaced with
\infty
.

Finally, we consider the case
\beta_{\ell}=0
. We can express:

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,\infty)=(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tau+1)^{-ix}e^{-\eta x}\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-ix\right)}\,dx.

We write
(\tau+1)^{-ix}=i(\log(1+\tau))^{-1}\frac{d}{dx}\left(e^{-ix\log(1+\tau)}\right)
and integrate by parts to obtain:

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,\infty)=-i(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}(\log(1+\tau))^{-1}\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq\right)}\\
-i(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}(\log(1+\tau))^{-1}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tau+1)^{-ix}\frac{d}{dx}\left(e^{-\eta x}\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-ix\right)}\right)\,dx\\
=-i(\tau+1)^{-\frac{1}{2}+iq}(\log(1+\tau))^{-1}\frac{1}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq\right)}+(\log(1+\tau))^{-2}O_{\infty}((1+\tau)^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

where we obtained the last equality by integrating by parts in
x
again to pull out another factor
(\log(1+\tau))^{-1}
.

We also obtain along
r=r_{0}
:

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r_{0})=-2^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}\frac{(\tau+1)^{-1}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r)\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+\tau)^{-ix}\frac{e^{-\eta x}}{\Gamma(-ix)}\,dx+O_{\infty}((1+\tau)^{-2})

We integrate by parts and use that
\frac{1}{\Gamma(-ix)}
vanishes at
x=0
to obtain:

\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+\tau)^{-ix}\frac{e^{-\eta x}}{\Gamma(-ix)}\,dx=-i\frac{1}{\log(1+\tau)}\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+\tau)^{-ix}\frac{d}{dx}\left(\frac{e^{-\eta x}}{\Gamma(-ix)}\right)\,dx\\
=\frac{1}{(\log(1+\tau))^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d}{dx}\left((1+\tau)^{-ix}\right)\frac{d}{dx}\left(\frac{e^{-\eta x}}{\Gamma(-ix)}\right)\,dx\\
=-\frac{1}{(\log(1+\tau))^{2}}\frac{d}{dx}\Big|_{x=0}\left(\frac{1}{\Gamma(-ix)}\right)+O_{\infty}((\log(1+\tau))^{-3})\\
=\frac{1}{(\log(1+\tau))^{2}}+O_{\infty}((\log(1+\tau))^{-3}).

We therefore conclude that:

(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,r_{0})=-\frac{2^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\mathfrak{w}_{\ell}^{0}(r)\left[\log(1+\tau))^{-2}(\tau+1)^{-1}+(\log(1+\tau))^{-3}O_{\infty}((\tau+1)^{-1}\right]\\
+O_{\infty}((1+\tau)^{-2}).\qed

Proposition A.7
.

Define the following constants: let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}_{k}:=

\displaystyle\>\begin{cases}(-1)^{k}2^{-(k+1)}\frac{\beta_{\ell}\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq+k+1)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq)\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq)},\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)),\\
2^{-(k+1)}|\beta_{\ell}|\\
\times\sqrt{\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}|\zeta(\beta_{\ell})|^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)2n}\left|\partial_{z}^{k+1}\>({}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1})\right|^{2}\left(\frac{1+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1+(2n-1)\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq;0\right)},\\
\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),\\
(-1)^{k}2^{-(k+1)}\frac{i\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq+k+1\right)}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)^{2}},\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0),\end{cases}

(A.23)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}_{\rm int}:=

\displaystyle\>\begin{cases}\frac{\left(q^{2}+\frac{1}{4}(1+\beta_{\ell})^{2}\right)|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}}{2(2+\beta_{\ell})|\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}\right)|^{2}},\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)),\\
\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}}{2}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}|\zeta|^{\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)2n}\left|\partial_{z}\>({}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1})\right|^{2}\left(\frac{1+\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1-\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1+(2n-1)\,\textnormal{sign}\,(q)\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq;-\varrho\right)\,d\varrho,\\
\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),\\
\frac{\frac{1}{4}+q^{2}}{8|\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)|^{2}},\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0).\end{cases}

(A.24)

(i)

The following bounds hold for
(r^{2}X)^{k+1}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}
with
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
: there exist constants
C=C(q,\beta_{\ell})>0
and
\widetilde{C}=\widetilde{C}(q)>0
, such that:

\displaystyle\left|(r^{2}X)^{k+1}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,\infty)-\mathfrak{c}_{k}(1+\tau)^{\frac{k+1}{2}+iq-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{\frac{k+1}{2}-\frac{3\beta_{\ell}}{2}}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)),

(A.25)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}(1+\tau)^{-\frac{k+1}{2}}|(r^{2}X)^{k+1}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}|(\tau,\infty)\geq

\displaystyle\>\mathfrak{c}_{k}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),

(A.26)

\displaystyle|(r^{2}X)^{k+1}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}|(\tau,\infty)\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{\frac{k+1}{2}}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),

(A.27)

\displaystyle\left|(r^{2}X)^{k+1}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}(\tau,\infty)-\mathfrak{c}_{k}\frac{(1+\tau)^{\frac{k+1}{2}+iq}}{\log(1+\tau)}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>\widetilde{C}(\log(1+\tau))^{-2}(1+\tau)^{\frac{k+1}{2}}\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0).

(A.28)

(ii)

Let
R>0
be arbitrary. Then there exist constants
C=C(q,\beta_{\ell},R)>0
and
\widetilde{C}=\widetilde{C}(q,R)>0
, such that:

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}|X(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}|^{2}(\tau,r)\,dr-\mathfrak{c}_{\rm int}(1+\tau)^{-\beta_{\ell}}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>C(1+\tau)^{-2\beta_{\ell}}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in(0,1)),

(A.29)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}|X(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}|^{2}(\tau,r)\,dr\geq

\displaystyle\>\mathfrak{c}_{\rm int}\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),

(A.30)

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[0,\infty)}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}|X(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}|^{2}(\tau,r)\,dr\leq

\displaystyle\>C\quad(\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)),

(A.31)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}|X(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}|^{2}(\tau,r)\,dr-\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{\rm int}}{\log^{2}(1+\tau)}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\>\widetilde{C}(\log(1+\tau))^{-3}\quad(\beta_{\ell}=0).

(A.32)

(iii)

Let
p\in(0,2]
and consider the energy density
\mathcal{E}_{p}
from §
2.7
and with
\Omega^{2}=1-2Mr^{-1}+Q^{2}
,
Q^{2}\leq M^{2}
. Let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then there exist constants
C_{N}=C_{N}(q,\beta_{\ell},p,N)>0
and
\widetilde{C}_{N}=\widetilde{C}_{N}(q,R,p,N)>0
, such that for all
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\leq N
:

\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}(rX)^{k_{2}}((\tau+1)T)^{k_{3}}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})]\,d\sigma dr\leq\begin{cases}C_{N}(\tau+1)^{p-2-\textnormal{Re}\,\beta_{\ell}}\quad&(\beta_{\ell}\neq 0),\\
\widetilde{C}_{N}\frac{(\tau+1)^{p-2}}{\log^{2}(1+\tau)}\quad&(\beta_{\ell}=0).\end{cases}

(A.33)

Proof.

We will consider the case
k=0
. We will omit the subscript
\ell m
in the notation
(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}
. The
k\geq 1
follows from a straightforward generalization. Note that for
\beta_{\ell}\in(0,\infty)
:

(r^{2}X\Psi^{\infty}_{0})(\tau,\varrho)=-\frac{\tau+1}{2}(\mathfrak{X}\Psi^{\infty}_{0})(\tau,\infty)=-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{\beta_{\ell}}}\zeta^{n}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq-n\beta_{\ell}}{}_{2}(\partial_{\varrho}{F}_{n\beta})(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})\\
=-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}(\partial_{\varrho}{F}_{0})(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})(1+O((1+\tau)^{-\beta_{\ell}})).

Note that

{F}_{0}(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})={}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1}\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2},\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2},\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2};-\varrho\right)=\frac{(1+\varrho)^{-\frac{1}{2}-iq-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}\right)}.

Hence,

(\partial_{\varrho}F_{0})(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})=-\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}\right)\frac{(1+\varrho)^{-\frac{3}{2}-iq-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}\right)}.

We conclude that

(r^{2}X\Psi^{\infty}_{0})(\tau,\infty)=\frac{\beta_{\ell}\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq+\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}\right)}{2\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}\right)}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq}(1+O((1+\tau)^{-\beta_{\ell}})).

Furthermore, using that
r^{-2}dr=-2(\tau+1)^{-1}d\varrho
along constant
\tau
level sets, we can express

\int_{R}^{\infty}r^{2}|X\Psi^{\infty}_{0}|^{2}(\tau,r)\,dr=\frac{(\tau+1)}{2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\tau+1}{2R}}|\mathfrak{X}\Psi^{0}_{\infty}|^{2}(\tau,\varrho)\,d\varrho\\
=\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}(\tau+1)^{-\beta_{\ell}}}{2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\tau+1}{2R}}|(\partial_{\varrho}F_{0})(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})|^{2}(1+O((\tau+1)^{-\beta_{\ell}}))\,d\varrho\\
=\frac{\left(q^{2}+\frac{1}{4}(1+\beta_{\ell})^{2}\right)|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}(\tau+1)^{-\beta_{\ell}}}{2|\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}\right)|^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\tau+1}{2R}}(1+\varrho)^{-3-\beta_{\ell}}(1+O((\tau+1)^{-\beta_{\ell}}))\,d\\
=\frac{\left(q^{2}+\frac{1}{4}(1+\beta_{\ell})^{2}\right)|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}(\tau+1)^{-\beta_{\ell}}}{2(2+\beta_{\ell})|\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq-\frac{\beta_{\ell}}{2}\right)|^{2}}(1+O((\tau+1)^{-\beta_{\ell}})).

By using that
r^{-1}=2\varrho(\tau+1)^{-1}
it is also straightforward to obtain for
p\in[0,2)
the existence of a constant
C>0
, such that:

\int_{R}^{\infty}r^{p}|X(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}|^{2}+r^{-2}(|(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}|^{2}+|T(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})_{\ell m}|^{2})(\tau,r)\,dr\leq C(\tau+1)^{p-2-\beta_{\ell}}.

It is moreover straightforward to commute with
(\tau+1)T
and
rX
and obtain for
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}
and
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}=N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
the existence of a constant
C_{N}>0
, such that:

\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}(rX)^{k_{2}}((\tau+1)T)^{k_{3}}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})]\,d\sigma dr\leq C_{N}(\tau+1)^{p-2-\beta_{\ell}}.

Now suppose that
\beta_{\ell}\in i(0,\infty)
. Without loss of generality, we will assume that
q>0
. We use the general expressions above to obtain:

|r^{2}X\Psi^{\infty}_{0}|(\tau,\varrho)\leq\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|}{2}(1+\tau)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}|\zeta|^{n}\left|\partial_{\varrho}F_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})\right|,

where the right-hand side converges. This follows from the proof of Proposition
A.4
where
\partial_{\varrho}F_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}
is replaced by
F_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}
, but the additional
\varrho
-derivative only introduces additional polynomial growth in
n
, which does not affect the convergence of the relevant series.

Furthermore, by applying the Minkowski inequality, we can also estimate:

\frac{(\tau+1)}{2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\tau+1}{2R}}|\mathfrak{X}\Psi^{0}_{\infty}|^{2}(\tau,\varrho)\,d\varrho\leq\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}}{2}\left(\sum_{n=0}|\zeta|^{n}||\partial_{\varrho}F_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}||_{L^{2}_{\varrho}[0,\frac{\tau+1}{2R}]}\right)^{2}.

By Lemma
A.3
, there exists a constant
C_{q,\varrho_{0}}>0
, such that we can estimate:

||\partial_{\varrho}F_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}||_{L^{2}_{\varrho}[0,\varrho_{0}]}\leq C_{q,\varrho_{0}}e^{\frac{\pi}{2}n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}.

Furthermore, we can apply Lemma
A.3
to obtain moreover for
\varrho\geq\varrho_{0}
:

||\partial_{\varrho}F_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}||_{L^{2}_{\varrho}[\varrho,\infty)}\leq C_{q,\varrho_{0},\epsilon}\varrho^{-3}e^{(\frac{\pi}{2}+\epsilon)n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}.

for a constant
C_{q,\varrho_{0},\epsilon}>0
. By the properties assumed on
\zeta
in Proposition
A.4
, we therefore conclude that there exists a constant
C_{q,\beta_{\ell}}
such that:

\frac{(\tau+1)}{2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\tau+1}{2R}}|\mathfrak{X}\Psi^{0}_{\infty}|^{2}(\tau,\varrho)\,d\varrho\leq C_{q,\beta_{\ell}}.

Furthermore, it is straightforward to extend the above estimate to obtain for
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}
and
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}=N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
the existence of a constant
C_{N}>0
, such that for
p\in[0,2]
:

\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}(rX)^{k_{2}}((\tau+1)T)^{k_{3}}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})]\,d\sigma dr\leq C_{N}(\tau+1)^{p-2}.

In order to obtain an appropriate lower bound for
|r^{2}X\Psi^{\infty}_{0}|^{2}
, we instead write:

|r^{2}X\Psi^{\infty}_{0}|^{2}(\tau,\varrho)=\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}}{4}(1+\tau)\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}(\tau+1)^{-k\beta_{\ell}}\zeta^{n}\overline{\zeta}^{m}(\partial_{\varrho}{F}_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}})(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})\cdot(\partial_{\varrho}\overline{{F}_{m\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}})(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})\\
=\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}}{4}(1+\tau)\sum_{n,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}e^{-i(n-m)\sigma}\zeta^{n}\overline{\zeta}^{m}(\partial_{\varrho}{F}_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}})(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})\cdot(\partial_{\varrho}\overline{{F}_{m\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}})(\varrho;\beta_{\ell}),

with
\sigma=\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}\log(1+\tau)
. Since the above series is uniformly absolutely-convergent, it follows that:

\left|S^{-1}\int_{0}^{S}(1+\tau(\sigma))^{-1}|r^{2}X\Psi^{0}_{\infty}|^{2}(\tau(\sigma),\varrho))\,d\sigma-\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}}{4}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}|\zeta|^{2n}|\partial_{\varrho}{F}_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta}|^{2}(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})\right|\\
\leq S^{-1}\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\\
n\neq m\end{subarray}}\frac{2}{|n-m|}|\zeta|^{n+m}|\partial_{\varrho}{F}_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}}|(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})\cdot|\partial_{\varrho}{F}_{m\textnormal{Im}\,\beta_{\ell}})|\varrho;\beta_{\ell})\leq CS^{-1}.

(A.34)

Using that for any continuous
f:(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
:

\limsup_{x\to\infty}f(x)\geq\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{1}{L}\int_{0}^{L}f(x)\,dx,

(A.35)

we therefore conclude that

\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}(1+\tau)^{-1}|r^{2}X\Psi^{0}_{\infty}|^{2}(\tau,0)\geq\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}}{4}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}|\zeta|^{2n}|\partial_{\varrho}{F}_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta}|^{2}(0;\beta_{\ell})\\
=\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}}{4}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}|\zeta|^{2n}\left|\partial_{z}\>({}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1})\right|^{2}\left(\frac{1+\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1-\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1+(2n-1)\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq;0\right).

Furthermore, we can express:

\int_{R}^{\infty}r^{2}|X\Psi^{\infty}_{0}|^{2}(\tau,r)\,dr=\frac{(\tau+1)}{2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\tau+1}{2R}}|\mathfrak{X}\Psi^{0}_{\infty}|^{2}(\tau,\varrho)\,d\varrho\\
=\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}}{2}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-ik\sigma}\int_{0}^{\frac{\tau+1}{2R}}\sum_{n,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\>n-m=k}\zeta^{n}\overline{\zeta}^{m}(\partial_{\varrho}{F}_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta})(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})\cdot(\partial_{\varrho}\overline{{F}_{m\textnormal{Im}\,\beta}})(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})\,d\varrho.

As above, we have that:

\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{R}^{\infty}r^{2}|X\Psi^{\infty}_{0}|^{2}(\tau,r)\,dr\geq\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}}{2}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\frac{\tau+1}{2R}}|\zeta|^{2n}|\partial_{\varrho}\overline{{F}_{n\textnormal{Im}\,\beta}}|^{2}(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})\,d\varrho.

By Lemma
A.3
, it follows moreover that
\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{\frac{\tau+1}{2R}}^{\infty}|\zeta|^{2n}|\partial_{\varrho}\overline{{F}_{m\beta}}|^{2}(\varrho;\beta_{\ell})\,d\varrho=0
, so we conclude:

\limsup_{\tau\to\infty}\int_{R}^{\infty}r^{2}|X\Psi^{\infty}_{0}|^{2}(\tau,r)\,dr\\
\geq\frac{|\beta_{\ell}|^{2}}{2}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}|\zeta|^{2n}\left|\partial_{z}\>({}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{1})\right|^{2}\left(\frac{1+\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1-\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq,\frac{1+(2n-1)\beta_{\ell}}{2}+iq;-\varrho\right)\,d\varrho.

The right-hand side is clearly strictly greater than 0.

Finally, we consider
\beta_{\ell}=0
. Then, after integrating by parts in
x
, we obtain:

(r^{2}X\Psi^{\infty}_{0})(\tau,r(\tau,\varrho))=-\frac{\tau+1}{2}\mathfrak{X}\Psi^{\infty}_{0}(\tau,\varrho)=\frac{1}{2}(\tau+1)^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\tau+1)^{-ix}e^{-\eta x}(\partial_{\varrho}F_{x})(\varrho;0)\,dx\\
=-\frac{i}{2}(\log(1+\tau))^{-1}(\tau+1)^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}(\partial_{\varrho}\>F_{0})(\varrho;0)(1+O((\log(1+\tau))^{-1})\\
=\frac{i\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq\right)}{2\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\frac{(\tau+1)^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}}{\log(1+\tau)}(1+\varrho)^{-\frac{3}{2}-iq}(1+O((\log(1+\tau))^{-1}).

In particular,

(r^{2}X\Psi^{\infty}_{0})(\tau,\infty)=\frac{i\left(\frac{1}{2}+iq\right)}{2\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)}\frac{(\tau+1)^{\frac{1}{2}+iq}}{\log(1+\tau)}(1+O((\log(1+\tau))^{-1})

and

\int_{R}^{\infty}r^{2}|X\Psi^{\infty}_{0}|^{2}(\tau,r)\,dr=\frac{\frac{1}{4}+q^{2}}{4|\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)|^{2}}(\log(1+\tau))^{-2}\int_{0}^{\frac{\tau+1}{2R}}(1+\varrho)^{-3}(1+O((\log(1+\tau))^{-1})\,d\varrho\\
=\frac{\frac{1}{4}+q^{2}}{8|\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}+iq)|^{2}}(\log(1+\tau))^{-2}(1+O((\log(1+\tau))^{-1}).

Furthermore, after applying Lemma
A.3
, it is straightforward to extend the above estimate to obtain for
\ell(\ell+1)<q^{2}
and
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}=N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
the existence of a constant
C_{N}>0
, such that for
p\in[0,2]
:

\int_{r_{+}}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k_{1}}(rX)^{k_{2}}((\tau+1)T)^{k_{3}}(\Psi^{\infty}_{0})]\,d\sigma dr\leq C_{N}(\log(1+\tau))^{-2}(\tau+1)^{p-2}.\qed

Corollary A.8
.

Let
\alpha_{+},\alpha_{-}\in\mathbb{C}
be defined as in Proposition
3.1
.
Then (
A.12
) and (
A.18
) are satisfied.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Corollary
3.2
.
∎
